1. `code/data_code.py` strips a top-level `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard (its body dedented), and the validator's DATA profile (`validate_data_code`) checks the result: every SECURITY rule except the save target (a snippet saves nothing, so since 2026-10-25 every save name and `.open` is a `banned-call` in it); the import surface COMMON plus the standard `random` and the scipy computing submodules (no plotting library, no sklearn, no statsmodels, whose `datasets` reach files and the network); every private attribute banned on any receiver; a top-level assignment to `df`; and a `literal-data` rule that counts numbers and text values (list, tuple and set elements and dict values, never dict keys) in every outermost literal container, a repetition by an int literal counted as its copies, at most 20 of either per container and 40 in the file, so the model cannot write rows from memory. A file, URL or network finding carries the fixed message "paste the file's contents in the data panel instead". The findings reach the root fenced as `<tool_notes>`; the root fixes the code and calls once more at most (ToolSafety allows two calls per invocation).
2. The rate and the budget: a per-user daily count (`AGENT_DAILY_DATA_RUNS`, 30), checked and booked before the run, and the run queue's slot with the turn's start in the rate window (`RequestLedger.run_slot`, the same helper the pipeline awaits), taken mid-turn while the stream reports the queue position; a full queue or an expired wait ends the turn with `error{code:"capacity"}`, and a run past the user's `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` across sessions with `error{code:"run_limit"}`.
3. The run: `services.backend.run_data` in one slot of the render semaphore's run lane, through the renderer's `POST /run-data` (see [Render](#render)) with a time limit of `AGENT_DATA_RUN_TIMEOUT_S` (20 s, at most 30). The harness runs the snippet in data mode under an audit hook and writes `df` as `out.csv`, and its outcome as `status.json` (its exit code and, for a broken row, column or size cap, the measured value and the cap). The host takes the exit code from `status.json`, because Cloud Run's sandbox launcher reports every non-zero exit of the sandboxed process as its own death by SIGPIPE (exit code -13, with no `[end]` line in `sandbox.log`; seen on every failed render and data run from 2026-10-11 to 2026-10-14), which turned a too-large table into "an error the sandbox does not name" in the live test of 2026-10-14. The tool trusts only what it parses itself: the exit code, which maps to a fixed line, the cap numbers, which make the line actionable (`df's CSV is 4,493 KB, over the 2,048 KB cap: keep every 3rd row (df = df.iloc[::3]) ... and round the floats to the decimal places that still keep the values distinct`), and `out.csv` within the parser's caps. The harness never rounds or thins a table itself: no fixed rule fits every table (a fixed number of significant digits collapses large values that differ only in their decimals, such as 1,000,000.1 and 1,000,000.9), so the root chooses the decimal places for the data it computed (`df.round(3)` for values between -50 and 50), and the tool description and the root prompt name the 2 MB cap of computed data (raised from the 200 KB paste cap on 2026-10-14, owner decision, so the live test's 10,000-step Lorenz run, 637,727 bytes, and 10,001 rows of six full-precision columns, 1,138,766 bytes, fit as they are) and that rounding up front. Nothing the snippet printed reaches the model: an exception is named by its class and the line of `snippet.py` it was raised on (`gates.error_summary`), never by its message, which a snippet could fill with an injection.
4. The table: `parse_dataset(find_table=False)`, at least one number or date column, then the same pre-filter and dataset judge as a paste (`dataset_guard.check_dataset`, one function the dataset route calls too, so strikes, token booking and attribution stay in one place; a refusal is the tool's `data_refused`), then the store: the next version with the default bindings (`push_version(rebind=True)`), or the first version of new data without data. The version keeps the Python that ran (`StoredDataset.code`), which the dataset answers and `done.dataset` carry as `code` and the data panel shows collapsed under `# code that made this data`. The change line is `computed from your code: <rows> rows, <columns> columns`.

The dataset route classifies a paste before it parses it (`data/classify.py`): code answers `422 code` (at any length, because code is never a table; `np.linspace(0, 10, 100)` would otherwise parse as a one-row table), a formula within the chat's cap `422 formula`; the page sends either to the chat as the user's turn by itself when the text fits a chat message (since 2026-11-04; an offer with `.send()` before), and asks to shorten longer code. The scope judge calls a snippet or formula whose job is to generate the plot's data `in_scope` in any language, code with another purpose (factoring a number, "run this script") `out_of_scope`, and code that touches files, the network or the system `attack`. `AGENT_DATA_CODE=false` switches the tool off (it answers `unavailable`); a renderer deployed before `POST /run-data` answers `404`, which the tool also answers as `unavailable`.

Built since 2026-10-28 (the admin fan-out, owner request of 2026-10-28): `.plot_all()` plots the confirmed table in every library of the page's header at once, for the admin's tests. Since 2026-10-30 (owner request) it is chosen in the header: `all`, a toggle after the libraries (a pill after the library pills, since 2026-11-02 the last entry of the library menu), turns the composer's plot button (`.create_plot() →`, or `.update_plot() →` once the library has a plot; `.start_over()` until 2026-11-07) into `.plot_all()`; before that it was a row of its own under the composer. While `all` is pressed the page's own session stays the data session, and any library picked in the menu turns `all` off again, the page's own library without switching. In step 2 the message box waits, since the fan-out takes no typed text, and the status line at the chat's foot (the line above the box until 2026-11-07) says to turn `all` off to chat; in step 1, with or without data, the chat about the data goes on as without `all`. Since 2026-11-09, data waiting in the message box makes `.plot_all()` active: the click reads it as Enter does, then confirms it and opens the panel once it reads clean. Before the confirmation a click on `.plot_all()` shows the same notice as `.create_plot()`. While the panel is open, the library menu (with `all`) and `.plot_all()` are disabled, and its `.close()` returns the focus to `.plot_all()`. The page opens a session per library (`FanOut` in `app/src/sections/agent-chat/`), and starts `create_plot` in at most `user_max_runs` sessions at a time, the caller's own cap that `GET /v1/status` reports (a pool in the page). Until 2026-11-01 that was the public `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` (3) behind one run slot and one start a minute, and a 15-library fan-out took about 20 minutes; since then (owner decision) the fan-out stays admin-only, also after the public launch, and runs in the run queue's admin lane: five plots at once (`AGENT_ADMIN_USER_MAX_RUNS`), one start every 10 seconds like every run, no per-user day caps or message minute, and the global day budget, raised to 100,000,000, as the only daily cap on model spend. The cap counts the admin's runs in every window, while each panel counts only its own, so since the same day a `run_limit` answer is no failure in the panel (`fanoutRules.ts`, `nextFanOut`): the library goes back to waiting, the panel starts nothing more until one of its own runs ends or 15 s pass, then tries one library again, and a library gives up only after 30 minutes of `run_limit`, which its card says. Three panels of 15 libraries started at once thus share the five places and finish all 45 plots. Every session is deleted when the panel closes or the page goes.

Built since 2026-11-01 (example data, owner request of 2026-11-01): "Beispieldaten" or "generiere Zufallszahlen mit Kategorien" in the data field ended in "no table found", because the text holds no table and the transcriber found no rows in it. Such a request now goes to the chat, where the root makes the data up with code. Detection has two halves and no new model call: the dataset route's classifier (`classify.looks_like_data_request`, between the code and the formula check) reads one line of at most 30 words for a fixed phrase in German, English, French, Spanish, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch or Polish ("Beispieldaten", "Zufallszahlen", "random numbers", "make up some data", "données d'exemple") with fewer than three values of its own (`generate.stated_values`, the formula guard's count with its range phrases left out, so "3 Kategorien und 20 Zeilen" stays a request and "Beispieldaten: Hund 7, Katze 3, Vogel 4" stays a table in words); and the transcriber, which already reads every other text without a table, answers `asks_for_data` for data described in words or in another language ("monthly sales for 4 regions"), honoured for text the chat takes when that answer holds no table or the text no values of its own (`classify.request_for_chat`, so "Beispieldaten mit 3 Kategorien, 4 Regionen und 12 Monaten", which the rules leave to the transcriber for its three numbers, still goes), and whatever table came with that answer is dropped. Either answers `422 data_request` (the rules' answer counts as no paste; the transcriber's does, since it reached a model), and the page sends the text to the chat as the user's message at once, with no offer to click (owner: simple), and empties the box. The scope judge's rubric calls a request for made-up example data `in_scope`, and only figures of the real world (a named company, place, index or dataset, "the real figures") `needs_data`. The root reads the spec's roles (`get_spec_brief`) and writes Python for `run_data_code` with `source_language` `example`: one column per role in the roles' order and kinds, named in the session language, realistic labels and ranges for the plot's subject, the counts and ranges the user names (otherwise a small table: 4 to 8 categories, 2 to 4 groups, 12 to 36 dates, 50 to 200 points for a scatter), and `np.random.default_rng(42)`, so the same request gives the same table. The model writes no row: the validator's `literal-data` rule holds for this code as for any other, and the sandbox computes the table. The version's change line says `generated example data: <rows> rows, <columns> columns` instead of `computed from your code`, and its source line for the adapter and the reviewer starts `generated example data, made up by Python code` (`dataset_context.code_source`); the rest is a `run_data_code` version (the data panel's code block, `.undo()` when there was data before, the confirmation). The run counts toward `AGENT_DAILY_DATA_RUNS` and takes the run queue's slot like any data run, so at the default of one start every 10 seconds the first `.create_plot()` can wait up to 10 seconds behind it. Generation by code rather than a server-side generator from the roles alone, because a spec's data needs structure that no list of role kinds states (a value for every pair of category and group, a trend over dates, a relation between x and y, high above low), which the root reads from the spec's description, and the code it ran is shown under the data. Telemetry: `data_input.format` and `outcome` `data_request` (`cause` `transcribed_data_request` from the transcriber), `chat_turn.kind` `example`. The live check of 2026-11-01 (Claude Haiku 5.5 on `eu`, the deployed renderer) took five requests to a confirmed table and a shipped plot, all `ok`: "Beispieldaten" on `bar-grouped` in matplotlib (16 rows of quarter, region and sales), "generiere Zufallszahlen mit Kategorien" on `scatter-basic` in plotly (200 rows in four categories), 36 months of temperatures on `line-timeseries` in ggplot2 (1,096 daily rows), "monthly sales for 4 regions over 6 months", which only the transcriber recognised, on `bar-grouped` in seaborn (24 rows), and 24 weekly values asked for in the chat on `line-timeseries` in Chart.js; $0.15 in all, about $0.03 a request with its plot.

Since 2026-11-01 (owner decision) the server copies the page session's confirmed current data version into each fan-out session (`POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/copy` with `{from_session}`, `copyData` in the page), so `.plot_all()` plots exactly what the user confirmed, whatever its origin: a paste, a file, a table the assistant changed (`prepare_data`, `.to_long()`) or computed (`generate_data`, `run_data_code`). The copy is the stored version as it is (`DatasetStore.copy`): the same canonical CSV, dtypes, column order, notes, file name, `source` and `code`, nothing parsed or judged again, so a library costs no paste and no model call, and one chat turn, its `create_plot`, which an admin's message limits no longer count against since 2026-11-01. The bindings, their confirmation and the session language come along; the copy is the target's first version, numbered 1, with no undo history, and the session state's `anyplot.dataset_copied_from` (the bundle's `session.dataset_copied_from`) records the source's version number. Both sessions are looked up under the caller's user id, so another user's session is `404 session_expired` and no data crosses users. The target must be a fan-out session (`source: fanout`) of the same spec without data (`409 has_dataset`), and the source must hold data (`422 no_dataset`) confirmed with its current bindings (`422 unconfirmed`); neither may have a turn, a render or a paste in flight (`409 run_active`). While the panel is open the page also locks the data card (`DataPanel`'s `locked`, with a line that says to close the panel first), so no paste, binding change, confirmation or undo can land between the copies of the libraries, which a new paste's version number (1 again) would not reveal to the server. Each copy takes the dataset's size in the store again, so a counter of its own bounds it (`COPIES_PER_MINUTE` 30, `COPIES_PER_DAY` 150 per user in `agents/anyplot/plugins/ledger.py`, constants like the outcome beacon's; since the same day an admin is exempt from both, because three windows of `.plot_all()` copy 45 datasets within a minute, and the store's byte cap still refuses a copy it has no room for; a per-user byte budget in the store is the follow-up before public use, see [Risks](#risks-and-mitigations)). The `dataset_copy` attribution line carries the outcome, the row and column counts, the version number and the source session's salted hash, never data, and a copy writes no `data_input` telemetry line. Until 2026-11-01 the page posted the paste its own session stored again (`DatasetState.upload`, `replayData`) and kept `.plot_all()` disabled for changed or computed data; against a server without the copy route (`404 not_found`, the page deployed first) it still does that where the data came from a paste or a file, and otherwise each library stops with `copy_unavailable`. Since the same day `all` is in the URL as `&all=1`, which a reload presses again once the libraries are known, and a library switch writes the library's `language` to the URL along with `library`.

### Session language

Built since 2026-10-12 (session language): owner decision of 2026-10-12, a session has one language for the replies and for every text in the plot (since 2026-11-01 a plot keeps its own, see below), and it is server-set state (`anyplot.language`, `anyplot.language_source`), never a rule the root re-derives each turn. It starts as `en` with the source `default`, whatever the browser's locale, so a message such as "sin x" gets an English plot. A language is a short name or code that matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z -]{0,39}$` (`agents/anyplot/language.py`); a known English name or endonym is stored as its code (`Deutsch` and `German` as `de`), anything else as typed (`Swedish`).

Since 2026-11-08 (owner decision: the page is English, so everything stays English until the user picks another language), nothing the user writes or pastes sets the session language. Until then detection set it, from a paste before any typed message (source `pasted`, since 2026-10-14) and from the session's first typed message (source `detected`), and the thread repeated its `# reply language: de` line for each. Now the language a judge names only makes the page offer it. When the scope judge names another language for an in-scope typed message, or the notes kept around a pasted table (the scope judge's `lang`) or its column names (the dataset judge's) are in another language, the server records the offer (`anyplot.language_offered`, at most 12 languages; `language.offer`, `language.data_offer`), and the turn's `done` or the dataset route's answer carries it as `language: {language, source, offer: {language, basis}}`, with the basis `message` or `data`. Both judges already return `lang`, so an offer costs no extra model call. The thread shows it as one line, `# German detected in your message · .switch()` (or `in your data`), whose ⓘ says that the button sets the replies and the plot labels, that everything stays English until then, and that the menu at the top changes it at any time. Each language comes once per session, and ignoring the line changes nothing. No language is offered after a pick (`chosen`), for the session's own language, for a refused message, or for a page action (`create_plot`, `summarize_data`, `example_data`), which skips the judge. Both judges answer `en` for text without a clear language, so English is offered only for a typed message of at least three words (`sin x` offers nothing), never for data, and only once the user took another language: the chat may offer English back, once. `.switch()` sends `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/language` with `via: offer`, which writes what the chip writes, the session language and the plot's, with the source `offer`, and the line then reads `# replies and plot labels: Deutsch`. With a plot on screen the click starts no run: the next version rewrites the plot's text in the new language (see below), and the line adds `· the next plot version uses it`. A pick at the top or a request in the chat for the replies' language ("auf Deutsch bitte", the root's `set_language` with `applies_to` `both` or `replies`) sets `chosen`, which ends every offer and closes the open lines; the turn whose root picked drops its message's offer from `done`. A request for the plot's text alone (`applies_to` `plot`) is no pick of the replies: "Beschriftung auf Deutsch" in an English session draws German labels and still offers German replies. The fan-out's dataset copy takes the page session's language, its source and its offers, and never offers. A session that still holds `pasted` or `detected` reads it as `default`. For a session language the server knows by name (`language.ENGLISH_NAMES`) other than English, the session block adds `- Reply language: German; ...` and an action message ends with `Reply in German, the session language.`, both from the server's table, never from the stored value: in the live test of 2026-10-14 the summary after a German CSV, whose only user text is the English `[action]` line, came back in English in 3 of 4 probe runs without them, and afterwards in German in 8 of 8 with the session-block line alone and 3 of 3 with both. The keys outlive a library switch. The usage telemetry writes one content-free `language_offer` line per offer sent to the page and per pick through the language route (`via` `shown`, `offer` or `header`, the language as a closed code; [Agent telemetry](../reference/agent-telemetry.md)); the page sends no analytics event for them. The chip in the chat header (`English ▾`, `Deutsch ▾` since 2026-11-06, with the source in its accessible name; `lang: en`, `lang: de · from data`, `lang: de · detected`, `lang: de · chosen` before) lists the ten common languages, the site's ten most used visitor languages in Plausible in that order (`en`, `zh`, `de`, `vi`, `es`, `fr`, `nl`, `pt`, `ru`, `it`), and a free-text field for any other. A choice calls `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/language` and sets the source `chosen`, after which no language is offered. A request in the chat ("labels in English", "Beschriftung auf Deutsch") makes the same change through the root's `set_language(language)` tool, which the server validates like `set_bindings` and refuses while a plot run is active. Its result carries no value: it says whether the session language `changed` (`de` against `Deutsch` is no change), for the reply, and whether the plot is `plot_outdated`, that is, whether the library's latest rendered version has its text in another language than the plot language. Only `plot_outdated` makes the root write the next version in the new language, because the two can differ: a plot made before the user took German from an offer is still English. The rewrite itself is the server's (since 2026-11-01): a change whose base version was written in another language carries `language_changed`, and the adapter rewrites the plot's text whatever else the change request asks for. So the root's change request after `set_language` holds only the message's other changes, and a run without any gets the server's `pipeline.LANGUAGE_REWRITE_REQUEST`. Until then `root.md` gave the root that fixed request, which ends "change nothing else", and "make the title German and use a log scale" reached the plot with the German title only (user test of 2026-11-01).

Since 2026-11-01 (owner decision after a live test that day) the plot's text has its own language. In that test a German paste set `de`, the first plot came out German, and the English follow-up "Use a log scale on y" recorded `en` and made the adapter translate the title and switch to English number format, while the axes and months from the data stayed German; and "Make the title German", typed in English, made the root's `set_language` turn the replies German too, since `set_language` then set the one language for both. Now that request changes only the plot's language (`applies_to` `plot`) and the replies stay English. The plot language is resolved once per run (`session_state.plot_language`): the language the user asked for the plot's text (`anyplot.plot_language`, written by the chip and by `set_language`), else the language the session's latest rendered version is written in, in any library, else the session language. So the first plot is in the session language, and every later version keeps the language of the plot the user has: a pick of another reply language changes the replies, and the plot only through the chip, an accepted offer or a request for its text. `set_language(language, applies_to)` takes `plot` ("labels in English", "make the title German"), `replies` ("answer in French") or `both` (the default, which the chip also does). `replies` stores the plot language as it resolves at that moment, so a first plot made afterwards does not follow the new reply language. The fan-out's dataset copy carries the page's plot language too. Since 2026-11-08 the live test's German paste would only offer German: its first plot would be English unless the user took the offer.

Every prompt that carries a language fences it, data, never an instruction, because a chosen language is free text; the value is still the language to write in. The session block names how the session language was set on a fixed line (`LANGUAGE_SOURCE_LINES`: "chosen by the user (in the chat header, or asked of you)") and fences it as `<reply_language>` under a heading that says to write the replies in it; since 2026-11-01 a plot language that differs follows as `<plot_language>`, under a heading that says it changes only through `set_language`. The adapter and the reviewer get the plot language as `<plot_language>`. Since 2026-10-15 the block stands in the root's system instruction (see the agent table above), and the root prompt says it is part of the instruction, never of the user's message, to follow it and never to question it; until then ADK sent it inside the user's turn, between its instruction markers, and a production session of 2026-10-12 (`20261012T211551Z-bcc5bcf1`, "klingon" in the chip) had the root refuse it as an injection and stay in English, and on 2026-10-13 the page's English `[action]` messages still set the reply language until the prompt said they do not. A language the model cannot write gives English with one sentence that says so, and the adapter notes it in `changes`. The adapter's `AdaptRequest` and the reviewer's `ReviewRequest` carry the plot language as `plot_language`. The adapter writes the title, axis titles, legend entries and annotations in it, while column names and labels from the data keep their words and language (since 2026-10-16 an identifier is written as text for a reader, `Reaktionszeit_ms` as "Reaktionszeit (ms)", per the style guide's "Axis and Legend Titles"); a change whose base version was written in another language carries `language_changed`, and its literal budget of 300 characters grows by 1.5 times the literal text the plan replaces, up to the first adaptation's 2,000, so a translation fits but a long new annotation on top of it does not. The reviewer judges the plot's own text against it under VQ-06, fail-open: a title or an annotation plainly in another language fails, a name or a label from the data never does (a raw identifier as an axis or legend title does, since 2026-10-16), and a text whose language it cannot judge is no defect. Code, comments and the root's `change_request` stay English. The `changes` notes, which the page shows under the plot, are written in the language the user chats in (owner, 2026-11-01): the plot language, or the session language when it differs, which `AdaptRequest.reply_language` then carries, fenced as `<reply_language>`. After every run, `done` carries `language: {language, source}`, with the offer of its message's language when it made one, so the page syncs its chip after a turn in which `set_language` changed it. The fixed refusals keep their hand-written lines, in every chip language and Polish, with the English fallback, and since 2026-11-08 every one of them, the judge's included, uses the session language's code, whatever language the message is in (`language.refusal_lang`: a code as it is, a known name such as `Deutsch` through a small table, anything else English); until then a judged refusal used the message's language, so a German message in an English session got a German refusal. Since 2026-11-08 ReplyLanguage (`plugins/reply_language.py`) also holds a reply in the default English to its language, for a language the page offered: a German reply to a German message or paste in an English session is asked again in English, while any other sure guess there (an English summary that quotes a table's German words) costs no re-ask (`counts_as_wrong`, verdict `not_offered`). Versions made before a change keep their text, each version stores the language it was written in, and the feedback bundle carries `session.plot_language` and `plot_language_source`. Fonts for non-Latin scripts are the renderer's concern (a separate change). One register per language (live test round 4 of 2026-10-15, a German summary said "Aus Ihren Notizen" where the rest of the chat said "du"): the fixed refusals address the user informally where the language has the choice (du, tu, tú, je), `root.md` tells the root to keep that register in every reply, and `tests/unit/agents/test_reply_register.py` checks the fixed texts for the formal words of German, French, Spanish and Dutch.

### Plot memory and the library switch

Built since 2026-11-02 (owner decisions of that day): a plot switched to another library must not start from zero, and the decisions that carry are visible to the user. The plot memory (`agents/anyplot/memory.py`, state keys `anyplot.memory` and `anyplot.memory_seq`) holds entries `{id, kind, target, text, expect, turn}`: a closed `kind` (`title`, `subtitle`, `axis_label`, `scale`, `limits`, `ticks`, `color`, `marker`, `line_style`, `reference_line`, `annotation`, `legend`, `theme`, `language`, `sort`, `layout`, `other`), an optional closed `target` (`x`, `y`, `color`, `series`, `plot`), a short library-neutral text of at most 200 characters ("horizontal reference line at y = 7", "x-axis label: 'Zeit (h)'"), the verbatim text it puts in the plot, and the user turn it came in. A fixed list of single keys would not do: the owner's examples include a reference line, a LaTeX formula as an annotation and outliers as red stars. A new entry of a single-valued kind replaces the one of the same kind and target; `reference_line`, `annotation`, `marker` and `other` add beside the others, unless the new one names the entry it corrects (`replaces`, "move the line to 8"). A decision already held is no new one: since 2026-11-05 its text counts as the same when it differs only in case, spacing or a closing punctuation mark (`memory.same_wording`), while the verbatim text it puts in the plot must match exactly, and `root.md` has the root leave a decision listed under "Plot memory", in whatever words, out of `remember`, and pass a change of one with `replaces`. At most 30 entries and 3,000 characters; past that a new entry is refused, never an old one dropped. The memory lives in session state only and dies with the session.

The root hands a change's lasting decisions over with the run, `plot_pipeline(..., remember=[...])` (and `keep_plot_request`, whose items wait with the kept note), at most five, and the pipeline stores them only once that run ships (`pipeline.settle_memory`). A separate tool after the run was the brief's first idea, but the Budget plugin strips every tool from the root's call after a shipped plot (the budget-exempt closing reply), so the decisions travel with the run, which also saves two model calls a turn. `forget(entry_id)` removes one, before the run that removes it from the plot. The session block lists the entries with their ids inside a `<tool_notes>` fence. Every run that changes a version, or takes a switch's carry, hands the adapter and the reviewer the memory with the run's own items applied, under "Standing decisions" in place of the earlier requests (`AdaptRequest.decisions`, `ReviewRequest.decisions`); the raw request list stays the fallback while the memory is empty. Both prompts say that a decision may name another library's feature, to be carried out with this library's equivalent, and that one the plot type cannot show is left out.

The switch (`POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/library`) keeps the data, the bindings and their confirmation, the language, a kept first-plot note (since 2026-11-02; it was dropped before), the versions and the memory, and writes the carry (`agents/anyplot/switch.py`, `anyplot.carry`): the source plot's theme when the user asked for it, the texts to check (the memory's verbatim texts, else the source version's own `expected_texts`) and the source lineage's requests. A version is filed under its (spec, library) pair (`CodeVersion.spec_id`, `VersionStore.latest_rendered(target=...)`), so the later plot-type switch, which today the route refuses with `422 invalid`, reuses all of it. The first run in the new target takes the carry like a kept first-plot note: `previous` stays None, so it starts from the new library's catalogue code with the new-plot literal budget and the new library's hints, renders in the carried theme, checks the carried texts unless it brings its own, and clears the carry once it ships. The answer's `carry` says what would carry and whether to offer that run (`offer`): something carries, and the target has no plot or its latest plot is older than some entries (`newer`), so switching back to a library with an older version never resumes a stale lineage silently. From the menu the run waits for the user's click (the `carry_plot` action); `create_plot` (`.create_plot()`, `.start_over()`) starts from the catalogue code whatever base the root passes, the way out of broken code, without the carry but with the visible memory (owner decision of 2026-11-02: nothing clears the memory implicitly). `GET` and `DELETE /v1/sessions/{sid}/memory/{entry_id}` let the page list the entries and remove one, and `DELETE /v1/sessions/{sid}/memory` forgets all of them for a blank start. Once the memory ever held an entry (its id counter moved on, `memory.memory_written`), its entries alone are the decisions that stand and travel, also none: the raw lineage requests, the fallback of a session that never remembered anything, never bring back what the user removed, and a carry run reads the memory's verbatim texts at run time rather than the ones the switch wrote. In the chat ("erstelle den Plot bitte in seaborn"), the root's `switch_plot(library, plot)` writes nothing: the turn's `done` carries `switch: {library, plot}` (the BFF declares the field), and the page makes the same switch with `source: chat` and, with `plot`, the carry run without a second click; a refused switch (`not_eligible`) shows in the thread. The session block names the libraries the root can switch to. The fan-out's dataset copy takes the memory and a carry for its library over, and answers `carry`, so `.plot_all()` plots the tuned plot in every library with `carry_plot`. The usage telemetry counts the entries by kind on the `plot_switch` line and marks a run `carried` or `fresh`, never with a text. `agents/evals/carry.py` checks the switch live: it seeds the owner's five examples in matplotlib, switches to plotly and to ggplot2, runs the carry once, and checks in code that the label was found and that the exported code carries every intent in the target's own API.

New data and what carries (owner decisions of 2026-11-08, after persona case 06, where a second paste got the first table's title): a paste keeps the memory, which only the user clears, but what a switch carried from a plot of the data before goes with every new data version, as the kept first-plot note does. A paste, a change, `.to_long()` and an undo write `anyplot.carry: None` (`IngestedDataset.state_delta`, `dataset_versions._delta`), the page ends its `carryOffer` with them, and a switch or the fan-out's copy after the data changed takes only the asked theme of a plot of another data version, never its texts or requests (`switch.source_plot`: every shipped version records the data number it drew, `CodeVersion.data_version`; the theme by the owner's decision of 2026-11-09, as "plot the new data" keeps it), so besides the theme only the memory travels. A plot wish sent with the new data, typed or in the notes pasted with it, now reaches the plot of that data: `keep_plot_request` keeps it also when the library's latest plot drew data a later paste replaced (`CodeVersion.earlier_data`, its data number below the current data's original; `pipeline.plot_shows_replaced_data`), the session block says so on the plot versions line, and the next fresh run takes it. A blocked run keeps it as well when the root sent it as a change of that old plot (`base: previous` on unconfirmed data, which a live eval of that day showed): it becomes the first plot's note. When a column the replaced data was bound to has no column of the same name or meaning in the new table (`bindings.columns_gone`; a column that is there but no longer fits its role does not count), the paste answer says `columns_changed: true`, and the page moves the remembered decisions to the new `# data` line until the next plot, as `3 earlier decisions ▾ · .forget_all()`; ignored, they stay.

"Plot the new data" (owner decision of 2026-11-08) has its own action, `plot_new_data` (`RequestLedger.new_data`), and its base is decided in code at the run (`pipeline.new_data_base`): the library's latest version while it drew a version of the current data's paste (its `data_version` at or above the original's number) and of the same table (`CodeVersion.data_source`, since `generate_data` and `run_data_code` replace the table but keep the paste's original) with the same bindings, role for role (`CodeVersion.bindings`), and no first-plot note waits; otherwise the catalogue code, as `.start_over()` starts, with the memory, the reason in closed codes (`other_paste`, `other_data`, `bindings_changed`, `kept_note`, `no_plot`). An undo back to the data the version drew starts from it without the request. On the latest version the run gets one server-written change request, `DATA_CHANGE_REQUEST`, as a language rewrite gets `LANGUAGE_REWRITE_REQUEST`: draw the new data, keep every other choice, change only what was written for values the new data no longer has or fits, never a standing decision. The adapter prompt is unchanged, and the stored version leaves that request out of its lineage (`lineage_requests`), so a later change never reads it as the user's. Persona case 02 lost tuning only the code held to the fresh start; case 06 showed what old code does to other data, which the guard sends fresh. The fresh fallback keeps the theme the user asked for on the latest version (`run_theme`'s `latest`). `PlotResult.new_data` goes to the root, whose reply names it, and to the page's `plot` event, where the card's label reads `new data on v3` or `new data, new from the catalogue`; `pipeline_run.new_data` counts the bases. A rename or a rebind keeps the fresh start until that count shows it is frequent, and a typed change on changed data still runs from the previous version as before. The page shows `.start_over()` beside the button whenever the library has a plot, so the catalogue plot is one click away instead of after a run.

The page (since 2026-11-02, the owner's decisions of that day) picks the library in a menu like the language chip (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/LibraryChip.tsx`), in place of a pill per library: the chip names the library, `matplotlib ▾` (`lib: matplotlib · python` until 2026-11-06; its accessible name keeps the language), and opens a popover with the libraries grouped by language (Python, R, Julia, JavaScript) and, for the admin, `all libraries` after them. The pills' rules stay: the library in use cannot be picked again, the menu waits while anything is in flight, during a run and while the fan-out's panel is open, a pick turns `all` off, and without a session (ineligible or failed) the other libraries are links that open the page anew. A refused switch shows its reason under the chip. When the answer says `offer`, the plot button keeps its name (`.create_plot() →`, `.update_plot() →` when the library has an older plot) and its click with an empty box sends `carry_plot`, while the status line names the decisions it keeps (`2 decisions kept in seaborn · …`; since 2026-11-07, the button read "plot in seaborn with your 3 decisions" before); `.start_over()` at the status line's end stays the plot from the catalogue code, which keeps the remembered decisions but not the carry, and typed text goes as a normal turn, which also takes the carry. The remembered list's `.forget_all()` (`DELETE .../memory`) is the only blank start: nothing else clears the memory. The offer ends once a plot ships in that library, whatever turn shipped it, or with a switch whose answer has none. The memory stands on a chapter line as the remembered decisions (`Remembered.tsx`, the owner's "Gemerkt"; since 2026-11-08, the v4 design, above the composer before): `3 decisions ▾` on the latest `# plot` line, where the next plot takes them over (before the first plot on the latest chapter line), with `· full` once the memory has no room, unfolds the entries under the line, each with `.forget()`, which the DELETE route removes, then `.forget_all()` and an ⓘ with what the list does. The list is read again after every turn's `done`, after a switch and from a removal's answer; while the memory is empty or unknown no count shows, never `0 decisions`, and the controls wait while anything is in flight (the server would answer `409 run_active`). A removal is announced by a status region at the chat's foot, outside the thread's log. Each shipped version names the entries it kept and added (`plot.memory`, by id); its open card names both on its first line (`kept: … · new: …`, on a phone under the image) while its label keeps the request it answered, with the texts the page held when the version and the read after its turn arrived, so a later `.forget()` never rewrites an older card. Editing an entry comes later. The page adds no Plausible event: the server's `plot_switch` line and the run's `carried` mark cover the switch.

### Request flow for "Find the right plot type" (phase 2)

Until phase 2 the root knows only the page's spec and no catalogue. Since 2026-11-04 `root.md` ("What you help with") answers a request for another plot type ("mach daraus einen Violin-Plot") or a question which plots would suit the data in one or two statements: switching the plot type in the chat is planned but not possible yet, this page adapts this plot, a kind of plot that fits the data may be named in one clause, and the catalogue at anyplot.ai/plots has the others, where each plot's page leads to its own chat and the data is pasted again. It never says the catalogue has a particular type. The scope judge reads such requests as `in_scope` (an example line in `scope_judge.md`, the `plot_type` cases in `scope.evalset.json` and in `fixtures/prep`).

The compact `id|title|cat:tags` index (about 13.7k tokens, cache-eligible) is appended to the root's `static_instruction` in phase 2 only; step 1 is page-scoped and the index would cost those tokens on every root call. The root calls `find_specs` (AND across categories, OR within one, values checked against the tag vocabulary) and `get_spec_knowledge` (including the synced "What a good version looks like" bullets) for at most three candidates, derives filters from the pasted profile, and replies with `[[spec:id]]` tokens that the UI turns into cards. `select_spec` validates the choice and swaps the snapshot. Embeddings (pgvector 0.8.5 on Cloud SQL PostgreSQL 18 plus gemini-embedding on `eu`) are added only if evals show that the index is insufficient.

### anyplot-agents internal API (`/v1`)

Callable only with Cloud Run IAM; the BFF mirrors it under `/debug/agent`.

| Route | Body and result | Errors |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /v1/status` | `{libraries, model, location, version, provider, waiting, in_flight, user_max_runs, tier, paused, resets_at}`: `waiting` entries and runs `in_flight` in every lane of the run queue, the caller's own cap (`AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS`, or `AGENT_ADMIN_USER_MAX_RUNS` for an admin), the tier the request was served as, and whether the service's day budget is spent until `resets_at`, the next 00:00 UTC | |
| `GET /v1/budget` | `{date, since, started_at, resets_at, paused, model, tokens, budget_tokens, usd, budget_usd, users, users_at_limit, limits}`: the admin's live view of the day from the usage book (`UsageBook.snapshot`), counts only, never a user id, for the instance's lifetime; an admin counts only at the caps that bind every tier. The BFF's `/debug/agent/budget` serves it behind the admin gate | |
| `POST /v1/sessions` | `{user, spec_id, library, locale, snapshot: CatalogueSnapshot{spec_id, title, description, data_roles[], notes[], code, library_version}, source?}` (at most 64 KB; `source`: `fanout` only, for the admin fan-out's sessions, whose usage telemetry it files apart from `live`) → `{session_id, eligibility, roles}` (`roles` since 2026-11-04: the spec's roles as the dataset route names them, for the page header) | `422 not_eligible` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/library` | `{library, snapshot, source?}` → `{session_id, eligibility, target, carry}`; keeps the dataset, bindings, confirmation, language, kept first-plot note, versions and plot memory, and writes the switch's carry (`switch.py`, see [Plot memory and the library switch](#plot-memory-and-the-library-switch)) | `422 not_eligible`, `422 invalid` (another spec), `409 run_active` |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/memory`, `DELETE /v1/sessions/{sid}/memory[/{entry_id}]` | the plot memory `{entries, max_entries, written}` (`memory.py`); a DELETE answers the memory after removing one entry, or all of them without an id | `404 not_found`, `409 run_active` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset` | `{text, confirmed?, source?}` → `{preview, profile, bindings, warnings, context_lines, roles, version, language}`; `source` is the name of a dropped file (at most 1,024 characters), screened on its own and answered as `file_name`, never a note; `context_lines` are the judged notes pasted around the table; `language` is the session language as `{language, source}` after the paste, with `offer` when the notes or the column names are in another language the page has not been offered yet and the user picked none (see [Session language](#session-language)); `roles` lists the spec's data roles as `{name, kinds, required, variadic, description, non_negative}`; a paste is `version` 1 and drops an earlier paste's undo history | `413 too_long`, `422 unparseable`, `422 formula` (text that asks for a formula, which the root's `generate_data` computes in the chat; never transcribed), `403 data_refused`, `422 code` (a program that generates data, which the root's `run_data_code` runs in the chat; checked before any parse) |
| `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/bindings` | `[Binding]`, validated by `bindings.apply` | `409 run_active`, `422 invalid` with the check's `errors` lines |
| `PUT /v1/sessions/{sid}/language` | `{language, via?}`, a short name or code that matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z -]{0,39}$`; `via` is `header` (the chip, the default) or `offer` (the `.switch()` of an offer line) → `{language, source}`: the user's pick in its stored form (`English` comes back as `en`), source `chosen` from the chip, after which no language is offered, or `offer` from an offer; the next reply and the next version use it (see [Session language](#session-language)) | `409 run_active`, `422` (the pattern, or another `via`) |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/convert` | `{data_base64, sheet?}` → `{text, format, kind, rows, columns, sheet, sheets, sheet_count, warnings}`: a dropped binary data file converted in the renderer's sandbox (`POST /convert`, `harness.py --convert`) into the text the paste box takes; nothing stored, no model call | `413 too_long`, `422 invalid`, `422 file_<reason>` with `measured` and `limit` for a broken cap, `403 session_closed`, `409 run_active`, `429 rate_limited` / `daily_limit` (`AGENT_USER_CONVERSIONS_PER_MINUTE` / `_PER_DAY`) / `locked_out`, `503 capacity`, `503 convert_unavailable` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/confirm` | → `{confirmed, rows, columns}`: step 1 of two, the user confirmed the dataset with its bindings | `409 run_active`, `422 no_dataset` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/undo` | → `{preview, profile, bindings, warnings, context_lines, roles, version}`: the version before the root's last `prepare_data` or `generate_data` is the current dataset again, bindings carried over, unconfirmed | `409 run_active`, `422 nothing_to_undo` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/copy` | `{from_session}` → the dataset route's shape (the fan-out session's first version, numbered 1) plus `confirmed`, `complete`, `missing_roles` and `copied_from`: the admin fan-out's byte-for-byte copy of the confirmed current version of `from_session`, a session of the same user and spec, into this fan-out session, with its bindings, their confirmation and the session language; no parse, no judge, no paste counted (see [the fan-out](#two-steps-data-first-then-the-plot)) | `404 session_expired` (either session, also another user's), `409 run_active`, `409 has_dataset`, `422 no_dataset`, `422 unconfirmed`, `422 invalid`, `429 rate_limited` / `daily_limit`, `503 capacity` |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/data.csv` | → the current dataset version's canonical CSV as stored (at most 4 MiB): the data panel's read-only view of every row (`.view_all()`, since 2026-11-02), which the page reads with a strict RFC 4180 reader and shows in the MIT edition of the MUI X Data Grid, every value as the file holds it; no model call, no state write, not blocked by a turn | `422 no_dataset` |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/messages?theme=` | `{text}` (at most 2,000 chars) or `{action}` (`create_plot`, `carry_plot`, `summarize_data`, `example_data`), and the optional query parameter `theme` (`light` or `dark`, the site theme the page shows, which a plot run of the turn renders unless the user asked for a theme) → SSE `anyplot/1`, started at once (only a `plot_pipeline` or `run_data_code` call waits in the run queue, mid-turn); before the scope judge, `budget`, `session_closed`, `rate_limited` and `daily_limit` are fixed refusals right after `ready`; `done` carries `bindings`, `data_confirmed`, `language` (`{language, source}`) and, after a turn whose `prepare_data`, `generate_data` or `run_data_code` changed the data, `dataset: {preview, profile, bindings, warnings, context_lines, roles, changes, version}` plus `code` for a version `run_data_code` computed (`version` 1 when `generate_data` or `run_data_code` made the session's first data) | `413 too_long`, `409 run_active` (a turn, a toggle or a paste in flight in this session), `422` for a `theme` other than `light` or `dark`; a full queue is the stream's `error{code:"capacity"}` since 2026-10-14 (`503 capacity` before the stream until then), a sandbox run past the user's `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` the stream's `error{code:"run_limit"}` since 2026-10-21 |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/cancel` | Sets `abort_signal` and cancels the task; a waiting sandbox run leaves the queue | |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/turn` | Header `Last-Event-ID: <run_id>.<n>` → SSE `anyplot/1`: the session's latest turn from its event `n + 1` on, live until the turn's stream ends (`agents/turn_log.py`), for a page whose stream the edge lost while the turn ran on; a read that never holds or stops the run | `404 not_found` (another turn is the latest, or none), `422` (an id of another form) |
| `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/versions/{version}/render` | `{theme}` → `{status: ok\|needs_attention\|failed, reason?, artifacts}`: the theme toggle renders `theme` of a finished version (0 is the latest) from its stored run form, synchronously, with no model call; `reason` is `canvas_padded`, `render` or `error` | `404 not_found`, `409 run_active` (the session has a turn, a toggle or a paste in flight; per session since 2026-10-21), `503 capacity` (no render slot in time, or the render store is full) |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/artifacts/{name}?v=&theme=` | Allowlist `plot-light.png`, `plot-dark.png`, the version's code artifact (`plot.py` for a Python library; `plot.R`, `plot.jl`, `plot.js` or `plot.tsx` for another language, since 2026-10-24), for a JavaScript version `plot.html` (since 2026-10-31), and `data.csv`; a PNG exists only for a rendered theme and carries the footer strip (a 3200x1800 render is served as 3200x1864) unless `AGENT_WATERMARK=false`; `plot.html` is the self-contained interactive page in `theme` (default: the version's), built on demand through the renderer's `POST /page`, never stored, wrapped in a sandboxed frame (`render/page.py`) and answered as an attachment with `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox` | `404`; for `plot.html` also `409 run_active` (it holds the session's registry entry like the theme toggle), `503 capacity`, `503 page_unavailable` (a renderer without `POST /page`, or none reachable), `500 page_failed` |
| `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/bundle?version=&include_data=` | The server-assembled feedback case bundle; its images carry the footer strip like the artifact route; with `AGENT_KEEP_REFUSALS` it also lists the session's refused messages as `refusals` `[{verdict, lang, text, at}]` | `404` |
| `DELETE /v1/sessions/{sid}` | Purges the session, the dataset store and the render store | |
| any | `404 session_expired` after scale-to-zero or the idle sweeper | |

Headers: `X-Anyplot-User` (the HMAC id from the admin BFF), or for the public lane `X-Anyplot-Anon` (the page's tab id) with `X-Anyplot-Network` (the client's IPv4 address or IPv6 /64), both salted per process on arrival (see [Bounds](#bounds)), plus `X-Anyplot-Tier` and `X-Request-Id`. Caller check: Cloud Run IAM verifies the ID token and forwards it with the signature replaced, so the app decodes the claims without signature verification and requires `aud` in `AGENT_SERVICE_URLS` and `email` in `AGENT_ALLOWED_CALLERS`. Cloud Run requires the service URL as `aud` even for a request to a tag URL, so `AGENT_SERVICE_URLS` holds the service URL, and a deploy smoke mints its token for that URL while it calls the `candidate` tag. When a request carries `X-Serverless-Authorization`, Cloud Run checks only that header and passes `Authorization` through unverified, so the app then reads `X-Serverless-Authorization` and ignores `Authorization`. A missing header is rejected when `ENVIRONMENT=production`; the check is skipped only in development. This is weak while anyplot-api runs as the shared compute service account; a dedicated API service account is an owner task before public use.

## Contracts

Schemas live in `agents/anyplot/schemas.py`: `ColumnProfile{name≤64, dtype, missing, unique, min, max, mean, median, top≤12, months?}` (`top` lists every value of a text or boolean column with at most 12 distinct ones, else its five most frequent, since live test round 8 of 2026-10-16, where the root never saw the sixth category `Übrige` of a six-row energy mix and told the user the plot had added it; `mean` and `median` for integer and number columns, six significant digits; `months` the months 1 to 12 of a text column whose every cell names a month, at least three, because the five top values cannot say whether `Jan` to `Mai` is the whole column, and `sort` orders such a column by the calendar, live test round 4 of 2026-10-15; the model-facing JSON leaves every empty field out), `DatasetProfile{rows, columns≤50, sample≤5 rows with cells≤40 chars fenced as <user_data>, source_format, decimal, warnings, list_columns?}` (`list_columns` the columns that came from the list fields of JSON records expanded into rows; a re-parse of a later version leaves it unset), `Binding{role ^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]{0,31}$, column}` (digits and capitals because 21 of 325 specs name roles such as `log2_fold_change`, `lower_95` or `temperature_K`), `PipelineArgs{change_request≤600, base: catalogue|previous, theme: light|dark = light}`, `AdaptRequest{code (working form), profile, bindings, loader_columns, hints, change_request, feedback, previous_plan?, allow_full, plot_language, language_changed}`, `Edit{find (exactly one match), replace}`, `AdaptPlan{edits≤20, full_code≤24 KB (repair attempts only), title≤120, changes≤5}`, `ReviewRequest{render_id, code, bindings, profile_summary, spec_brief, change_request, gate_notes, plot_language}`, `Defect{id ∈ fixed set, theme light|dark|both|code, observed, target, likely_cause}`, `Verdict{ok, defects≤5}`, `PlotResult{status ok|needs_attention|failed|not_ready, reason?, attempts, artifacts, changes, residual_defects}`, where `artifacts` lists the PNG of the rendered theme only.

### Parse

`agents/anyplot/data/parse.py` has no ADK import. Limits: UTF-8 at most 1 MB for a paste (200 KB until 2026-10-14) and 2 MB for a table the service computed (`MAX_COMPUTED_BYTES`, passed as `max_bytes` by every re-parse of the service's own CSV), a canonical CSV of at most 4 MiB, 50 columns, 20,000 rows and 200 characters per cell. It rejects NUL and control characters, strips the BOM, applies NFC, and strips zero-width and bidi control characters. Headers are canonicalised (NFC, control characters stripped, names over 64 characters renamed, duplicates suffixed, empty names become `column_N`, each rename a warning), and the canonical names are written into `data.csv`, which is the single source of column names. Formats: JSON records, a column map, an object of values or an array of `[category, value]` pairs (the last two become the columns `category`, the keys kept as text, and `value`, source format `json_pairs`; nesting depth at most 2, except the nested objects of records described below; a repeated key keeps its last value, with a warning), a JSON-stat 2.0 dataset (since 2026-10-14, source format `json_stat`; see below), JSON Lines (since 2026-10-16, live test round 6, source format `json_lines`: when the whole text is no JSON but its first line is one JSON object, every non-empty line must be one and they read as JSON records; a line that is not names its number and the JSON error; up to 10 lines above the first object, a title or an export's comments, become notes when every line from there on starts with `{`; a dropped file named `.jsonl` or `.ndjson` reads as JSON Lines first, so one record is one row, while a pasted single object of values stays category/value pairs, with a warning when its values mix text and numbers), or delimited text with the delimiter sniffed from `, ; \t |`. Since 2026-10-16 every refusal names its cause (owner rule: people paste reasonably clean data, but a refusal says what it fails on): the agents service answers a refused paste with `cause`, the parser's message, which the BFF passes on one line of at most 300 characters and the page shows after its own text. A table whose rows have more values than its header names every ragged line with its field count, grouped by count, the first five lines of a group and the rest counted, and is never repaired; empty fields past the header's last column are left out with a counted warning, and the warning about short rows names their lines. Text that starts like JSON and is none of the readers' shapes names the JSON (or, behind an assignment or with a call in it, the Python) error's line and column, counted from the paste's own first line, with a hint for a comma after each line of objects, a missing comma and unquoted keys; JSON that Python reads as names (`true`, `null`, an unquoted key) gets the JSON error. The page shows the cause in place of its own line. A damaged file the converter refuses as `corrupt` names its broken part from a closed set (owner decision of 2026-10-16: `zip`, `workbook`, `sheet`, `parquet`, `arrow`, `npy_header`, `npy_data`), carried from the sandbox's `status.json` through the renderer's `ConvertRun.damage`, the agents service's `damage` and the BFF to the page, never as an exception's text; the renderer leaves the key out while it is empty, so an agents service deployed before the field reads every other answer. Since 2026-10-15 (live test round 3) two more shapes read. A table aligned with spaces (source format `whitespace`: a weather or statistics office's `.txt`, such as MeteoSwiss' homogenised monthly series) splits at runs of two or more spaces, so a header such as `Max Temp` stays one name; a tab is no separator there. Spaces are tried only when none of the four real delimiters finds a table (a CSV or TSV whose text fields hold spaces stays CSV or TSV), and they need stricter evidence, because they also separate the words of prose, the items of a list (`Hund 7`, which stays with the transcriber) and the keys and values of a metadata block: at least two data rows, a header line right above the first data row that holds no value (two or more years excepted, a wide table's `Land  2020  2021`), and no key-value block (a header whose first cell, or rows whose every first cell, ends with a colon). Owner principle of the review of 2026-10-15: a silent misread is the worst failure, so a refusal beats a guess. Single spaces split a table only of numbers and dates (every row at the header's width, no text cell, no two neighbouring fields that read as one value such as `1 234` or `2024-01-01 12:00`), with a warning on every such read: a single space also sits inside a two-word name (`Anna Lee`), so a table with a text column goes to the transcriber. A field in matching quotes loses them. A short row of a fixed-width layout (a blank cell) has its fields placed under the header's columns by their character position, never padded at the end, also below the last full row until a note, a source line or a summary row (`Total`, left out like other summary rows); a field that overlaps no column or more than one, columns whose own ranges overlap, and a row that does not line up, also a value row right below the table, are `unparseable`. The metadata block above such a table becomes notes like any other left-out lines. A line of the paste that only declares the missing-value mark (`Missing values: NA`, `nodata = -9999`; a sentinel number, negative or at least 999 in size, or one to three symbols or letters, never an answer such as `none`) counts as missing already while the table is found, so a row of marks stays a row; it blanks the cells of number and date columns only, a number matching by value (`-999.0`), with one warning that counts them, while a text category `M` or `NA` (Namibia) stays (owner decision of 2026-10-15). JSON records may nest objects, which become columns named with dots (`country.value`, `indicator.id`) down to three levels; an object nested deeper, or a field holding a list of objects or lists, is left out with one warning that names it (owner rule: canonical data is one flat CSV). A field holding a list of values expands to long format (owner decision of 2026-10-15, one measurement curve per record with `x: [...]` and `y: [...]`): the lists of one record expand together into one row per value, paired by position (so independent lists of one length, such as `tags` and `scores`, are paired too; the warning names every expanded field), the other fields repeated; an empty list leaves its cells empty, non-empty lists of different lengths in one record are `unparseable` with a message that names the record, the fields and their lengths, and so is a field that is a list in one record and a single value in another. When no text or whole-number field has a different value in every record, compared as `data.csv` will hold it (a unique measurement such as a temperature never counts), a first column `record_id` numbers the records, or `record_id_2` and so on when a field already has that name. Above the 20,000-row cap each record keeps evenly spaced values with its first and last one, the smallest thinning that fits, with a warning that gives the counts and the remedy (fewer points for one record, fewer records or points for several), which the root passes on. Records may also come wrapped as data APIs answer: an array of metadata objects and exactly one list of records (the World Bank's `[{page, pages, per_page, total, ...}, [records]]`), or an object with exactly one list of records beside other fields (`{"data": [...], "meta": {...}}`, also under `results`, `records`, `items` or any other key); the metadata's text, number and boolean fields go to the notes as `<name>: <value>`, and two lists of records, or metadata without records, are `unparseable`, since the parser takes no table by guess. A list beside the records counts as their schema only when its objects hold nothing but descriptor keys (`name`, `type`, `format`, `description` and the like) and their names cover every field of the records; descriptors beside an empty list of records are `unparseable`. A schema with rows of cells (CKAN, Socrata, Frictionless, pandas `orient="split"`: column names under `fields`, `columns`, `schema.fields` or `meta.view.columns`, rows as lists) reads as one record per row; rows without names that fit them are `unparseable`, so the schema is never taken for the data. BigQuery's own `rows: [{"f": [{"v": ...}]}]` shape is not read (its cells are a list of objects, which is left out, so the paste is refused). JSON-stat is still recognised first. The dataset pre-filter (`instruction_like`) reads the table with its constant columns, which come back into `data.csv` when the notes are dropped. The root's prompt lists every control of the chat page (`.open()`, `.create_plot()`, `.update_plot()`, `.undo()`, the binding dropdowns, the language chip and so on) and forbids naming any other, since it once told a user to pick "a separator that recognises spaces", a setting that does not exist; `tests/unit/agents/test_prompt_controls.py` checks that each control the prompt names is a label in the page's code, and, since a missing `.open()` once made it deny that the page takes files, that each button of the data card is named in the prompt's data panel bullet. JSON-stat is what statistics offices' APIs answer (the Swiss BFS PxWeb API, Eurostat, Statistik Austria, Statistics Norway): an object with `"class": "dataset"`, `id`, `size`, `dimension` and `value`; a JSON-stat 1.x bundle of one dataset is read too, a bundle of several, a collection or a lone dimension is `unparseable`. It is the one shape nested deeper than 2 (about five levels), and the 50-level bracket count still applies. The parser checks it strictly before it builds a row (as many sizes as dimensions, the product of the sizes at most 20,000 and equal to the number of values, `value` as an array or as a sparse object of positions, every dimension with as many categories as its size, `category.index` as an array or an object of positions), and refuses any mismatch as `unparseable` with a message that names no data. It flattens the dataset into a long table (owner rule: long format, no wide pivot; `prepare_data` can pivot later): one column per dimension with more than one category, named by the dimension's label (else its id), its cells the category labels (else the codes) kept as text, never typed and never missing (`NA` for Namibia stays `NA`, a year code stays text; a label two categories share gets each one's code in parentheses), then one column `value`, the only typed column, whatever the dataset's unit (a dimension named `value` in any case becomes `<label> (dimension)`, so the measure keeps the name), in the row-major order of `value`; a missing value stays empty, and `status` symbols are ignored with one warning. A dimension of one category is the same in every row, so it is no column: `<dimension>: <category> (the same in every row)` goes into the notes (`context_lines`), as does a pasted text column with one value in every row (`CONSTANT_NOTE_SUFFIX`, since 2026-11-05: the suffix says what the line is, in the data card and for the assistant, which then states the value as a fact of the data; a reading aid, never a mark of trust, since a pasted note can end the same way and every note stays data), after the dataset's `label`, its `source` and its `note` lines, so the pre-filter and the scope judge screen them like any pasted note; the warning only counts the dimensions left out. Since 2026-10-14 a pasted delimited or JSON table gets the same treatment: a text column with the same value in every row of at least two (`Kanton` = `Schweiz`, `Geschlecht` = `Geschlecht - Total`) moves into the notes as `<name>: <value>` with one warning that names the columns, never the values, unless fewer than two columns would remain; a re-parse of the service's own CSV (`parse_dataset(canonical=True)`: `prepare_data`, `generate_data`, `run_data_code`, `.to_long()`) keeps such a column, because an operation or the user's code made it. Only text columns move (owner decision of 2026-10-14). The values live in the notes only while the notes are kept: the parse keeps the table with its constant columns at hand (`ParsedDataset.with_constants`), the dataset judge reads that table, and a path that drops the notes (a refusal of the notes judge through `without_context`, or a store without screened notes such as a fixture seed or an eval's ingest) takes it, so the columns come back. Since 2026-10-15 a pasted column with a header whose every cell is missing (an API export's unused schema fields: an ECB CSV carried 14 such columns) leaves the table with one warning that names the columns, before the 50-column check, also when only one column with values remains; a canonical re-parse keeps it. A JSON table may therefore carry up to 200 keys (`MAX_RAW_COLUMNS`) before that check. A missing cell is the empty cell in `data.csv` and nowhere else: the run form's loader reads per column when the dataset has a text column (`keep_default_na=False` with `na_values` of `[""]` for a text column and the parser's missing tokens, bound once to `na_tokens`, for every other column, so the exported `plot.py` still reads a user's own R or spreadsheet export with `NA` or `-` in a number or date column), so a category `NA` (Namibia, North America) from a JSON key or a JSON-stat category survives the loader, `prepare_data` reads the CSV the same way, and a canonical re-parse keeps every text cell. A pasted `NA` is still missing (R and many exports write it so), except in a text column of two- or three-letter upper-case codes with at least two other codes, where it stays a code with a warning (owner decision of 2026-10-14). Pasted text is rarely a bare table, so since 2026-10-14 (owner rule) the delimited reader first finds the table among the lines (`table_block`): its data rows are the lines of the most common field count with numbers or dates in the same columns, the header is the line right above the first data row, and a title, an export's metadata (Open-Meteo writes two such lines above its header) or a sentence that explains the columns above it, a source line or a note below the last data row, and up to three summary rows at the end (`Total`, `Summe`, `Mittelwert`…) are left out, each kind with one warning in the preview; the delimiter is sniffed from the first 50 lines the same way, by the table with the most values. A table without a number or date column keeps every line, as before. The lines left out above and below the table are not thrown away (owner rule of 2026-10-14): they are the user's notes about the data, kept as `ParsedDataset.context_lines`. Each left-out record becomes one text line, its fields cleaned like cells (invisible characters removed, whitespace and line breaks collapsed to one space), the empty ones dropped and the rest joined with the delimiter's display form (`, ` for a comma, `; ` for a semicolon, ` | ` for a pipe, a space for a tab), so a sentence that its commas split reads as it was pasted. Summary rows are data and never notes. All lines together hold at most 2,000 characters, the chat message cap (`MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS`), with a warning when the cap cut them. The profile and `data.csv` stay as they were; a JSON or literal paste (except JSON-stat) and every re-parse of the service's own CSV (`find_table=False`) have no notes. A Python literal (`[`, `{` or `(`, also behind an assignment such as `cat_day = {...}`, which is stripped) is read like JSON with `ast.literal_eval`, which evaluates literals only; a name or a call is refused, and a bracket count that skips quoted strings and comments refuses more than 50 nesting levels before anything is evaluated. Text that starts with a bare `(` and is neither JSON nor a literal (a header such as `(in %) Land`, prose in parentheses) is delimited text as before. Everything is read as text first, then dates with explicit formats (an ambiguous day/month order is a warning), then numbers; a decimal comma is accepted only with a date guard. Since 2026-10-15 the parse keeps what the user pasted, or a warning says what changed (owner rule: precision is never lost silently). A number keeps its value, written in its shortest round-trip form (`1.50` is `1.5`, `1.5e3` is `1500.0`: the spelling changes, the value does not; owner decision of 2026-10-15: the value is enough, and one warning counts the numbers whose trailing zeros, exponent or plus sign went and names their columns); a number with more significant digits than a float64 holds (15 to 17, such as `0.1234567890123456789`, or a whole number past 2^53 beside decimals) is stored rounded with a warning that counts the cells, also from JSON and Python literals, whose float texts are kept until the typing reads them; a column of whole numbers past the int64 range stays text, so no digit is lost; an integer column's profile `min` and `max` are exact integers. In a text column, a cell whose surrounding spaces were removed (not the padding of a space-aligned table, nor the space after a CSV delimiter, which is layout) and a token such as `None` or `-` read as missing are counted in a warning; NFC composition gets one warning that counts the lines it changed. JSON's `NaN`, `Infinity` and `-Infinity` are missing values, counted in one warning. A whole number past int64 written with dot groups stays text too. Timestamps with fractions below a microsecond beside a date outside 1677 to 2262 stay text with a warning, because pandas holds a nanosecond only in that range and the loader would have left the column text without a word. The page's preview shows each cell with its whitespace: a line break as `↵`, a tab as `→` and every space of a run as `·`, drawn muted and named for a screen reader, so `a  b` never reads as `a b`; the profile's sample and top values, which the model reads, stay one line. Two roundings are deliberate and stay (owner decisions of 2026-10-15): the profile's `mean` and `median` carry six significant digits, for the model only (the page never shows them); and an integer past 2^53 in the profile loses its last digits in the browser, whose numbers are float64, which only touches the binding hint that names a negative minimum. Since 2026-10-15 numbers with unambiguous thousands groups are numbers too (`_as_grouped_numbers`): two or more dot or comma groups (`7.184.003`, `1,234,567`, which a German-format paste had left as text), a comma group before a dot decimal (`1,234.5`), or apostrophe and space groups (Swiss `1'234'567`, `1 234,5` with a no-break, narrow no-break or thin space too) where some cell holds two groups or a decimal part, the decimal mark the other one and the same in every cell; dotted dates stay dates. A single space or apostrophe group (`123 456`), IPv4 addresses and any column whose name says id, code, version, phone or postcode (`ID_WORDS`) stay as they are. A column of `1,234` shapes reads as comma thousands beside a comma-grouped column, and a column of `1.234` shapes as dot thousands beside a decimal-comma or a dot-grouped column, each with a note; a dot number elsewhere (a time `12.30`, a rating `4.5`) is no evidence, so a bare `1,200` stays a decimal comma with its note. In a file with an unambiguous decimal-comma column (`6,07`) the note goes. Since 2026-10-22 (issue #12224) a column of `1,234` shapes whose unit is a currency (`€ $ £ CHF EUR USD GBP`) reads as comma thousands with a note, since a money amount mostly has at most two decimals, so `$1,200` is 1200 while `$1,25` stays 1.25; a file that speaks for a decimal comma keeps the question instead (owner decision of 2026-10-22): one delimited by semicolons, or one with a column of dot groups (`7.184.003`, also one kept as text past int64), of `1.234` shapes or of comma numbers with a zero-led group (`0,125`), since a fuel price of `1,789 €` has three decimals. A whole number past int64 in a column read as comma thousands stays text, as with dot groups. A column whose every cell carries the same unit (`65 %`, `15,6 h`, `€ 5`, `CHF 1.200`, also with a no-break space between number and unit) is read as numbers, the unit moving into the column name (`Anteil (%)`) through the canonical-header path, with a warning, unless the header already names the unit; a column where only some cells carry a unit, or two units, stays text, as does one whose "unit" is a month, a boolean or an ordinal suffix (`5 Jan`, `1st`). The output is a canonical `data.csv` (comma, dot decimal, ISO dates) in a session-scoped dataset store (in memory, tied to the session's lifetime, the idle sweeper and the DELETE route; never a `user:` artifact, which would outlive the session and be listable by the model), plus the profile and default bindings (role dtype match, then name similarity, each column once; a categorical, text or boolean role takes a column with one distinct value only when no column with two or more fits, so a constant `Kanton` never becomes the series; since 2026-10-15 such a role ranks a real text column of 2 to 60 distinct values first, then an integer column of years (named so, `Jahr`, `year`, then an unnamed run of every year of its range), then a text column with more values, an ordinal integer (every value of a short range, `Quartal` 1 to 4), other integers of few values, a text column of numbers and any other integer last, and a number role that takes no dates ranks year columns after every measure (name similarity decides first; a price of 1,000 to 2,200 is no year without a year name or a full yearly run), while a time axis (`x (datetime/numeric)`) ranks them first among the numbers, so `bar-basic` on a year and two measures binds the year as the category and a measure as the value; since live test round 3 of 2026-10-15 a number role that takes no dates (a measure: `y`, `value`, `size`) also ranks weak measures last, `weak_measures`: a column with one distinct value in a table of at least five rows (a World Bank `decimal` of 0s), a year column, and a month, quarter or week of the year (an integer column named `Month`, `Monat`, `Quartal`, `Woche` and the like within that part's range, or every value from 1 to 12 or 1 to 4 beside a year column; unit and duration words such as `Minuten`, `Days to ship` or `kW` are measures); an optional measure role and every member of a number family past the first never take a weak measure, so they stay empty rather than plot a flat line or a month (owner decision of 2026-10-15: an optional measure takes any measure that is not weak), and a weak measure a family leaves out never becomes a series label; an axis role (`x`) avoids only constants and ranks years first, so a scatter of `Month, Temperature` or `Rank, Points` keeps the first column on x, and, only in a table expanded from JSON list fields (`DatasetProfile.list_columns`), on a spec with an x axis: the number columns outside the lists hold one value per record (`record_values`, never one named like an axis such as `zeit_s` or `Frequency (Hz)`) and rank like weak measures, for x too, a measure that must take one takes a parameter before a record number (`id`, `record_id`, `Kanal`), and after the years x ranks a list column named like an independent variable first, so three curves of `probe`, `temperatur_c`, `zeit_s` and `spannung_v` bind x to the time, y1 to the voltage and the series to the probe, never x to the temperature (live test round 4 of 2026-10-15), while a bar chart may still take the temperature as its value; a pasted table binds as before (review of #12197: a general "one value per group" rule demoted `step` on a scatter of `step, target, reading`, `quota` in `country, year, quota, exports` and reordered `speed_kmh, stopping_distance`); a categorical, text or boolean role ranks a column of codes (`country.id`, `iso3code`, short capitals such as `AT`) after a name column related by its name (the same stem, `country.id` and `country.value`, `product_code` and `product_name`) with the same number of distinct and missing values, after name similarity, so the series reads `Austria`, not `AT`;
