| The cgroup's `memory.current` is unreadable | `MemAvailable` from `/proc/meminfo` is logged with every render and refuses a render below 1,024 MiB (`503 low_memory`) |
| Many concurrent sandboxes crash a 4 GiB instance; one is safe | One render slot per renderer, themes one after the other, a 30 s wait for the slot (`503 busy`) |
| `/healthz` never reaches the container | No health route; Cloud Run's TCP startup probe |
| matplotlib renders in about 2.5 s and seaborn in about 3.3 s at p95, at 2 vCPU | `AGENT_RENDER_TIMEOUT_S` (60) is far above a normal render |

stdout and stderr are read while the sandbox runs and kept bounded (the first and last 4 KiB of stdout, the last 64 KiB of stderr, of which 2,000 characters go back), and the outputs are read with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `fstat`, so a symlink, a FIFO or an oversized file is refused (`output_rejected`). The probe is written by the code under test, so the renderer drops one nested deeper than 16 levels or holding a number a float cannot carry, and turns `NaN` into null; the harness's own peak-memory and CPU line is taken only as finite numbers in range. An `OSError` on the host (a full volume, a failed fork) is `503 io`, never a plot failure. The log carries ids, exit codes, reasons and timings, never code, data, stderr or images; `/var/log/sandbox.log` on the host records every launcher call and is the source of truth for launcher metrics.

uvicorn does not cancel a request handler whose client went away, so `POST /render` runs the render as a task, races it against the connection's `http.disconnect`, and cancels it when the caller is gone; the executor's kill path then stops the sandbox and frees the slot. Because Cloud Run's front end may not pass a closed connection on, the `remote` backend also posts `/render/{job_id}/cancel` when its own render is cancelled (an abort, the request deadline) or its answer does not come in time. `job_id` and the requested themes are an idempotency key (the backend sends one request per theme under the job's id): the backend's retry after a connection error or a front-end 429 or 5xx may replay a request that did reach the renderer, so a replay with the same payload joins the render in flight or gets the stored answer of a finished one (the last 16), and another payload under the same key is refused with `409 job_conflict`; a job's code never runs twice because of a retry, and the last caller to leave is the one whose departure cancels the render.

The `remote` backend mints the ID token for `AGENT_RENDER_URL`, the renderer's service URL, from the metadata server on Cloud Run; in development it sends `AGENT_RENDER_TOKEN` when set, or the `id_token` of the developer's Application Default Credentials (`agents/README.md`). It retries a connection error or a Cloud Run front-end 429 or 5xx without the renderer's JSON `detail` after 1, 3, 8 and 15 s within 30 s (spike S saw a 500, then a 429, and a 200 about 17 s after the first request to a service scaled to zero), never the renderer's own refusals. A `launcher` reason becomes `RendererUnavailable`, because it is the renderer's failure and not the code's, so it spends no repair and the theme toggle records no try; every other failure maps to `RendererUnavailable` too, and its message is logged.

A run renders one theme, the one `PipelineArgs.theme` names, and the host gates judge exactly the themes of the job (an output for another theme is ignored, a missing one fails R1). The theme toggle renders the other theme of a finished version later, as a job of its own with the same code and `data.csv`, and adds its PNG to the version's render. Renders are strictly serial per instance, because spikes S and S2 showed one 4 GiB instance serves one sandbox at a time safely. No backend keeps a semaphore of its own: `Services.backend` wraps whichever backend `make_backend` built in `SerialRenderer` (`render/serial.py`), which renders one theme per slot under `AGENT_RENDER_CONCURRENCY` slots, 1 by default, so every backend is serial by construction, `adk web` runs included; the `remote` backend therefore sends one theme per request. The renderer holds one render slot of its own on top, because the deployed agents service and a developer's `adk web` call it independently. A freed slot goes to a waiting pipeline render before a waiting theme toggle, so a run waits for at most the render in progress; a render's timeout starts when it runs. The theme toggle records a theme that failed the host gates and renders it at most twice; a renderer that could not run is not recorded. `harness.py` (copied into the renderer image at `/opt/anyplot/harness.py`) prints its `HARNESS` start line, sets the resource limits soft and hard alike (CPU seconds and file size always; the address space and the process count when the renderer passes them, because outside a sandbox `RLIMIT_NPROC` counts every process of the user), copies the baked font cache into `MPLCONFIGDIR`, appends the installed fallback families (`FALLBACK_FAMILIES` in `harness.py`: Noto Sans, Noto Sans CJK JP, and the Noto Sans faces for Arabic, Hebrew, the nine Indic scripts, Thai, Khmer, Myanmar, Ethiopic and two symbol blocks) to every font lookup after the font the code chose, so DejaVu Sans stays the catalogue's first font and text in any of those scripts still draws, whatever `font.family` the code or seaborn's `set_theme` set; records every code point matplotlib reports as having no glyph in any font of its text, plain text and `$…$` math alike, as `missing_glyphs` in the probe; registers a `Figure.savefig` probe that writes `probe-<theme>.json` (plotnine saves a figure built without a canvas, so since 2026-10-25 the probe gives it the Agg canvas a PNG save would; altair, pygal and lets-plot draw no matplotlib figure and get a probe of their drawn texts alone, `install_text_probes`), compiles the plot file and runs it as `__main__` under the plot guard, and prints its peak memory and CPU time in a closing `HARNESS` line.

Host gates: R1 (exit code and outputs present) and R2 (PNG hardening: `lstat` with no symlinks, at most 10 MB, magic bytes, Pillow under `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS`, non-blank with less than 99.7 % of the canvas in one colour, re-encode) are blocking; a render that fails either is discarded and never becomes `best`. R3 (canvas within 16 px through `core/canvas.py`, lifted from `.github/workflows/impl-review.yml`) is repair-triggering with a fallback: an R3 miss after normalisation is a defect line for the repair; if it persists after the last allowed attempt, the host pads the PNG of the rendered theme to the target canvas (never crops), the padded artifact counts as having passed the host gates, and the result ships as `needs_attention` with the residual line `canvas padded after render (<theme>)` and the VQ-05 line for that theme, never silently. A job with both themes pads each theme that missed and keeps the other theme's PNG as it rendered. The theme toggle answers a padded theme as `needs_attention` with the reason `canvas_padded`. The probe gates (G3 clipping, G5 annotation out of view, G7 overlapping text: tick labels among themselves, and since 2026-10-12 every other drawn text (axis label, title, legend text, annotation, or a text no role names) against any drawn text by more than 3 px, named by role and never by content, with a polar hint when an axis label of polar axes is involved, after the owner's polar case where the radial label `y` sat on the `270°` tick label and the reviewer called it missing; G8 row fidelity (more point marks than 1.5 times the rows plus 10), skipped for a plot type that draws one mark per unit of a count, because its mark count follows the values and not the rows (`data/signs.py` `UNIT_MARK_SPECS`, from the specs' own wording: `pictogram-basic`, `waffle-basic`, `dot-matrix-proportional`, `parliament-basic` and every variant of those prefixes; a feedback case of a seaborn pictogram drew 77 icons for 11 rows by design and got a repair to "draw marks only from df"), G9 more than 500,000 plotted points, which still holds for those, G10 characters without a glyph, and since 2026-10-15 G11 per-mark value labels and decorations past what the marks leave room for, and G12 a decimal mark that is not the plot language's) are advisory because in-sandbox data can be tampered with: they can only trigger a repair or add notes, never fail a render. Since 2026-10-12 the probe also describes each polar axes (`polar`: the radial labels' angle, the visible theta span and a histogram of the plotted points by 5° of angle and by tenth of the radius range, all in the axes' own theta coordinates), and `agents/anyplot/render/polar.py` turns it into a measured target, not a gate: when the radial tick labels' ray holds plotted points and a ray every 22.5° inside the span holds at most half as many, `GateReport.repair_hints` gets one line such as "polar radial tick labels: at 67.5° they sit on 10 plotted points → ax.set_rlabel_position(270), the freest direction, 0 points there. Likely cause: ...". It never starts a repair; a repair attempt that runs for other findings carries the latest render's line first among its `hints`, and the adapter prompt says to apply it with exactly that value. The owner's case of that day (seaborn `polar-scatter`, `y = sin(x)`) had the repair guess 67.5°, onto the data; evenly spread angles give no line, but a cardioid does, because a point counts only when its distance from the ray as a share of the radius is small and it is outside the innermost tenth. The first attempt gets no such hint: nothing is rendered yet, and the angle of the user's column as the plot will draw it (degrees or radians, offset, direction) is only known from the render. A renderer limit that stopped a run (`memory`, `disk_budget`, `file_budget`, `output_rejected`) is recorded as `R1-<reason>` among the failed gates. The defect-line helpers (`DEFECT_RE`, `weakness_class`, `defect_ids`, and the `format_defect` builder) live in `core/defects.py`, the canonical stdlib-only implementation, and the PNG auto-reject checks (AR-04 blank and AR-07 format; R2 measures with their `dominant_color` and its own 99.7 % threshold, 98 % until 2026-10-26: sparse pygal and lets-plot charts reach 98 % to 99.44 %, a bare canvas or a title alone 99.78 % and more) live next to the canvas gate in `core/canvas.py`, so the agents image needs no `automation/` or `scripts/` files. `automation/scripts/regen_gate.py` keeps a parity-tested copy of the grammar, because the workflows run it as a single-file copy with the runner's system Python, where `core` is not importable. The pipeline switches to `core.defects` in the later change that also switches the canvas gate and updates the copy sites: the `cp` step in `impl-review.yml`, the `git show` in `impl-generate.yml`, `review_retest.py materialize`, and the overlay in `review-retest.yml`.

**Scripts and fonts.** Plot text can be in the scripts most users' data and languages bring: the renderer image installs Noto Sans, Noto Sans CJK and the Noto Sans faces of the scripts DejaVu Sans lacks (37 files, 46.8 MB, Regular and Bold where the family has both), and the harness appends them to every font lookup, so a Chinese title or a Devanagari tick label draws with the right glyphs while the code's own first font stays in charge of Latin text. Right-to-left scripts work: the pinned matplotlib lays text out through libraqm, so Arabic and Hebrew render shaped and in reading order, within one label aligned by the code's `ha`. Three limits remain, and G10 reports the first and the last: a `$…$` math string uses matplotlib's math fonts without fallback and draws `¤` for every character they lack, so non-Latin words belong outside math text; Han characters take the Japanese regional forms of the CJK face until a caller passes the plot's language (`text.language`, a follow-up with the language chip); and a character outside the installed families draws as a Last Resort placeholder box. Most pictographs are not in that last group, because Noto Sans Symbols2 or DejaVu Sans draws them as monochrome outlines (📈, 📊, ⚠, 😀), but 🔥, ✅, the flag letters, Tibetan and Mongolian have no glyph in any installed font. G10 counts those characters deterministically from matplotlib's own reports, names up to three of them with their code points (`U+1F525 🔥 fire`), tells the repair that a user's column name stays as it is, and keeps the line in the result's residual lines even when the reviewer passed the render, because a measured missing glyph is not a judgement.

**Label density and number formats.** Two findings of the live tests of 2026-10-14/15 became measured gates. The catalogue draws some elements once per mark of its few example marks: bar-grouped puts a value label, a dot and a drop shadow on each of its 12 bars, and the adapter kept them on the 52 bars of 26 regions times two series, because "keep the same style" and the reviewer's house-style exemption covered them. The harness's `density_report` (probe field `density`, per axes) counts the bars and points, the texts at a data coordinate whose anchor sits at a bar's end, inside a bar's segment (`bar_label(label_type="center")`) or on a point (`ax.annotate`, `ax.bar_label`, `ax.text`; heatmap cell text and callouts in axes coordinates are not value labels, and neither is a text matplotlib clips away in a zoomed view), which of them are numbers, the dots on bar ends (scatter marks or `plot(..., "o")` markers) and the shadowed bars, and measures the median bar label width against the space between neighbouring labelled bars (unlabelled bars between them, such as an offset shadow series, do not shrink it). Bar ends come from the bars' data coordinates, so an inverted axis works. G11 writes a VQ-03 line (owner, 2026-10-15: the thresholds stay) when more than `MAX_VALUE_LABELS` (25) bar positions carry a label (a stacked bar's segment labels count once), when more than 25 numeric labels sit on points (named points, the point of scatter-annotated, never count), from six labels on when a bar label is wider than 90 % of the space per bar, or when more than `MAX_DECORATIONS` (25) dots or shadows are drawn. The adapter prompt and the reviewer's and scorer's house-style exemption now say that an element whose count grows with the data is not house style. The rule itself has one source shared with the catalogue pipeline: the "Per-mark Labels and Decorations" section of `prompts/default-style-guide.md` and the VQ-03 paragraph of `prompts/quality-criteria.md`, which the adapter reads verbatim; `adapter.md` only points at it. Second, number formats followed English whatever the plot language ("6.0 M" and "5.30" in German plots), and `locale` is a banned import. `language.NUMBER_FORMATS` is the server's table of the decimal mark, the thousands mark and the words for large numbers per language of `ENGLISH_NAMES` (`de`: `,`, `.`, Tsd./Mio./Mrd.); the adapter's static instruction carries it with the rule to format through a `FuncFormatter` or an f-string whose marks are swapped through a placeholder (`.replace(",", "_").replace(".", DECIMAL).replace("_", THOUSANDS)`), exact for the space-grouped languages too. A region such as `de-CH` with its apostrophe (7'184'003) is a follow-up of its own setting (owner, 2026-10-15). G12 reads the probe's `labels` field (the drawn texts by kind, from block F) and its `tick_axes` field (`harness.tick_axes`: whether each axis's tick labels are numbers, dates, categories or labels the code set itself), and writes a VQ-06 line when an annotation, or a tick label of a numeric axis, uses the other decimal mark, mathtext read as drawn: a single point followed by other than three digits in a decimal-comma language (`5.30`, `6.0 M`, `5.30 €`), a point after a whole part of 0 (`0.125`), a point after a comma, or commas grouping thousands; dates (a zero-padded `14.10` in an annotation reads as a day and month), years, version strings, titles, legend entries and tick labels the code set itself never count, and no label text reaches the line.

Later languages reuse the CI commands: R's binary on `plot.R` (since 2026-10-29 the row runs `/opt/R/4.6.1/lib/R/bin/exec/R --no-echo --no-restore --no-save --file=plot.R` with the environment R's `bin/R` script would set and `LANG=C.UTF-8`, not `Rscript`, whose wrapper starts would die under the exec filter; the harness preloads the filter into the child, `agents/renderer/execdeny/execdeny.c`, and refuses the run, exit 3, unless the library reported `on`; the agents service sends R jobs since 2026-10-29, through `RRuntime`); Julia's launcher on `plot.jl` (since 2026-10-29 the row runs `/opt/julia-1.13.2/bin/julia --project=/opt/julia-project --startup-file=no --history-file=no --compiled-modules=strict --heap-size-hint=1536M plot.jl` under the preloaded exec filter, with a stacked `JULIA_DEPOT_PATH=/tmp/depot:/opt/julia-depot`, the `JULIA_CPU_TARGET` the toolchain image precompiled the catalogue's `Project.toml` and `Manifest.toml` for, one thread and the offline switches; under `strict` a package image that does not load fails the render in about a second instead of compiling under its timeout; since J1.1 the row adds `-L /opt/anyplot/toolchain/warmup.jl` before `plot.jl`, which loads the toolchain's precompile workload, a package whose image holds the native code of the calls a plot makes most and of the data loader's read, compiled with CSV, DataFrames and TimeZones loaded, and cut an adapted render from 15.9 to 5.0 s locally; the agents service sends Makie jobs since 2026-10-30, through `JuliaRuntime`, and the row keeps Julia's first `ERROR:` line in front of the stderr tail); Node by its path on the catalogue's own harness (since 2026-10-30 the row runs `/opt/node/bin/node /opt/anyplot/js-render/render.mjs --library=<library> plot.js`, `plot.tsx` for MUI X, without the exec filter, because Node starts esbuild and Chromium and Chromium starts its own processes, while the plot code runs in the page, which has no process API; the toolchain image carries Node 22, the pipeline's major, the repository's `package-lock.json` installed with `npm ci` under `/opt/anyplot/node_modules`, Playwright's Chromium headless shell alone under `/opt/pw-browsers`, and Liberation Sans, Serif and Mono for this row alone through its own `FONTCONFIG_FILE`, so the catalogue's page font stack resolves as on the pipeline's runner while the other rows keep their fonts; the agents service sends Chart.js, D3, ECharts and Highcharts jobs since 2026-10-30, through `JavaScriptRuntime`, each with its table as `data_json`; since 2026-10-30 the row's environment switches on the harness's sandbox mode, `ANYPLOT_SANDBOX=1` with `ANYPLOT_NO_HTML=1`, which the catalogue pipeline never sets and without which the harness renders as before: every request of the page aborted by a route and refused by a content security policy (`default-src 'none'`, inline scripts and styles, `data:` and `blob:` images, `data:` fonts, no form submission, no `<base>`), every WebSocket closed, `window.open` returning null, WebRTC connecting nowhere, the zone UTC, the page's V8 heap capped at 1 GiB, the console kept to the first five errors, MUI X's imports closed to the image's allowlist of bare specifiers, and one final stderr line `render: error: <kind> <Class> <file>:<line>: <message>` that the renderer's 2,000-character tail always keeps; a job may carry the table as typed JSON, `data_json`, which the renderer writes as `data.json` and the harness injects as `window.ANYPLOT_DATA`, one object per row in column order); bokeh and plotly render through the same Chromium in two steps, because a plot-mode harness cannot start a program once its guard is armed (the plot guard refuses a browser started from plot code; `agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md`): since 2026-10-31 (PB-1; the agents service sends plotly and bokeh jobs since PB-2a, the same day, through `PythonRuntime`) their rows run `harness.py --figure <library> plot.py -- /opt/node/bin/node /opt/anyplot/js-render/render.mjs --library=<library>`, in which the harness forks the plot step before it arms anything, the plot writes its figure as JSON (`fig.write_json`, `bokeh.embed.json_item`) under the plot guard, the exec filter and the Python limits, and the harness, never armed, then runs the JavaScript row's harness on it with that row's environment and limits, which draws it as data with the installed package's own bundled JavaScript in a sandbox-mode page whose policy admits scripts by a nonce alone, at the figure's own CSS size and the device scale that makes the canvas 3200x1800 or 2400x2400; the renderer image installs plotly and bokeh without kaleido or Selenium. Since 2026-10-24 the renderer holds these commands in one table (`agents/renderer/commands.py`, keyed by the job's `(language, library)`, the library checked against the catalogue's fifteen) and never takes a command from a request; a non-Python row runs through the harness's exec mode (`harness.py --exec -- <command> plot.<ext>`: the start line, the limits set and inherited, the end line from the child's rusage), so the launcher retry, the watchdog and the kill path hold for every language; a row says which limits differ (`RLIMIT_AS` off for the JavaScript and Julia rows, whose V8, Chromium and LLVM reserve address space far beyond their resident memory, and `RLIMIT_NPROC` off for the JavaScript row alone, whose one Chromium render runs 77 tasks; since 2026-10-25 `RLIMIT_AS` is off for altair's Python row too, because vl-convert's embedded V8 aborts at start under any limit up to 64 GiB while its resident set is about 270 MiB), which limit it sets in place of the configured one (since 2026-10-26 lets-plot's Python row has its own 2048 MiB, `RuntimeCommand.rlimit_as_mb`, because its Kotlin/Native rasteriser aborts every 3200x1800 export under the configured 1 GiB while 2048 MiB still stops a runaway scale, which a 6400x3600 export shows at 2580 MiB; the renderer's switch still rules, so a configured limit of 0 leaves every row unlimited), which environment it adds (lets-plot's: Arrow's system allocator, `ARROW_DEFAULT_MEMORY_POOL=system`, because pyarrow's default pool reserves a 1 GiB arena on the first pandas string column, and `FONTCONFIG_FILE=/opt/anyplot/fonts.conf` for the fontconfig its bridge links; an in-process row's environment and limit reach the sandbox through `executor.sandbox_env` and the local Docker backend through `PythonRuntime.env`, which since 2026-10-26 is `sandbox_env` key for key, the sandbox's `PATH` and limits included, but `RLIMIT_NPROC`: the local container runs as the developer's user, whose processes on the whole machine count against it, so `--pids-limit` bounds the processes there) and what render time a repair reserves; `GET /status` reports under `languages` which toolchains the image carries, and a job whose row cannot run here, its language's toolchain or the row's own binaries missing (Node for plotly's and bokeh's screenshot step), is `503 runtime_unavailable`.

### Footer strip

Every PNG a user sees or downloads carries a strip appended below the plot (owner decision of 2026-10-10): "made with any.plot()" on the left and "anyplot.ai/<spec-id>" on the right. `agents/anyplot/render/watermark.py` composites it at image level after the run, so it never enters the user's code, and everything upstream keeps the raw render: the host gates R1 to R3, the probe gates, the reviewer, the render store's `pngs` and the exported `plot.py`. Only the artifact route's `plot-light.png` and `plot-dark.png` and the feedback bundle's images carry it. The route composes the strip in a worker thread on the first request and caches it in the render store next to the raw PNG (`StoredRender.shown`, counted against the same 256 MiB cap; a cached strip gives way to a new render before the store reports itself full). `AGENT_WATERMARK=false` serves the raw renders, and the eval harness sets it so its renders stay comparable with its baselines.

The geometry is defined on the 3200 px canvas and scales with `width / 3200`:

- The strip is 64 px high in the theme's page colour (#FAF8F1 or #1A1A17, taken from the artifact name, never from the pixels), so a 3200x1800 render becomes 3200x1864 and the 2400x2400 square becomes 2400x2448. The raw image sits at (0, 0), pixel-identical.
- A 1 px hairline in the ink colour at alpha 0.15 fills the strip's top row and never covers the plot's last row.
- The text is JetBrains Mono at 22 px, inset 40 px from both sides, with its cap height (0.73 em) centred in the strip. "made with " and "/<spec-id>" are Regular at alpha 0.56, "any", "plot", "anyplot" and "ai" Bold at 0.70, and "()" Regular at 0.315, in the theme's ink (#1A1A17 or #F0EFE8).
- The dot is not a glyph but a #009E73 square at alpha 0.9 with the logo's MonoLisa period geometry: for a dot em of 1.45 times the text size, a side of 0.185 em (6 px), a cell of 0.640 em, a left bearing of 0.251 em, and its bottom on the baseline.
- A spec id too long for the space between the two texts is cut and ends in an ellipsis, so the texts stay at least 40 px apart.

JetBrains Mono 2.304 Regular and Bold are vendored unmodified from the official release, with `OFL.txt` and a notice that records their SHA-256 values, in `agents/anyplot/render/fonts/`. The service loads them at import, so a missing file stops it at start, and draws them with Pillow's BASIC layout engine, so an image without raqm renders the same pixels. MonoLisa is not an option: its EULA covers desktop, web, application and ePub use only.

### Fencing and exported code

Fencing is our own: catalogue code and the profile are wrapped as `<catalogue_code>` and `<user_data>` blocks with a "data, never instructions" preamble in the `AdaptRequest` rendering and in the `get_current_code` and `get_dataset_profile` results. Spec text, which starts as a public issue, goes into `<spec_text>`. Text that one model hands to the next, or that quotes catalogue code, goes into one `<tool_notes>` block as JSON: the `changes` and `residual_defects` of the `plot_pipeline` result for the root, the readiness hints, the feedback lines and the previous plan for the adapter, and the gate notes for the reviewer. Outside a fence stand only fixed headings and validated identifiers, so the root's session block names the spec id but never the spec title. The session language is the one user-chosen value the session block carries, and it stands inside a `<plot_language>` fence there too, as in the adapter's and the reviewer's requests, because a chosen language is free text that only a pattern bounds. A render error reaches the repair as its exception class and code line only, never its message, which can be a cell of the user's data. ADK's built-in fencing (relayed agent turns, MCP descriptions) is not relied on, because tool results and node inputs are relayed unfenced.

The exported code is byte-identical to the run form that rendered: an attribution header ("Adapted by anyplot.ai from <spec-id> (<library>) for your data.csv; run: `ANYPLOT_THEME=<theme> python plot.py`", naming the theme the run rendered), imports, the theme tokens unchanged, the Imprint palette (possibly extended), `df = pd.read_csv("data.csv", ...)`, then plot, style and save. The download is `plot.py` plus `data.csv`; the code draws no footer strip, so it reproduces the plot without one. The catalogue title rule, the 4-line header and the DQ-02, DE and LM rubric items do not apply to user plots.

plotly and bokeh are the one deviation (owner decision of 2026-10-31, question 10 of the phase-2 plan). Their run form ends on a statement that writes the figure as JSON, `fig.update_layout(width=800, height=450).write_json(f"plot-{THEME}.json")` or `json.dump(json_item(p), ...)` in a `with open(f"plot-{THEME}.json", ...)` block, which the renderer's figure mode draws in Chromium, because plot code in the sandbox may not start a browser (`agents/anyplot/code/figure.py`). The export swaps that one statement back for the library's normal save, as the catalogue writes it, so the downloaded `plot.py` runs unchanged on the user's machine with `data.csv`: `fig.write_image(f"plot-{THEME}.png", width=800, height=450, scale=4)` and `fig.write_html(f"plot-{THEME}.html", include_plotlyjs="cdn")` for plotly (kaleido writes the PNG, at the scale that gives the chat's canvas), `export_png(p, filename=f"plot-{THEME}.png")`, `output_file(f"plot-{THEME}.html")` and `save(p)` for bokeh (Selenium and a browser driver write the PNG), with bokeh's imports swapped to match. Everything else is the run form, byte for byte.

## Guardrails

Plugin order on `App`, where the first non-None result wins and a plugin never raises (on internal failure it returns a blocking response): `ScopeGuard → Budget → ToolSafety → EmptyReply → ShippedReply → ReplyLanguage → ContextFilter(num_invocations_to_keep=6)`. EmptyReply (since 2026-11-06, `plugins/empty_reply.py`) reads the root's final response that carries nothing for the user (no tool call and no text outside thoughts: no content, an empty or blank part, a thought only), which the stream translator would drop, so that the turn sent `done` alone (a persona test's paste with a note got no assistant turn): it asks the root once more, with the same request and a line saying the last answer came back empty, with its tools only while the turn has run none (after a tool, a second call of one that stores something would apply it twice), and puts the answer in the empty response's place in place, so ShippedReply and ReplyLanguage after it check the answer, and an answer that calls a tool goes on in ADK's flow; the call is booked like any other. When the answer is empty too, or no ask is possible (no kept request, no budget, a failed call, the turn's one ask spent), the turn fails with `no_reply` (`error{code:"no_reply"}`), and a paste the turn was to summarize stays unsummarized, so the next turn summarizes it; a turn that already carries its answer keeps its empty closing reply without an error: a shipped plot, a library switch the page carries out, or a change a tool stored (data, bindings, a language, a kept plot request, a forgotten memory entry), which `done` brings to the page and which sending the message again would apply twice. Since 2026-11-08 the stored change is read from the record of the turn's calls (`RequestLedger.tool_calls`, from each tool's own answer), so a change still counts when ToolSafety then refused its result for its keys or its size; a `plot_pipeline` call that kept the first plot's request counts as none, so a summary turn whose plot ToolSafety refused still owes its reply. It logs `empty_reply` with the verdict `reasked`, `no_reply` or `empty_kept` and the reason. A reply that only the output sanitiser empties (a bare link, an HTML block) fails the turn with `no_reply` in the stream translator the same way, with the same exceptions. ShippedReply (`plugins/shipped_reply.py`) checks the root's final text reply in a turn whose plot shipped (`ok`, `needs_attention`; `RequestLedger.shipped_in_turn`, which stays set after the Budget plugin spends `plot_shipped` on that reply's call): a shipped plot was drawn from confirmed data, so every sentence that names `.confirm()`, step 1's old button, which has that name in every language, is dropped (the page lost the button on 2026-11-04, when `.create_plot()` took the confirmation into its own click, and the prompt no longer names it; the check stays as the backstop and does not move to `.create_plot()`, which is the right next step after a turn that shipped a plot and then changed the bindings or the data), and the rest of the reply stays word for word. A feedback case had the root ask for `.confirm()` after an `ok` plot although the result said `data_confirmed: true` and `root.md` forbids it. Dropping costs no model call and rewords nothing, so a notice the reply must carry is never lost, which a re-ask cannot promise; a reply in which every sentence names the button stays whole, and a sentence that says the data is unconfirmed without naming the button stays too. It edits the reply in place and returns None, so ReplyLanguage still checks the edited reply; a reply ReplyLanguage asks for again comes from a direct model call that no plugin sees, so it gets the same check (`scrub_shipped_reply`) before it replaces the first. It logs `shipped_reply` with the verdict `dropped` or `kept` and the sentence count. Since 2026-11-07 the same plugin also drops, in every turn and not only after a shipped plot, each sentence that names the composer's plot button (`.create_plot()`, `.update_plot()`, `.plot_all()`, the same in every language): the chat's status line names that button and what it waits for, and `root.md` says a reply never tells the user to press it, a rule the prompt itself had broken in four places. It logs `plot_button` with the same verdicts; a reply in which every sentence names the button stays whole. ReplyLanguage (since 2026-10-14, `plugins/reply_language.py`) checks the root's final text reply against the session language, since 2026-11-08 the default English included: an offline function-word and script classifier (`language_detect.py`, about 50 µs a reply, no package and no model call; the scope judge's `lang` names the user's message, never the reply) names the reply's language only when it is sure, and on a sure mismatch the plugin logs `reply_language` and asks the root once more, without tools, with the wrong reply as the previous turn and a line naming the language; the new reply replaces the old one only when it is in the session language, the extra call is booked like any other, and a fixed refusal is never checked. Re-ask once rather than only log is the owner's decision of 2026-10-14 (`REASK = True`); a re-ask that raises logs the verdict `error`, and one cut at the output cap (`MAX_TOKENS`) or not surely in the session language keeps the first reply. The detector ignores code fences (an unclosed one to the end), backticks, double and single quotes, and names Vietnamese only with its function words besides its letters. `GlobalInstructionPlugin` is not needed because the policy lives in the root's constant `static_instruction` and only the root talks to the user. A request-scoped ledger (a context variable keyed by invocation id) is shared by ScopeGuard, Budget, ToolSafety and the pipeline.

- **ScopeGuard.** The BFF and anyplot-agents reject text over 2,000 characters (`413 too_long`), so the judge always sees the whole message. In `on_user_message` it skips structured actions, blocks non-text parts, checks the per-user and global budget in the ledger before calling the judge and books the judge's `usage_metadata` to it, and judges all text parts plus the last assistant turn (at most 500 characters; the last reply a model wrote, never a fixed refusal or the outage line, since 2026-10-14: in a live session a German weather question was rightly refused `needs_data`, and with that refusal as its context the judge refused the next message, "Welcher Tag im April war der wärmste, und kannst du ihn im Plot markieren?", a question about the user's own data, as `needs_data` too; the withheld placeholder among the user turns still marks a refused turn and names its verdict, `[message withheld by scope policy: needs_data]` or `…: out_of_scope`, so "do it anyway" after a `needs_data` refusal gets `needs_data` again) and the user's last three earlier turns (at most 1,500 characters), with delimiters escaped (spaced and fullwidth tag forms too), so a request split across three turns is judged as a whole; the root sees six invocations, so a request spread wider than the judge's window is bounded by the plot-text budget below instead. The verdicts are `in_scope`, `out_of_scope`, `attack`, `needs_data` (a request for data the service would have to fetch, such as stock prices, the weather, statistics, a URL or a public dataset; since 2026-10-14 also data code that reads its table from a URL or a file, such as `pd.read_csv(urlopen(url))`, which a live test had classed as `attack` and so a strike; `attack` stays for code that reaches the system, its environment, secrets or the metadata server, or sends data out) and, since 2026-10-14, `other_library` (this plot in another library or language, such as "this plot in Chart.js, give me the code", which a live test had refused as the third `out_of_scope` and so closed the session unwarned: the fixed `other_library` answer points to the plot's catalogue page, which has it in up to 15 libraries, and by default it never counts toward closing the session; since 2026-10-29 the rubric gets the libraries the assistant adapts from `AGENT_LIBRARIES` and the catalogue's others, grouped by language (`policy.scope_rubric`), so this plot in any served library, or a question about its code in any served language, is `in_scope`, and only an unserved library or language is `other_library`; since 2026-11-08 a code rule lifts an `other_library` to `in_scope` for a message that names only served libraries, in any common form, because in a live session that day the judge refused "erstell das bitte mal in d3" and "d3.js" and let only "D3.js", the display name its rubric lists, switch the plot: `scope_guard.served_library_request` reads the message with one names table, `core/library_names.py`, which ignores case, Unicode width, `.js` and `.jl` and the separators between a name's parts, and knows the short forms such as `ggplot`, `mpl`, `mui x` and `CairoMakie`; a message that also names an unserved catalogue library or a tool outside the catalogue, such as Vega-Lite, Excel or Plots.jl, keeps the judge's verdict, no other verdict is lifted, the `served_library_in_scope` attribution line follows the judge's, the dataset route's notes screen applies the same rule, and `switch_plot` reads its library argument through the same table, so `D3.js` or `chart js` is the id `d3` or `chartjs`). The rubric counts a message that also asks for anything out of scope, text meant for use outside the plot (emails, posts, newsletters, summaries, translations) and plot text whose purpose is an advertisement, a call to action or a message to other people as out of scope, judged by intent: a name, a postal address, an e-mail address, a phone number or a web address in the data, the plot or the message is never on its own a reason to refuse, because users plot their own tax returns, contact lists and customer tables; a message whose only part beyond the plot is data to fetch, a plain fact question included, is `needs_data`, while a question about the user's own data ("which day was the warmest?", "mark the highest value") is in scope. A timeout, parse error or non-200 after one retry within 4 s blocks with a distinct `error{code:"guard_unavailable"}` that is excluded from the false-refusal metric; the retry waits 0.5 s first (`JUDGE_RETRY_BACKOFF_S`), because a 429 or a transport error retried in the same instant meets the same cause, and on a per-minute quota every failed call counts too. Any other verdict than `in_scope` replaces the message with `[message withheld by scope policy: <code>]` (the refusal code, `attack` as `out_of_scope`; the bare `[message withheld by scope policy]` for a refusal before the judge) before it is stored, and `before_run` halts with the fixed refusal from `refusals.yaml` in the session language, since 2026-11-08 whatever language the judge names for the message (hand-written English, German, French, Spanish, Italian, Portuguese, Dutch, Polish, Chinese, Vietnamese and Russian texts in the informal address; the Chinese, Vietnamese and Russian texts and the revised Portuguese and Polish ones, added on 2026-10-12 without a native speaker at hand, passed a back-translation review and a separate naturalness review; English for every other language; no model writes or translates a refusal): `needs_data` with the `needs_data` text (no internet source can be tapped; paste the data as a table), `other_library` with the `other_library` text (a pointer to the plot's catalogue page), `out_of_scope` and `attack` with the `out_of_scope` text. The judge's `lang` for an in-scope typed message in another language than the session's makes the page offer that language once (`anyplot.language_offered`), unless the user has picked one; it never sets the session language, and every refusal, the judge's and the ones sent before it (`budget`, `unsupported_content`), uses the session language (see [Session language](#session-language)). Each `attack` verdict of the scope or dataset judge is a strike, counted once per distinct message or dataset, so retrying one input that the judge wrongly called an attack costs one strike; from `AGENT_ATTACK_STRIKES` (3) strikes a day on, the user gets the `locked_out` refusal (the `budget` refusal before 2026-10-14) for the rest of the UTC day, and a paste `429 locked_out`, and only a new day, a redeploy or a scale-to-zero resets it. Every token-path caller of the BFF shares one user id, so in phase 1 the owner's own jailbreak testing on that path spends the shared strikes: raise `AGENT_ATTACK_STRIKES` for such a session, or use a separate user id. With `AGENT_KEEP_REFUSALS` (off by default) a refused message's text and verdict stay in a ring of 20 per session in memory, shown only in the feedback bundle; this is a phase-1 stub, readable while the session lives through the bundle the page's `.save()` downloads (`GET /debug/agent/sessions/{sid}/bundle` on the BFF, since 2026-10-12), until the refusals are stored with the quick feedback. The dataset judge call at parse time uses the same plumbing with a data rubric that judges injection only, never personal data, behind a deterministic pre-filter for text addressed to an AI. In `on_event` the ScopeGuard also checks the root's final reply before ADK stores it, with the output guard's reply cap below, and stores a refused reply as the fixed refusal, so it never re-enters the judge's or the root's context; after a `plot_pipeline` call it stores the reply cut at the cap instead.
- **Budget.** `before_model` halts with a fixed `LlmResponse` only for the root, because a halt inside the adapter or the reviewer would break their schema parsing; the pipeline calls `budget.check()` before each `run_node` and finishes with `PlotResult(failed, reason=budget)`. `before_tool` on `plot_pipeline` counts pipeline runs, but not a call that ToolSafety refuses: invalid arguments, or a call the turn's bounds stop (`tool_safety.pipeline_bound`, which both plugins read: a second call, a switch asked for in the turn, a call after the turn's own data change or on the summary turn of an unconfirmed paste). A run that answers `not_ready` itself counts, as before. Limits: per request 12 calls or 160k tokens; per user per day 40 pipeline runs or 2M tokens (about 54 warm runs, so the run cap binds first, but about 26 for a user whose every request starts cold); global per day 6M tokens, three users at their daily budget, which pauses the service. The token limits count cost-weighted tokens: each kind weighs its list price relative to an uncached input token (cache read 0.1, cache write 1.25, output 5; `BUDGET_WEIGHTS` in `plugins/ledger.py`), and the judges' tokens count as well. With a warm prompt cache the median run of the spike-X rerun booked about 37k weighted against 70k plain tokens. On a cold cache a reviewed run with two attempts books about 78k, and the costliest one about 115k with a second review (117k with three attempts), which the 160k request limit holds with about 36 % to spare. Before a review the pipeline also keeps 35k (`REVIEW_RESERVE_TOKENS`) for the review and the root's closing reply, so a tight budget skips a review rather than answering a shipped plot with the `budget` refusal; the reserve is the measured need, and the guarantee is structural: once a plot has shipped in the invocation, the root's next call is exempt from the halt once and runs without tools, so it can only be the closing reply (bounded by the root's 4,096-token output cap); any further root call is checked again. Phase-1 daily counters live in memory for one instance lifetime, and the `aiplatform` spend cap is the real backstop; persisting the counters is a phase-2 item. The plugin writes one attribution JSON line per hook (request id, HMAC user, session hash, agent, tool, argument hash, verdicts, usage, `model_version`, latency) and never content.
- **ToolSafety.** `before_tool` enforces a per-agent allowlist (the root's twelve session tools and its `plot_pipeline` workflow; none for the adapters and the reviewer; on a plot button's turn only the reads and `plot_pipeline`, every other tool answering `plot_as_confirmed`, see [Two steps](#two-steps-data-first-then-the-plot)), Pydantic validation of the arguments (`FUNCTION_TOOL_ARG_VALIDATION` is off by default), no links (`http`, `https`, `ftp`, `file`, `data` or `javascript` with text right after the colon, or `www.`; a caption such as "Data: my tax return" is not one) or paths in string arguments, as link hygiene (`run_data_code`'s `code` is exempt, because a division reads as a path and the DATA validator bans URL literals itself), and then the turn's caps (one `plot_pipeline`, two `prepare_data`, two `generate_data`, three `query_data` and two `run_data_code` calls, counted from the turn's allowed calls, each refusal with a fixed message) and the pipeline's bounds (`pipeline_bound`: a switch asked for in the turn, the turn's own data change, and the summary turn of a paste while the data is unconfirmed, the last two refused before the run with the run's `not_ready` records; see [Two steps](#two-steps-data-first-then-the-plot)). Personal data in a `change_request` (a name, an address, an e-mail address, a phone number, a bare web address such as `jana-novak.de`, which the root writes without its scheme and `www.`) passes: whether plot text is an advertisement or a message to other people is the scope judge's call, by intent. In the pipeline, a change of a previous version may add at most 300 characters of new string literals (bytes literals and f-string parts included; 2,000 for the first adaptation and for a change that switches the session language, and 2,000 for all versions together against the catalogue code; dataset headers and cells are free): the budget bounds how much text the model writes, not what it says. `after_tool` applies a key allowlist and an 8 KB cap (24 KB for code). `on_tool_error` returns `{"status":"error","code":ENUM}` and never exception text.
- **Output sanitiser** in the stream translator, deterministic: `message` events only for `author == "anyplot"` final responses (adapter, reviewer and pipeline-branch events map only to `status` and `plot`); strip URLs with a scheme or `www.`, `data:`, `javascript:` and `mailto:` links, HTML, markdown images and fenced code blocks longer than 10 lines; keep `[[spec:id]]` only for registry ids; cap text at 1,200 characters; final text only. Personal data stays: names, postal and e-mail addresses, phone numbers and bare web addresses pass through to the chat, and only a link with a scheme, `www.` or `mailto:` is stripped, as link hygiene. The output guard (`agents/anyplot/output_guard.py`, run by the ScopeGuard before the reply is stored) never refuses a root reply longer than 1,200 characters once sanitised, in any language and with no word list: since 2026-11-06 it asks the root once for a shorter reply, the way ReplyLanguage asks again (the root's own request without tools, the long reply as its previous turn, and a line asking for at most 800 characters with the same language, answer and notices), and stores and sends that answer when it fits; otherwise the shorter of the two goes out cut at 1,200 characters. Attribution verdicts `reply_too_long`, then `shortened` or `reply_cut` with the reason. Until 2026-11-06 such a reply became the fixed `out_of_scope` refusal, unless the turn called `plot_pipeline`, and the persona tests of that day got the refusal for an on-topic summary of a pasted bank export and an on-topic answer about the user's data. The root's "two to five sentences, or a short list", which sized the cap, is about 300 to 900 characters across languages, and a list of eight steps fits in about 600, so the cap leaves a third on top; since 2026-11-05 the root asks for one or two short sentences (four at most with its notices), well within it. It counts characters, not sentences, because a sentence count needs a segmenter per script and counts every list item. The cap bounds how much one reply can carry; an off-topic reply that got past the judge is cut, not caught, by it.

| Threat | Controls |
|---|---|
| Off-topic use, jailbreak, multi-turn drift | A judge on every free-text turn that fails closed and sees the user's last three turns, the 2,000-character limit, rejected text never stored (unless `AGENT_KEEP_REFUSALS` keeps it for the bundle), attack strikes, a closed tool set, policy in an untemplated `static_instruction`, the root "never" list, the output guard's reply cap on the root's replies in every language, and the judge-only scope eval (first run on 2026-10-10: every adversarial case refused, no false refusal) including general-programming requests framed as plot-code questions, mixed and plot-framed requests, and multi-turn drift |
| Requests for data the service would have to fetch | The `needs_data` verdict ends the run at the first turn with a fixed reply (no internet source can be tapped; paste the data as a table), the root's backup rule with the same reply, and no network access anywhere in the service |
| Spam or scam text in the plot or the reply | The scope rubric's rules on plot text (an advertisement, a call to action or a message to other people is out of scope, judged by intent) and on text for use outside the plot, with the root's prompt and the adapter's prompt saying the same; a 300-character new-literal budget on a change and 2,000 over all versions; the output guard's reply cap; the footer strip on every served PNG. Personal data is allowed in plots and in the chat: a name, an address, an e-mail address, a phone number or a web address is never on its own a reason to refuse. Links are the one limit, as link hygiene in what the model writes: a bare web address (`jana-novak.de`) passes everywhere, a link written with a scheme or `www.` is stripped from replies and plot-event lines (`mailto:` links too) and refused in a tool argument, and a dataset cell with a link plots as it is. Open: whatever reaches the PNG through dataset cells (data is data), and a string the code builds at run time (concatenation, `%` formatting, numbers) escapes the literal budget |
| Injection through pasted data | The raw dataset never reaches a prompt or a tool argument; only bounded, sanitised, fenced samples do: a deterministic pre-filter over every header and cell for text addressed to an AI (a link of any scheme, `javascript:` included, is data and passes), then the dataset judge, which judges injection only and never personal data, on every header and the root's and the adapter's 5 sample rows and top values (up to 12 per column) with cells in full (values whose 40-character display copies coincide are all judged), in parts of about 4,000 characters, at parse time; the profile's at most 5 sample rows (cells at most 40 characters) for the root and the adapter; canonical headers; bindings validated on the server; adapter output bound to a schema and linted. The one model that reads raw pasted text is the transcriber (free text of at most 4,000 characters and 200 lines that the parser cannot read as a table): the pre-filter runs on the raw text first, the model answers only under the `TranscribedTable` schema (names, rows, a 200-character note), its table is parsed like any paste and shown to the user for confirmation, and the confirmation goes through the pre-filter and the judge like every other upload |
| Injection through catalogue code, comments or image labels | Own fencing; validated adapter output; a tool-less reviewer with a fixed-id schema that can trigger at most one bounded repair |
| Code escape | The two-profile AST validator, with every submodule of the packages it admits whole reviewed or banned; loader substitution; no executors anywhere; the harness's audit hooks in plot and data mode (no `exec` of plot code, no process, socket, native library or write beyond the outputs); the sandbox (no egress, environment or metadata server, read-only root filesystem) in a renderer service of its own with a zero-role service account; resource limits; a host timeout plus a confirmed kill and `sandbox delete`; a run watchdog (bytes, files and `MemAvailable`) on an in-memory, size-limited run volume that the service requires; per-job directory wipe; the phase-0 probe suite and the sibling-directory spike |
| Resource abuse (denial of wallet) | Ledger budgets including the judge, and the global day budget (100,000,000 weighted tokens, about $10) for every tier, admins included; the run queue in front of the sandbox runs (five runs in flight, one start every 10 s, at most 60 waiting; the admin's cap of five runs only for a tier the BFF asserts behind the caller check); the per-user message limits (10 chat turns a minute, 200 a day), checked before the judge; a session closed after three scope refusals; one turn per session, at most two sandbox runs per public user across sessions (`AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS`), and one `plot_pipeline` per invocation; `max-instances=1`; a CSRF header and Origin check on the cookie-authenticated BFF; a spend cap on `aiplatform.googleapis.com` only (never on `run.googleapis.com`, which would pause `anyplot-api`); the `AGENT_ENABLED` kill switch |
| Data leakage | A server-derived HMAC user id; ownership checks on every route; no content in logs or spans (`ADK_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT_IN_SPANS=false`, `OTEL_INSTRUMENTATION_GENAI_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT` unset); in-memory stores tied to the session lifetime plus an idle sweeper and a DELETE route, so anyplot stores the personal data a user plots nowhere unless they submit quick feedback, whose case bundle always holds the conversation, the code, the images and the dataset profile with its column names, sample rows and top values, any of which can show that data, and the full data file only when its data box is ticked; the translator drops arguments, tool outputs, thoughts and `error_details`; the `eu` endpoint; PNG only in the UI, no model HTML, no auto-loaded URLs |
| The data sandbox used for something other than the plot's data (`run_data_code`) | The scope judge (a snippet whose job is to generate the plot's data is in scope; a general-purpose program is out of scope; code that touches files, the network or the system is an attack); the DATA validator profile (no plotting stack, sklearn or statsmodels, no private attribute on any receiver, a top-level `df`); a per-user daily count, the run queue's slot and a start in its rate window, and at most two calls per turn; the snippet runs in the same sandbox as a plot (no egress, read-only root, rlimits, watchdog) under the harness's audit hook |
| Native code or raw memory from a data snippet (`ctypes` through a NumPy array, `from_address`, `as_strided`) | The validator's escape names on any receiver; the harness's audit hook refuses every `ctypes.*` event after numpy and pandas are imported, and a refusal the snippet caught still fails the run |
| Rows written from memory instead of computed | The DATA profile's `literal-data` rule (more than 20 numbers or 20 text values in one container, dicts included and a repetition such as `[1, 2, 3] * 10` counted as its copies, or 40 of either in the file); the root's rule never to invent values; values the user wrote out belong in the data panel, where the dataset judge reads them |
| Injection through a data snippet's output | The tool never returns the snippet's stdout or stderr: an exception is its class and line only, every other failure a fixed line by exit code; the computed table goes through the pre-filter and the dataset judge like a paste, and a refusal is `data_refused` |
| A forged result from a data snippet | The host trusts only the exit code, `status.json` (opened by the harness before the audit hook, out of the snippet's reach, and parsed strictly) and `out.csv` within its caps; the `HARNESS` lines are advisory; writes are refused by the audit hook, so the snippet cannot write `out.csv` itself |
| Code pasted into the data field | The paste classifier answers `422 code` before any parser or the transcriber reads it, so a snippet never parses as a table and no model computes its rows; the page offers to send it to the chat |
| Session poisoning or BFF bypass | Our own FastAPI, never `get_fast_api_app`; clients never write state or events; the BFF allowlists bodies; the agents app checks the IAM-forwarded ID-token claims; `ToolCallIntegrityPlugin` once sessions persist |
| Phase 2 | Model Armor through direct sanitize calls on `modelarmor.eu.rep.googleapis.com` (once per user message and once per final text, failing closed, with `logSanitizeOperations=false`); `ModelArmorPlugin` only if a spike shows acceptable call counts, because it re-screens on every model call |

**Layers against code escape.** The harness's audit hooks (`DataGuard`, `PlotGuard` in `agents/anyplot/render/harness.py`) are defense in depth, not the boundary: a hook shares the interpreter with the code it judges, so code that changes the interpreter's shared state in place (the harness's own objects, the import machinery, the builtins) can change what the hook decides, and no audit event sees that change. Native libraries that cffi opens raise no audit event either. The boundaries are, in order:

1. The validator, which refuses the names those routes need (`sys`, `cffi`, every dunder name) before any code runs.
2. The kernel's exec filter: a seccomp filter installed on every thread (`SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_TSYNC`) and inherited by every child, which refuses every `execve` and `execveat` and which no code in the process can lift.
3. The resource limits, soft and hard alike.
4. On Cloud Run, the gVisor sandbox that `sandbox do` starts for each job (spikes S and S2): no egress (the metadata server, DNS and TCP all fail), none of the service's environment or tokens, a read-only root filesystem (the renderer passes no `--write`), no view of the sibling sandboxes, and a lifetime of one job.

The known limits of the in-process layer are listed in the PB-1 section of `agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md`.

## Feedback capture

Every agent result can be reported with one click and a short text, and the report carries the full case, so prompts, validator rules, gates and the model pin can be tuned from real sessions. It mirrors the site feedback (`api/routers/feedback.py`, `FeedbackWidget`, triage under `/debug/feedback/*`) but stores a case bundle instead of a message.

Built since 2026-10-12, the capture half. Every result card carries the control (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/ResultFeedback.tsx`): the four reactions, an optional text of at most 500 characters, `# include data.csv` (unticked, with a consent line under the form; until 2026-11-06 ticked by default on the admin lane), and `.send()`, which shows `sent · v<n>` afterwards and takes an edit that stores a new case; sending never waits for a turn and never starts one. The BFF route `POST /debug/agent/sessions/{sid}/feedback` (`api/routers/agent.py`, `api/agent_cases.py`) fetches the version's bundle from anyplot-agents and writes the case to `cases/<case_id>/` in the bucket that `AGENT_CASES_BUCKET` names (`anyplot-agent-cases` by default): the PNG of each rendered theme, `plot.py`, `data.csv` only with the box ticked, and `manifest.json` last, with every field of the planned table row below plus `case_uri` and the bundle without its PNGs and data ([Feedback case](../reference/api.md#feedback-case)). The case id is the UTC time plus eight random hex digits, so ids sort by time; no upload replaces an object. An edit sends the earlier case's id as an optional `replaces`, which the manifest keeps, so the triage can tell which case an edit replaces; without it, the newest case of a session hash and version is the one that counts. The replaced case stays in the bucket, its `data.csv` too, until the lifecycle rule deletes it. The bundle carries no `model_version`, token or cost totals yet, so those manifest fields are `null`. `agents/evals/import_case.py` turns a saved bundle (`.save()`) or a case directory copied from the bucket into an eval case in the git-ignored `agents/evals/.cases/`, which the harness reads as promoted cases (`agents/README.md`, "Import a feedback case"); its expected outcome follows the triage rule below, thumbs up `accepted`, thumbs down `rejected`, and `--expected` for bug and idea. The bundle stores no change request and no bindings per version, so the tool matches a version's request by position only when the transcript's plot turns and the versions agree in number, and refuses otherwise until you pass the request yourself. The triage half is built since 2026-10-12 as well: the `agent_feedback` table and its migration, the "Agent cases" section in `DebugPage`, and `agents/evals/sync_cases.py`, which pulls the promoted cases from the bucket. Not built yet: the delete-my-case route.

- **UI.** On the result card and in the chat page's floating-action slot: thumbs up, thumbs down, bug and idea (the existing reaction set), an optional text of at most 500 characters, and a checkbox "include my data file", off by default and behind an explicit consent line for everyone since 2026-11-06 (on by default for admins in phase 1, until the admin's chat became the visitors'). Sending is optional and never blocks the flow. One submission per result version; a later edit replaces it.
- **Route.** `POST /debug/agent/sessions/{sid}/feedback {reaction, message?, include_data, result_version}` on the BFF, behind `require_admin` in phase 1; the public phase reuses the feedback router's per-IP-hash rate limit and honeypot. The BFF calls `GET /v1/sessions/{sid}/bundle` on anyplot-agents, which assembles the case on the server so that anyplot-agents stays credential-free: the transcript of user and root messages (never tool outputs or thoughts), the catalogue snapshot id, the working and run code of every version, the adapt plans and defect lines, the plot results, the dataset profile and bindings, the PNG of every rendered theme as the user saw it (with the footer strip) with its gate outcome, and a config stamp (`AGENT_PROVIDER`, `AGENT_MODEL`, `model_version` from `usage_metadata`, the judge model, prompt file hashes, validator and normaliser version, ADK version, token and cost totals, latency per step). `data.csv` is included only when `include_data` is set. Besides the feedback route, the admin chat page's `.save()` in the header's "⋯" menu (not on the public lane since 2026-11-02) downloads that bundle through the BFF's `GET /debug/agent/sessions/{sid}/bundle` as one JSON file (`anyplot-session-<id>.json`, with `data.csv` while its `# include data.csv` box is ticked, the phase-1 default), so a session can be kept and handed on by hand.
- **Storage**, written by the API, which already has database and GCS access: a row in a new table `agent_feedback` (Alembic migration) with `id, created_at, user_hash, session_hash, spec_id, library_id, language, reaction, message, include_data, status (new|in_progress|done|wont_solve), case_uri, model, model_version, prompt_hash, pipeline_status, attempts, tokens, cost_estimate`; the bundle as `cases/<id>/{manifest.json, plot-light.png, plot-dark.png, plot.py[, data.csv]}` in a new private EU bucket `anyplot-agent-cases` (public-access prevention, uniform access, lifecycle delete after 180 days by default; never `anyplot-images`). The attribution log stays content-free; the case bundle is the only place content is stored, and only on explicit submission.
- **Triage.** A new "Agent cases" section in `DebugPage` lists cases with filters by reaction, status, spec, library and model version, opens a case (transcript, code diff against the catalogue original, the image of every rendered theme, defect lines, config stamp), updates the status, and offers **Promote to eval case**, which asks for an explicit expected outcome (`accepted` or `rejected`; the form preselects `accepted` for thumbs up and `rejected` for thumbs down, and makes the owner choose for bug and idea reactions, because those do not map to a fixture outcome) and copies the case (dataset, bindings, spec and library, the chosen outcome) to the `evals/cases/<id>/` prefix of the same private bucket. The harness syncs that prefix into a gitignored `agents/evals/.cases/` directory at run time. Promoted cases are never committed: they contain users' data, and anything under `agents/evals/fixtures/` ships under the repository's MIT licence. Only synthetic fixtures (the spike-X perturbation datasets) are committed. This is the bridge from real sessions to the regression harness.
- **Feedback without a plot.** Since 2026-10-12 a session that made no plot can be reported too: the owner's session that asked for a feature column from a sine table never reached a result card, so he had to paste its JSON by hand. While the thread has a turn, no turn runs and the session has no version, `.feedback()` at the end of the chat's status line (since 2026-11-07; `# no plot yet? tell us what went wrong` below the composer before) opens the same control (`ChatFeedback.tsx`) as `# feedback on this chat`, right above that line; it sends `result_version` 0, the chat itself. The bundle route answers `version=0` with the conversation, the profile, the bindings and no version, and with `include_data` the session's current data as the top-level `data_csv` (`null` before any data, which the BFF answers with `422 no_data`; the control then shows `# no data yet` and cannot tick the box). The case holds `data.csv` (when asked for) and `manifest.json`, no image and no `plot.py`; its row carries `result_version` 0 and no pipeline status, and the same `replaces` chain links an edit. The triage labels it `chat, no plot`, shows no renders, and refuses its promotion (`422 no_plot`): the plot harness has nothing to replay, and `import_case` refuses it, because such a case belongs in the prep or scope eval fixtures.
- **A case explains itself.** A chat case holds only the conversation, so it could not tell a plot the page did not show from a run that failed while the reply called it ready, and a round that ended `render_failed` named no gate and no error. Every form of the bundle carries two content-free facts in `session`: `versions`, the number of versions the session holds, and `last_run` (`schemas.LastRun`: the latest plot run's status, reason, stored version, user turn and rounds, kept in the session state by every run that yields a result, `not_ready` too). Each round (`schemas.Round`) also carries `gates_failed`, the ids of the host gates any of its renders failed or reported on (`R1`, `R1-timeout`, `R3`, `G8`), and `sandbox_failures`, the closed class of each render that crashed (`key` for a `KeyError`, `timeout`, `memory`): the usage telemetry's vocabularies, never a message, because code under test can raise a data cell. The root's copy of the result leaves those two out. The manifest keeps them as part of the bundle, so its schema stays 1 and no column changes; the case detail answers them as `rounds`, `session_versions` and `last_run`, and the triage view shows a line per round (`round 1: render_failed at rendering · 1 finding · gates R1 · crash key`) and a session line (`session: 0 versions · latest run (turn 2): failed, render · stored no version`), with the latest run's rounds on a chat case. Since 2026-11-09 every form of the bundle also carries `turns`, the record of the session's latest 50 turns (`agents/anyplot/turn_record.py`), because the persona round of 2026-11-06 still had to infer root causes such as whether a refusal came from the output guard or from the root: per turn its number (the transcript's user line; a turn refused before the judge has none and shares the next turn's number), `action`, `kind`, `refusal` and `error` as the `chat_turn` line has them (`telemetry.turn_codes`), every root tool call in order with the guards' verdict, what it did and its answer's code (`RequestLedger.tool_calls`, refused calls included), the verdicts of the turn hooks (`ledger.TURN_HOOKS`: the scope judge, the output guard, the reply checks, the Budget plugin's exemption and halt, the run queue, the route's refusals before the judge) as `(guard, verdict)` pairs that `attribution` itself notes, and the `prepare_data` operations. Closed codes only (`other` outside a set), with `attack` folded into `out_of_scope`; a ring per session in `Services.turns`, gone with the session. The record is no join key: a turn's sequence of hooks and verdicts fingerprints the session's attribution lines, which carry the user and the session hash, so the triage never matches it against a log or telemetry line.
- **Analytics.** `agent_result_feedback{reaction, include_data, subject}` with enum properties only; `subject` is `result` or `chat`.
- **Privacy.** Phase 1 holds owner data only. Before real users can submit: consent text on the control, a legal-page entry for the case store (purpose, retention of 180 days, EU bucket), `include_data` off by default, and a delete-my-case route keyed by the case id shown to the user.

## Usage telemetry

Built since 2026-10-16 in the repository; the Google Cloud side is a one-time setup ([Agent usage telemetry](../reference/agent-telemetry.md)). Feedback cases answer "what went wrong in this session"; the telemetry answers "how is the chat used": which inputs arrive and which are refused, how many turns a first plot takes, how often a run needs a repair, which sessions end with a kept result, and what each step costs.

- **Content-free by construction.** Owner rule of 2026-10-16: no user input, question, reply, data value, column name, file or sheet name, or cell, ever; only enums, coarse buckets, counts, durations and ids from closed sets. Each event is a strict pydantic model (`agents/anyplot/telemetry.py`), warning sentences become closed codes before they reach a line (`agents/anyplot/data/warning_codes.py`), and a canary test runs a whole session with distinctive content and fails if any of it appears in a line. Content still reaches the owner only through the explicit feedback case above.
- **Four events, one writer.** `data_input`, `chat_turn`, `pipeline_run` and `outcome`, one wide JSON line each, all written by anyplot-agents on its own logger; the page's outcome beacon (copy, download, save, feedback) goes through the BFF to `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/outcome`, so every line carries the same salted session hash. The per-hook attribution lines stay for debugging and keep the HMAC user id; telemetry lines never carry it.
- **Session hash.** HMAC-SHA256 of the session id under a random per-process salt: the id is in every request URL Cloud Run logs, so a plain hash could be joined to the request log. Sessions die with the instance, so the salt lives exactly as long as they do.
- **Storage and analysis.** A sink routes `jsonPayload.log_type="agent_telemetry"` from anyplot-agents into the log bucket `agent-telemetry` (europe-west4, 180 days, owner decision of 2026-10-16), which BigQuery reads through a linked dataset. The committed views (`agents/telemetry/views/*.sql`) deduplicate by idempotency key and add sessions with a 30-minute inactivity rule, usage, failures and cost; Looker Studio reads them, and the admin `/debug` page shows a small "agent usage" card through `GET /debug/agent-usage` (aggregates only).
- **Harness runs and fan-out sessions.** The eval harnesses tag their lines `source: harness`, and the sessions the admin's "plot in all libraries" opens tag theirs `source: fanout` (since 2026-10-30); the views and the card count `live` sessions.
- **Privacy.** The legal page's data assistant section describes the telemetry: the purpose, the fields in plain words (the plot language among them since 2026-10-30), 180 days, the EU location, no content, pseudonymous rather than anonymous. A changed field updates it ([On the legal page](../reference/agent-telemetry.md#on-the-legal-page)).

## Model-version regression harness

Testing a newer model version, the other provider's arm, a different judge model, or a prompt or validator change is one command locally or one workflow dispatch in CI, and the answer is a diff against the pinned baseline.

Built on 2026-10-10: the harness, the two fixture generators and the 146 fixture cases, 150 since 2026-10-15 (the 127 of the full matrix and the 23 stress cases with their model-free `--static` stage), the reports, the diff, the review gallery, the blind two-run gallery with its scoring, the model-free catalogue eligibility sweep `agents/evals/eligibility.py` (`agents/README.md`, "Run the regression harness"), and the judge-only scope eval `agents/evals/scope.py` with its refusal recall and false-refusal rate (`agents/README.md`, "Run the scope eval"; its first run passed both gates). Not built yet: `--prompts <dir>`, the Gemini baseline, and the CI workflow.

- **Fixture cases**, each a directory with `case.json` (spec, library, bindings, expected `accepted`, `rejected` or `unknown`, tags, the perturbation and the source of the data, and optionally a session `language` that the harness chooses through the language route before the first turn; a promoted case carries its session's when the user picked it, or, for a case from before 2026-11-08, detection set it), `data.csv`, and an optional `change_request.txt`. Two sources: the committed synthetic fixtures in `agents/evals/fixtures/cases/<id>/`, which are the spike-X matrix (10 specs times matplotlib and seaborn times 6 perturbation datasets) and the same specs and datasets for plotnine and altair (since 2026-10-25) and for pygal and lets-plot (since 2026-10-26; 360 generated cases, written by the seeded generator `agents/evals/make_fixtures.py`, of which the readiness scan blocks the 6 on the pygal file of `line-timeseries`, and those of `histogram-basic` too until its regeneration of 2026-10-28) plus eleven hand-written cases (one of them, since 2026-10-12, a German follow-up message that sets the session language; four, since 2026-10-15, choose a language and cover label density and number formats; four, since 2026-11-01, send a follow-up that asks for two changes at once, a language or a theme with a log scale, a grid or a colour, or one as a control, and carry `checks` that measure each part in the shipped version's code, language, theme and drawn texts) and the 23 stress cases of `agents/evals/make_stress.py` (tag `stress`, left out of `full`; see [Bounds](#bounds)), and the promoted feedback cases synced from the private bucket into the gitignored `agents/evals/.cases/` directory (never committed, because they hold users' data). The tag `smoke` marks the 16 cases of the smoke set (12 until 2026-10-25, when one plotnine and one altair case joined, and 14 until 2026-10-26, when one pygal and one lets-plot case joined). The scope set is the judge-only case file `agents/evals/scope.evalset.json` (see Evals below); the injection and code-escape sets stay as flow evals.
- **Harness** (`agents/evals/matrix.py`, the spike-X runner made permanent): runs every case through the real `/v1` flow in-process (httpx `ASGITransport` against `agents.main:app`, the same plugins and run queue, the models from the settings, and the render backend `--renderer` names: `remote` against a deployed renderer, `local` in Docker, or `fake` for a dry run of everything but the render) with candidate settings that are passed only to the harness: `--provider` (alone it selects that provider's default models; the settings refuse a model from the other provider), `--model`, `--judge-model`, `--location`, `--cases smoke|full|stress|<glob>`, `--static` (only the model-free static stage of `agents/evals/stress.py`: parse, eligibility, bindings, loader, prompt sizes and the stand-in renders), `--repeats N`, `--budget-usd` (stop before a case that could take the estimated list-price cost past it), and `--gcloud-token` (mint the developer's renderer token with the gcloud CLI and renew it before its one-hour expiry). Before the first case it renders each library's catalogue file once through the configured backend, without a model call, and stops with exit code 2 when that fails; during the run a pipeline that ended in `RendererUnavailable` (the `error` class on its `pipeline_result` attribution line), or three errors in a row, stop it with exit code 4, so an outage never becomes a matrix of failed runs or a baseline. Per case it records the status and reason, attempts, gate failures by gate, validator rejections, edit-apply failures, the reviewer verdict, LLM calls, `model_version`, tokens (prompt, candidates, thoughts, cached, cache writes, the judge's input and output), cost at list price, the time to the first event, the end-to-end time and the render time, and, since report schema 2, the stage that stopped each attempt and the run (for example `adapter_truncated`, `edit_apply` or `reviewer_defects`, never a bare `validation`), the shipped and the last reviewed attempt, each attempt's adapter outcome, finish reason and plan shape, the edit-failure kinds, every model call's finish reason and tokens, the answers that failed their schema, by agent kind and outcome (`answer_outcomes`: `repaired` or `refused`) and by broken rule (`answer_rules`), the edits each tolerant matching tier applied (`edit_tolerant`), and the module labels of banned imports (`banned_imports`). It reads them from the stream and from the content-free attribution lines that the pipeline, the Budget plugin and the judges write per request id. It writes `<out>/<date>-<model>.json`, a Markdown summary, and a review gallery of 30 renders sampled at random from the runs that passed, with an accept and reject pair each and a JSON export, and prints a diff table against `agents/evals/baselines/<pinned-model>.json` (or `--baseline`) over the cases both reports hold: pass rate, accept-match rate (cases whose outcome matches the case's expected outcome), cost per successful plot, p50 and p95 latency, and every case that flipped. A run passes when it ships a render that passed every deterministic gate (no padded canvas, no ADAPTATION finding); every rate counts harness errors as runs that did not pass. The owner's judgement is the acceptance measure: to compare two arms, `python -m agents.evals.report blind` merges both reports into one gallery of the same cases with the arm hidden in a separate key file, and `report score` gives the acceptance per arm. The exit code is 1 when the pass rate on the shared cases drops below the baseline minus a tolerance (5 percentage points by default) or a run crashed in the harness, 2 for a setup error, 3 when the budget stopped the run, and 4 for an outage.
