- **Baselines.** `agents/evals/baselines/claude-haiku-5-5.json` is committed with the default model: the thinking run of 2026-10-11 on main at `e2e25ef61` (122 cases with one run each, root thinking 1,024 and adapter thinking 2,048, through the deployed renderer; 122 passed, 62 `ok`), which replaced the thinking-off rerun of spike X of 2026-10-10 (244 runs). `gemini-3.8-flash.json` follows with the Gemini arm, so the price and quality comparison is a diff between two reports. `--save-baseline` refuses a run that stopped early, holds promoted cases, or has a run that ended in an error. A model bump is a PR that changes the `AGENT_MODEL` default and adds the new baseline file; a unit test asserts that a baseline exists for the pinned model once any baseline is committed. The same mechanism covers judge-model bumps and ADK upgrades, because the ADK version is part of the report stamp.
- **CI.** `.github/workflows/agents-eval.yml` has `workflow_dispatch` inputs `provider`, `model`, `judge_model`, `location`, `cases` (smoke or full) and `repeats`, and a nightly `smoke` run on the pinned model. It authenticates through Workload Identity Federation, sets `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=eu`, uploads the report as an artifact and writes the diff table into the job summary. It mirrors the candidate-versus-baseline pattern of `review-retest.yml` (see [Review retest](../workflows/review-retest.md)). A lifecycle probe in the nightly run calls `models.get` on the pinned model and fails loudly on a 404 or a retirement notice, so a forced bump of the pinned model, or of the Gemini arm's short-term 3.8 Flash tier, is noticed before users are.
- **Cost.** A full run is 367 cases (123 until 2026-10-15, 127 until 2026-10-25 and 247 until 2026-10-26) times at most 2 attempts. Measured on 2026-10-10 at list price for the full matrix of that day: $0.52 on the Claude Haiku 5.5 arm ($0.0051 per passed plot, median $0.0041, 4.77 model calls per run) and $15.89 on the Gemini 3.8 Flash arm ($0.169 per passed plot, median $0.124, 4.80 model calls per run, $3.99 of it on failed runs); both fit inside the spend cap, and `smoke` (12 cases) was about a tenth of that run. Today's full run holds about three times as many cases, and the smoke set's 16 are about 4 % of them.

## Serving and infrastructure

- **`anyplot-agents`**, a new Cloud Run service built from `agents/Dockerfile` and deployed by `agents/cloudbuild.yaml` (both built on 2026-10-10; the service has run at `https://anyplot-agents-r3tvmejsmq-ez.a.run.app` since 2026-10-11, deployed by the `deploy-agents` trigger on every push to `main` that touches it) with the renderer's candidate-then-smoke-then-promote steps and `--update-*` flags only. Like the renderer's, its deploy step creates the service on the first build without `--no-traffic`, because gcloud rejects `--no-traffic` on a new service, and only when `services describe` reports the service as not found; the API side stayed dark through `AGENT_ENABLED=false` until the switch-on of 2026-10-11. Flags: `gcloud run deploy anyplot-agents --region=europe-west4 --execution-environment=gen2 --no-allow-unauthenticated --service-account=anyplot-agents@anyplot.iam.gserviceaccount.com --cpu=1 --memory=2Gi --min-instances=${_MIN_INSTANCES} --max-instances=1 --concurrency=80 --timeout=1200 --update-env-vars=^|^... --no-traffic --tag=candidate`, where `_MIN_INSTANCES` is 1 since 2026-11-02 (0 before): a warm instance keeps the in-memory sessions and day counters through idle periods and spares the first message a cold start, and the account's unused Compute Flexible commitment absorbs its idle cost (about 0.44 CHF a day at list price) until the commitment's first part ends on 2027-01-15. No secrets and no Cloud SQL in phase 1. The smoke step checks that Cloud Run IAM refuses an anonymous `GET /v1/status` with exactly `403` (a `401` would come from the container's own caller check, past IAM) and, with `_SMOKE_AUTH=true`, calls `/v1/status` with an ID token impersonated for `anyplot-agents@` (the renderer's token recipe) and checks that it names the provider, model, location and libraries the build configured; the bootstrap build runs with `_SMOKE_AUTH=false`, because the service has no URL and no invoker binding before it exists. The image installs `--extra agents` without plotting extras, copies the source tree with the prompts, the fonts and the catalogue prompt sources at their repository paths, runs as uid 1000, and fails its build when a prompt source or a font is missing: the build imports `agents.main` and reads the two judge rubrics, which the service otherwise reads on first use. The root `.dockerignore` keeps `agents/evals` and ADK's local `.adk/` store, both of which can hold users' data, out of the image. `.github/workflows/ci-image.yml` builds it before merge and checks `/v1/status`, the production default's `401` for an anonymous caller, the files in the image and the uid. The concurrency and the timeout follow the run queue, not the memory: the queue, not Cloud Run, limits the runs in memory, but every queued run holds an open stream, so the instance must accept the queue's 60 waiting streams, its 5 runs in flight and the other routes (`--concurrency=80` since 2026-11-01; 20 before, for 10 waiting streams and 1 run), and one turn can last the 600 s maximum wait plus the 540 s deadline (`--timeout=1200` since 2026-10-13, 900 before). The FastAPI app in `agents/main.py` sets `docs_url=None`. The service runs no code of its own users: it has no `--sandbox-launcher`, and every render goes to `anyplot-renderer` through the `remote` backend. Its 2 vCPU and 4 GiB were sized when it still hosted the sandboxes; since 2026-10-29 it runs on 1 vCPU and 2 GiB (owner decision): over the 14 days before, its hourly p99 peaked at 0.46 of a core and 370 MiB, and the worst case of about 1.1 GiB (the dataset and render stores' 256 MiB caps, the process, a 1 MB paste's parse and the PNG gates) keeps headroom, because an out-of-memory kill would lose every session in memory. The renderer keeps 2 vCPU and 4 GiB: its renders peaked at 1.2 cores (the sandbox's gVisor and the host share the CPU with the render), a Makie render needs up to 1.5 GiB, and a single render is no faster with more CPUs (a Makie heatmap took 8.9 s locally at 1, 2 and 4 CPUs alike: Julia compiles on one thread). The renderer scales to zero and bills only while it serves a request; the agents service keeps one instance warm (see its `_MIN_INSTANCES` above).
- **`anyplot-renderer`**, a phase-1 Cloud Run service that runs the code (`agents/renderer/`), deployed by `agents/renderer/cloudbuild.yaml` with the same candidate, smoke and promote steps. Since 2026-10-29 its image builds on the toolchain image `anyplot-toolchain` (`agents/renderer/toolchain.Dockerfile`: `python:3.14-slim` pinned by digest, R 4.6.1 with the pipeline's package set, and the exec filter's preload library for the exec-mode rows; step 2a of `agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md`). The deploy hashes the toolchain's inputs (`agents/renderer/toolchain.py`), builds and pushes `anyplot-toolchain:<hash>` to the private Artifact Registry repository `anyplot` only when that tag is missing, and builds the renderer from it by digest; the CI job pulls the same tag through Workload Identity Federation (`roles/artifactregistry.reader` on the repository) or builds it. Its deploy step creates the service on the first build, because gcloud refuses `--no-traffic` for a service that does not exist yet; it does so only when `services describe` reports the service as not found, and stops on any other describe error, so a permission or API error never sends all traffic to an unsmoked revision. Flags: `gcloud beta run deploy anyplot-renderer --region=europe-west4 --execution-environment=gen2 --sandbox-launcher --no-allow-unauthenticated --service-account=anyplot-renderer@anyplot.iam.gserviceaccount.com --cpu=2 --memory=4Gi --min-instances=${_MIN_INSTANCES} --max-instances=1 --concurrency=4 --timeout=300 --add-volume=name=runs,type=in-memory,size-limit=512Mi --add-volume-mount=volume=runs,mount-path=/tmp/runs --no-traffic --tag=candidate --update-env-vars=^|^ENVIRONMENT=production|RENDERER_ALLOWED_CALLERS=...|RENDERER_AUDIENCES=...`, where `_MIN_INSTANCES` is 0 unless a build sets 1 to keep an instance warm. One instance with one render slot, because renders are serial; `--concurrency=4` so a second caller gets the slot's `503 busy` after 30 s instead of a Cloud Run 429, and a cancel reaches the instance while a render holds the slot; the run directories on the in-memory volume (see [Render](#render)). No public domain, no secrets, no Cloud SQL and no role: the service account exists only to be an identity. The process runs as root, because `sandbox do` creates the gVisor sandboxes; the model-written code runs inside them. The smoke step checks that IAM refuses an anonymous caller (403) and, with `_SMOKE_RENDER=true`, checks that `/status` reports the launcher and the volume and renders a matplotlib plot through the candidate as the service's own account, which proves the launcher, the volume and the harness together; a Makie, a Chart.js and a plotly render and a data run follow, and each one gates the promotion too. These render probes live in `agents/renderer/deploy_smoke.sh`, which the step calls with the token in the environment, because Cloud Build refuses the whole build when one step argument is longer than 10,000 characters: the inline step reached 11,347 with the plotly probe, and PB-2a's renderer did not deploy (build `dc1cf3ae` of 2026-11-01). `tests/unit/test_cloudbuild_configs.py` keeps every Cloud Build config of the repository under that limit. The build impersonates `anyplot-renderer@` for that token (`gcloud auth print-identity-token --impersonate-service-account`), because a build cannot mint an ID token for its own identity: gcloud refuses `--include-email` for it and Cloud Build's metadata server has no `identity` endpoint (builds `da6f53f5` and `a9d3ac7f` of 2026-10-10). The token is minted for the service URL while the requests go to the `candidate` tag URL, because Cloud Run accepts no other audience. `.github/workflows/ci-image.yml` builds the same image before merge and renders a seaborn plot through its harness.

| Service | Runs | Called by | Identity and roles |
|---|---|---|---|
| `anyplot-api` | The site API, the admin BFF under `/debug/agent` and the public lane under `/agent` (dark until `AGENT_PUBLIC_ENABLED`) | Browsers, through Cloudflare | `anyplot-api@`, a dedicated least-privilege account since 2026-11-02 (the default compute service account before) |
| `anyplot-agents` | The ADK runner, the run queue, the gates, the stores | `anyplot-api`, the deploy smoke (`roles/run.invoker`) | `anyplot-agents@`: `roles/aiplatform.user`, `roles/telemetry.writer` |
| `anyplot-renderer` | Code in Cloud Run sandboxes, one at a time | `anyplot-agents`, the owner's `adk web`, the deploy smoke (`roles/run.invoker`) | `anyplot-renderer@`: no role |
- **IAM** (owner tasks, all granted on 2026-10-11 except the Workload Identity Federation principal's): the service account `anyplot-agents@` gets `roles/aiplatform.user` and `roles/telemetry.writer`; the API's runtime identity and `anyplot-agents@` itself (the deploy smoke calls the candidate as that account) get `roles/run.invoker` on the service; the Cloud Build identity gets `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `anyplot-agents@` (to deploy as it, and to mint the smoke's token as it); the service account `anyplot-renderer@` gets no role; `anyplot-agents@`, the owner's account and `anyplot-renderer@` itself (the render smoke calls the candidate as that account) get `roles/run.invoker` on `anyplot-renderer`, and the Cloud Build identity gets `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `anyplot-renderer@` (to deploy as it, and to mint the smoke's token as it); the GitHub Workload Identity Federation principal gets `roles/aiplatform.user` for evals. Phase 2 adds `cloudsql.client` with a read-only role `anyplot_agents_ro` (SELECT on specs, impls, libraries and languages only, never `feedback`), `secretAccessor` on single secrets, `storage.objectAdmin` on the private bucket, and `modelarmor.user`.
- **Environment on anyplot-agents**, as `agents/cloudbuild.yaml` sets it: `ENVIRONMENT=production`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT=anyplot`, `GOOGLE_GENAI_USE_ENTERPRISE=TRUE`, `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION=eu`, `AGENT_LOCATION=eu` (never europe-west4), `AGENT_PROVIDER`, `AGENT_MODEL` and `AGENT_JUDGE_MODEL` from the substitutions `_PROVIDER`, `_MODEL` and `_JUDGE_MODEL`, whose defaults are the settings defaults (`anthropic-vertex`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, `claude-haiku-5-5`; the Gemini arm: `gemini`, `gemini-3.8-flash`, `gemini-3.5-flash-lite`), so a deploy switches the arm without a code change, `AGENT_LIBRARIES=matplotlib,seaborn,plotnine,altair,pygal,letsplot` (`_LIBRARIES`, every library with a runtime since 2026-10-28), `AGENT_RENDERER=remote`, `AGENT_RENDER_URL=<anyplot-renderer service URL, status.url>` (`_RENDER_URL`, which the deploy requires, because `--update-env-vars` would otherwise replace a working URL with nothing), `AGENT_ALLOWED_CALLERS=<API SA email>,<anyplot-agents@>` (`_API_CALLER`, by default the Compute Engine default service account, and `_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, so a build cannot drop either), `AGENT_SERVICE_URLS=<service URL, added by the deploy>[,<_AUDIENCES>]`, and `ADK_CAPTURE_MESSAGE_CONTENT_IN_SPANS=false`. The budgets, limits and deadlines (the `AGENT_*` budgets, `AGENT_MAX_LLM_CALLS`, the run queue, the render and request bounds) are not set, so the deployed code's settings defaults apply (`agents/anyplot/settings.py`, see [Bounds](#bounds)) and a change to them is a reviewed code change; `ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS` is not set either, because every run of the service passes its own `RunConfig`. On anyplot-renderer: `ENVIRONMENT=production`, `RENDERER_ALLOWED_CALLERS=<anyplot-agents@>,<Cloud Build identity>[,<owner email>]`, `RENDERER_AUDIENCES=<service URL, added by the deploy>[,<OAuth client ids of the owner's tokens>]`, never a tag URL, which Cloud Run does not accept as an audience; the limits keep their defaults (`agents/renderer/settings.py`). On anyplot-api: `AGENT_ENABLED` (true since 2026-10-11 through the deploy-api trigger's `_AGENT_ENABLED`; false ships the routes dark, answering 404), `AGENT_PUBLIC_ENABLED` (since 2026-11-02, the trigger's `_AGENT_PUBLIC_ENABLED`, default false: the public door on top of `AGENT_ENABLED`, which stays the master switch; flipping it is the public launch, and the deploy smoke checks the door answers as set), `AGENT_SERVICE_URL` (the agents service's URL, `_AGENT_SERVICE_URL`), and `AGENT_USER_ID_KEY` from Secret Manager (bound since 2026-10-11; the BFF answers 404 while any of the three is unset, so a deploy never breaks). On the app: `VITE_ENABLE_AGENT_CHAT`, a build-time flag that tree-shakes the chunk.
- **Sessions and artifacts.** Phase 1 uses `InMemorySessionService`, `InMemoryArtifactService` and in-memory dataset and render stores; they are consistent because `max-instances=1`, and scale-to-zero shows "session expired". Phase 2 moves to `DatabaseSessionService` in a separate database `anyplot_agents` on `anyplot-db` with its own user and an hourly purge of sessions older than 24 h, because `alembic/env.py` has no `include_object` filter and ADK's `create_all` tables would read as drift; artifacts go to `GcsArtifactService` on a private EU bucket with a 1-day lifecycle, never `anyplot-images`.
- **BFF** in `api/routers/agent.py`: `APIRouter(prefix="/debug/agent", dependencies=[Depends(require_admin)])`. A small refactor `require_admin_identity()` returns `AdminIdentity(email|None, via)` and `require_admin` wraps it unchanged. `user_id = "adm_" + HMAC(key, email or "token")[:16]`. POST requests need `Content-Type: application/json`, `X-Anyplot-Client: agent-chat/1` and an allowed Origin. ID tokens come from `google.oauth2.id_token.fetch_id_token` (skipped for localhost). The messages route is an async generator declared with `response_class=EventSourceResponse` (which is what gives FastAPI's automatic 15 s pings; Cloudflare returns 524 after 125 s), reads upstream with httpx `aiter_lines()`, assembles complete events, re-validates each event type against the `anyplot/1` allowlist, and yields `ServerSentEvent(event=..., raw_data=...)`; on upstream failure it emits `error{code:"upstream"}`. Routes mirror `/v1` plus `GET eligibility`, `POST sessions/{sid}/feedback`, and the triage routes `GET /debug/agent/cases`, `GET /debug/agent/cases/{id}` and `PATCH /debug/agent/cases/{id}`. The deploy smoke test expects 401 on `/debug/agent/status`.
- **Public lane** in `api/routers/agent_public.py` (since 2026-11-02, shipped dark): `APIRouter(prefix="/agent")` behind `AGENT_PUBLIC_ENABLED`, which calls the admin lane's route functions with its own context. The identity is `require_public_identity`: the page's tab id from `X-Anyplot-Anon` plus the client's network from `client_ip` (Cloudflare's `CF-Connecting-IP`; IPv4 address or IPv6 /64), both forwarded upstream for the agents service to salt (`X-Anyplot-Anon`, `X-Anyplot-Network`), with `X-Anyplot-Tier: public`. Opening a session and storing a feedback case need a Cloudflare Turnstile token in `X-Turnstile-Token`, which `api/turnstile.py` redeems at siteverify, failing closed; the other routes act on a session such a call opened ([Bot check](../reference/api.md#bot-check)). The session id travels as `X-Anyplot-Session`, never in a URL, because Cloud Run keeps request URLs with the client address for 30 days. Admin-only and absent: the fan-out's `dataset/copy` and `source: fanout`, `.save()`'s `GET /sessions/{sid}/bundle`, `GET /budget` and the `/cases*` triage; a test maps every admin route onto a public one or that list. `GET /agent/lane` answers `{"open": true}` without an identity or an upstream call, so the frontend learns at runtime whether to show the entry points: one switch opens and closes everything. A per-instance stream cap (`AGENT_PUBLIC_MAX_STREAMS`, 20) and a per-network hourly feedback cap (`AGENT_PUBLIC_FEEDBACK_PER_HOUR`, 20) are the lane's own; the per-user and per-IP limits live in the agents service. The route table is in [the API reference](../reference/api.md#public-lane).
- **SSE protocol `anyplot/1`**, translated from ADK events and never forwarded raw: `ready{v, run_id}`, sent when the turn starts, at once; `status{step:"queued", position, waiting}` mid-turn while its `plot_pipeline` or `run_data_code` call waits in the run queue, at once, on every change and every 15 s unchanged (`position` 1 runs next, `waiting` counts every queued entry, this one included); `status{step:"started"}` once when a run that waited takes its slot, the only end of the wait a turn without pipeline steps has before its reply; `status{step, attempt, max_attempts}` for the pipeline's steps (`max_attempts`, since 2026-11-08, the run's round cap `AGENT_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, so the page counts tries from the server); `message{text}` (root final text only), `plot{PlotResult}` (with `memory{kept, added}` beside `version` since 2026-11-08: the plot memory entry ids the stored version kept from before the run and the ones it added, from `RequestLedger.shipped_memory`, settled with the version; never on a plot without a version), `refusal{code, text}`, `error{code, ref}` with codes `capacity` (also for a run that waited the queue's maximum), `run_limit` (since 2026-10-21, a sandbox run past the user's `AGENT_USER_MAX_RUNS` across sessions), `deadline`, `guard_unavailable`, `no_reply` (since 2026-11-06, the root wrote no reply, also when asked once more), `upstream` and `internal`, and `done{llm_calls, tokens, bindings?, data_confirmed?, dataset?, language?, turn?}` (all but the first two are optional: after a run `bindings` and `data_confirmed` carry the session's bindings and whether the data is confirmed, so the page syncs its dropdowns and its step after a turn in which the root's `set_bindings` changed them, `dataset` carries the new data version after a turn in which the root's `prepare_data` or `generate_data` changed the data, and `language` carries the session language as `{language, source}`, `source` one of `default`, `chosen` and `offer` (`pasted` and `detected` until 2026-11-08), and `offer` (`{language, basis}`) when the turn's message offered a language, so the page syncs its chip (the dataset route's answer carries the same field after a paste); a turn refused before the queue, such as the budget refusal, and a session gone meanwhile omit them; since 2026-11-08 `turn`, last, after a turn that ended without an error, a refusal or the user's stop (`main.ended_well`): what it changed, one of `data`, `plot`, `switch`, `language`, `kept`, `memory` and `answer`, the first in that order the turn changed, derived in `stream.turn_outcome` from the ledger's record of the root's tool calls, `RequestLedger.tool_calls`, which ToolSafety writes per call as a closed tool name, the guards' verdict and a closed outcome, never an argument; the feedback case bundle is to read the same record). The translator tolerates the ADK 2.x `node_info` and `output` fields. A user already over the daily budget gets `ready`, `refusal{code:"budget"}` and `done` without waiting in the queue. Because queued time does not count toward the agents service's deadline, the BFF restarts its own turn budget (`AGENT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_S`) on every queued status, with the queue's 15 s heartbeat on top because the run may start that long before its first event, and on the first event after the wait. Nothing moves a turn past `AGENT_TURN_MAX_S` (1,190 s), which stays below anyplot-api's `--timeout=1200`: a turn still queued when its run could no longer finish inside that cap ends with `error{code:"capacity"}`, and closing the upstream stream takes it out of the queue before it spends a token. Every event carries the SSE id `<run_id>.<n>` and the agents stream writes a `: ping` after every 15 s without one, as the BFF's own route does, so no hop idles during a model call. The session keeps its latest turn's events (`agents/turn_log.py`): when the connection between the browser and the edge fails mid-turn (an HTTP/3 error 104 s into a 126 s turn on 2026-11-05, which both origin hops ran to its end and whose plot they stored), the page reads the rest with `GET .../turn` and `Last-Event-ID` after 1, 2, 4 and 8 s instead of showing an error, and a retry click after a cut it could not read reads the cut turn first, so the same plot is never made twice. The log only records: a client the agents service sees go still cancels the run.
- **Frontend.** A lazy `app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.tsx` at `/agent`, one page for visitors and admins since 2026-11-06 (see **One page** below; the admin's page was `/debug/agent` until then), laid out as one chat column (since 2026-10-11, "chat first") in one terminal-style frame (since 2026-11-06, the v4 design; `ChatFrame.tsx`, style guide §7.9): a 1 px rule with a small radius, the tab label `~/anyplot.ai/agent` on its top border, the header row in plain words inside it, dashed rules under the header row and above the composer, and at its foot the status line `>>> step N of 2 · …`, which replaced the step header above the column and the step lines above the message box; the session's opening, an ineligible pair and an error stand in the same frame below the header row, and the admin fan-out's panel stays below it. The column grows with the window up to 1360 px (960 px until 2026-11-02): the data card, the plot cards and the code take the full width, while the thread's replies, turns and notices and a card's text lists keep a readable line of at most about 80 characters (`CHAT_TEXT_MAX`). Since 2026-11-05 the composer's one message box takes the data too (the data card's textarea with `.parse()`, `.open()`, `.replace()`, `.keep()` and its drop zone until then): a paste is routed before it reaches the box (`routeInput.ts`: a table, JSON, a web table copied one cell per line or a list of label/value pairs attaches, at any length, and so does short text of at most 2,000 characters that is mostly values, at least three and half its words, without a question, a plot wish or a URL, which the server's code reads or its transcriber offers for `.use()` (owner rule of 2026-11-09; until then text over 2,000 characters attached whatever it held, and a short list such as `a:7,b:12,c:5` stayed a message the root could not read data from), while one line, code, prose, a formula or a request for data stays typed text, also over the cap, where the box holds it with its red counter; the shared cases in `routeInput.cases.json` name the server's class for each, and `tests/unit/agents/test_route_cases.py` holds `classify_paste` to them; since 2026-11-05 Enter routes the box's own text the same way when no attachment waits, so a table typed by hand, or pasted where the browser reported no paste, goes to the dataset route at once without a note, and × on its red chip puts it back into the box), and a file opened with `.open()` in the line above the message box, dropped anywhere on the chat or pasted as a file always attaches (a binary format read on the server first). The attachment waits above the text as a chip (`AttachmentChip.tsx`, `useChatAttachment.ts`): its name or `table`, its lines and size, and ×; red with the reason over 1 MB or 20,050 lines (one JSON document exempt from the line count, JSON Lines not) or when the dataset route refused it. Enter (or the `↵` in the box on a touch screen) posts it with the typed text as its note, which becomes the first turn (see the request flow, step 3); a table read from text puts the note back into the box, and `.discard()` of it gives back what it came from: short pasted text into the box, a file or a text over 2,000 characters as the attachment. While a chip waits, the plot button takes it along (since 2026-11-08, inert before: the owner opened a file, typed a note and found the button greyed out): one click sends the chip as Enter would, with the typed text as its note, and once the reading comes back clean the page confirms the data and starts the plot by itself, as a click on the plot button would on that data, under the button's name in the thread (`plotChain.ts`). A table typed into the box goes the same way. Clean means that the data was stored, the reading's turn reached its `done` without a refusal or an error, every required role has a column, the data card shows no warning (the parser's, a paste of several tables, a wide table's `.to_long()` offer, a bound column's own warning), the server's binding check names none (the page asks it once more when the reading's turn changed the data or the bindings, and the card shows under the bindings line the check's lines that no bound column's own warning holds: a role left open for negative values, a column of another window, an inverted bound pair, a key whose rows repeat), and the reading's last reply asks no question (its last visible character is a question mark, as the service's `ends_with_question` reads it). Anything else stops at the data card, where the status line says what to look at (`1 warning on the data · check above, then .create_plot()`, `the assistant asked a question · answer it in the box, or .create_plot()`), and the next click plots as before; so does a refused, failed or stopped reading. Typing, a paste, Enter, another click, a control of the data card or a look at it (`.change()`, `.view_all()`) while the reading runs ends the chain, which never plots twice and is never restored after a reload. While the chain reads, the status line says `reading your data · the plot follows by itself unless something needs a look`; while a chip waits, it names both keys (`.create_plot() reads the attached table and plots it · Enter ↵ only reads it`), and after a plot the button reads `plot the new data`. Enter still only sends. The button is active whenever Enter would send the box's data: one rule decides the button and its click (`plotClick`). Since 2026-11-09 this includes the admin's `.plot_all()`: with `all` pressed, the one click reads the chip and opens the fan-out once it reads clean, never over a panel still open. Until then, `.plot_all()` stayed inert while a chip waited, and only Enter took the chip. A browser that hides pasted text from the paste event (iOS Safari) is routed on its `insertFromPaste` input only. Since 2026-11-05 the data card (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/DataPanel.tsx`) stands in the thread right after the paste it came from (`ChatThread.tsx`; the first turn of the thread until then): the paste is the user's line as its chip named it (`▤ table · 1,204 lines · 48 KB`), and the card follows; a later paste gets its line and a card of its own below, and the card it replaced stays folded where it stood; a table read from text waits in a card of its own at its paste, which `.use()` turns into the data card and `.discard()` into a `# table discarded` line; example data, a formula or code without data adds the card at the end. Since 2026-11-07 the reducer keeps an item per data version with a snapshot of what it held, numbered per session (`data v3`, since the server's version starts over with every paste), an item per library switch with the decisions it carries, and on a typed turn what it changed (`TurnOutcome`), and since the v4 chapters of the same day the thread renders them all (`threadModel.ts`, `ChatThread.tsx`): it reads in chapters, a `# data` or `# plot` line (`ChapterLine.tsx`, the name an `h3` in a row between two rules) where the talk turns from the data to the plot and back, a library switch is a line of its own (`· plotting in seaborn from here, 3 decisions kept`), and every data version but the live one folds to its label (`▸ data v2 · sheet "2026" · 12 rows × 5 cols`, `FoldedData.tsx`), which opens it read-only from its snapshot. Since 2026-11-08 a typed turn that changed nothing is a question, the third kind of turn: the server's `done.turn` names what a turn changed, the page holds it against what the turn's stream showed (a plot, a data version, a switch, a chosen language win over the server's `answer`, and a value the page does not know is a change, never a question) and against the memory read after the turn, and only then gives the bubble a `?` mark and the reply a first line `? answer · nothing changed`; the note typed with an attachment is never one. A chapter's line counts its questions (`· 2 questions`), a button that moves the focus to each in turn with a short green highlight, and the latest `# plot` line holds the remembered decisions (`3 decisions ▾`, see "Plot memory and the library switch"). Where a version goes (`placeData`, owner, 2026-11-07): a turn's puts the live card right after the turn's message, before its reply (the card arrives with `done`, after the reply streamed in, and `HoldReadingPosition.tsx` scrolls by the card's height so the reply stays where the user reads it; the browser's own anchoring moved it, since it anchors on the message above the card); a paste brings a card of its own at the end; one of the card's own controls (`.undo()`, `.to_long()`, the table and sheet pickers) moves it to the thread's end, before and after a plot alike, after a plot as a new `# data` chapter, so each older version stays folded with its own summary under it. The number a version carries comes from one function, `dataVersionNumber` in `useAgentSession.ts` (the reducer's own count until backend PR B4c numbers versions once per session on the server). The card moves under its own id, so React keeps its state, and the keyboard focus follows it (`useFollowFocus.ts`: back to the control that made the version if it is still there and enabled, else onto the card's label). The page names a data version only by that number: the card's label, its `# changed` and `# undone · back to the version before` lines, its full view and the status line never show the server's `version`. The page's own turns show as the button the user pressed, as it read, with the library for a plot (`.create_plot()`, `.update_plot()`, `plot the new data`, `.start_over()`, also for a switch's carry, which read `plot in seaborn with your 3 decisions` until 2026-11-07; the action's name until 2026-11-05), and the `summarize_data` turn, which no button sends, shows no line of its own. The assistant's replies set a passage in backticks off as inline code, without the backticks. The first reply after the live card comes into view as it arrives while the focus is in the composer, since a tall card would leave it below the fold. The card (since 2026-11-07 framed like the chat window, `CardFrame.tsx`, with the version on its top border, `data v3 · made long · 48 rows × 4 cols`) shows the preview table, its columns headed `name · type` on one line (`integer` reads `number`, `datetime` reads `date`), the bindings in one line under it (`x (across): month · y (up): revenue`; only `x` and `y` name their direction) with `.change()`, which opens the binding dropdowns (one per role until 2026-11-07, always open) and becomes `.hide()`, the dropdowns opening by themselves while a required role has no column or the server refused the bindings (on a phone the card shows no preview table, and `.view_all()` and `.change()` stand on its last line), a sheet picker for a workbook that reads the stored file again (`DatasetState.file`), and the `# changed` block with `.undo()` after the assistant changed the data or computed it from a formula, without `.undo()` when the formula made the session's first data; `.confirm()` sat at its foot until 2026-11-04; since then `.create_plot()` at the composer's end confirms the data in its own click. A formula, code or a request for data sent as an attachment gets the server's `422 formula`, `422 code` or `422 data_request`, and the page sends the text to the chat as the user's turn by itself (since 2026-11-04 for a formula and code, which the card offered with `.send()` before); code over the chat's 2,000 characters turns the chip red with a line that asks to shorten it. The assistant's summary of a stored parse is the next turn, below the card. The live card stays open in both steps since the v4 chapters of 2026-11-07: until then the click of `.create_plot()` collapsed it into a header pinned above the thread (`PinnedHeader`, `useDataCardView`) whose `.show()` and `.hide()` opened and closed it. Any binding change, undo, data request or new paste drops the confirmation on the server, and the page returns to step 1; a change of the data after a plot puts the card at the thread's end, so it is where the user reads. The page's `scroll-padding-top` (56px, the site's sticky masthead) keeps the focus and a jump below the masthead. The status line at the frame's foot (the step line above the composer until 2026-11-06) names what dropped the confirmation as the plot button's description (`bindings changed`, `data changed`, or `check the data` when only the server's word dropped it). Since 2026-11-06 the thread gets no notice of its own for it: the notice stood right above the step line and said the same, and every state a loss leaves is step 1 with data, where the step line shows. The first binding setup after a paste, where nothing was confirmed yet, names nothing. After a change to data the user had confirmed, the plot button reads `plot the new data` (since 2026-11-05) and the step line ends with that name (the card's own step-1 line went with the pinned header). A plot the server refuses with `data_unconfirmed` adds no row to the thread: the hook drops the confirmation once the turn is over, so a data change of the same turn, which arrives with its `done`, names the loss in the step line. A refusal reads with a plain label (`can't fetch data`, `outside this chat`, `chat paused`, ...; `REFUSAL_LABEL` in `messages.ts`, since 2026-11-06), and its code sits behind the label's ⓘ; the admin fan-out panel, whose rows read `<state> · <code>`, keeps `refused · <code>` with the same label as the line under it. The step line follows the data the card shows: a data request or undo after the loss names the changed data, and a new paste, which nobody has confirmed yet, names no loss. A table read from free text that waits for `.use()` or `.discard()` holds the page in step 1, even when a turn reports the server's confirmation of the stored data. A run's progress grid (below the thread from 2026-10-12, in the admin menu since 2026-11-09, where admins open it while visitors and admins read the same working line; the owner's variant A: one row per round, `first try`, `second try`, ... (`repair 1` until live round 9 of 2026-10-16, which read as no second round; a running repair round says what it fixes in plain words on the right, `fixing text size, layout`, from the rubric ids, `Round.defects`, that the round before passed on), the same four steps in the same columns so each step lines up under the round above, and how the round ended on the right, such as `✓ ok`, `3 things to fix`, `✗ code check failed` or, for the shipped round of a `needs_attention` result, `~ 1 note left` (the round marked `shipped`, which is not always the last: a repair that fails its check leaves the earlier render shipped); since 2026-11-05 a review or a measurement that found things to fix is the loop's normal course, so its step keeps a dot and its outcome a neutral ink (`✗ review: 3 findings` in red before); the step a round failed at (a render, a code check, an unusable answer) shows a red ✗ and the steps after it a hollow dot; the facts come from `PlotResult.rounds` and the `outcome` and `findings` that the `repairing` status carries for the round before it; the result card keeps the same grid under `# rounds`, behind its status line since 2026-11-02, and names the shipped round the same way (`second try`), or, when an earlier round's render won, which one (`first try shipped of 2`); on a phone the round's label and outcome take a line above its steps), a Stop button (`AbortController` plus the cancel route) and the composer. Since 2026-11-09 one working line at the thread's foot (`WorkingLine.tsx`, `workingLine` in `steps.ts`) says what any turn in flight does now, in the assistant's slot, for every turn and on both lanes: `drawing your plot · 0:42` for a plot run and a turn that reached a pipeline step, from the second try on with the try (`second try`, or `try 2 of 2` from the `max_attempts` the server sends on each step; never on the first try, owner 2026-11-09), on a phone `drawing · 0:42` (`drawing · second try · 0:42` from the second try on); `waiting · you are 3rd` while the own turn waits in the run queue, without a clock; the words of a turn without pipeline steps (`summarizing your data`, `making example data`, `working on your message`) until the first reply; `writing the reply` below a run's card (since 2026-11-05), one row of the reply's line height at the thread's gap, so the reply starts where the line started; `leaving the queue…`, `stopping after this step…` or `stopping…` after Stop, and `connection lost · reconnecting…` while a cut turn is read again. An error that ends the turn (`no_reply`, `capacity`, `deadline`) takes its place. The clock (`RunState.startedAt`, set when the page starts the turn and again at the end of a wait in the queue; `RunClock.tsx`) ticks once a second in a leaf of its own, computes the time from the clock at every tick, so a throttled background tab reads right again, and is hidden from screen readers; the late read of a cut turn has none. The line took the place of the thread's in-progress line, of the progress grid below the thread and of the queue counter beside the line (2026-11-04 to 2026-11-09), so one position shows on screen. Since 2026-11-04 the site's loader (`LoaderSpinner`, inline: the dots the plots gallery shows while it loads, still under `prefers-reduced-motion`, hidden from screen readers) marks the chat's waits in place of a pulsing `…`: the session's opening, where the public lane's bot check runs; the working line; and the attachment while a file is read on the server or the data is parsed. A sheet picker's read of another sheet counts as work in flight (`readSheet`), so the composer waits until its parse starts. While anything is in flight, the bindings block says that the bindings wait, and a running parse says so on the attachment (`parsing your data`). The composer is one row in two fixed places since 2026-11-07 (`Composer.tsx`, style guide §7.9), the message box with a green border and a `❯` before it and the plot button, under one quiet data line that is never gone: `# no data at hand? .example_data() · .open() a file`, highlighted until the first data arrives, then muted, `# other data? .open() · .example_data()` (the two are links there, `<button>`s with 44 px targets at phone width). Nothing comes or goes and nothing moves: a state changes only how the data line reads, whether the plot button is filled (from the moment a dataset is stored or data waits in the box) and which controls are inert, and an inert control is `aria-disabled`, so a focused one keeps the focus and a second key press does nothing (the page's `keepKeyboardFocus`, which moved the focus into the box when a run replaced a button with Stop, went with the fixed places). The plot button stands quiet and inert before data; with data it is `.create_plot() →`, which confirms the data and its bindings as the card shows them and starts the plot in the same click, and stops with the refusal under the composer when the confirmation fails; with an empty box the click starts the plot as it is, with text the text goes as the user's turn after the confirmation, so the assistant makes the plot with that change. Once the library has a plot it reads `.update_plot() →`: on confirmed data it sends the box's text as Enter does (a question or a change on the latest version) and is inert while the box is empty; on data or bindings changed since, it confirms and plots anew from the catalogue code with an empty box too, and after a change to data the user had confirmed, or a new paste after a plot, it reads `plot the new data` (since 2026-11-05). It waits while the box holds more than the message cap. Its description is the status line at the frame's foot, which in step 1 names what the click waits for (`step 1 of 2 · decide on the table first: .use() or .discard()`, `step 1 of 2 · pick a column for y, then .create_plot()`), else what to check, and after a lost confirmation what lost it (`back to step 1 of 2 · bindings changed · …`), and in step 2 what the click does before the library's first plot, the decisions a plot after a switch keeps (`2 decisions kept in seaborn · …`), else the confirmed data by its card's number and its shape (`step 2 of 2 · data v3 fits · 48 rows · 4 columns`; the server's `version 3` until 2026-11-07). While a plot runs, the status line reads `step 2 of 2 · drawing your plot · 0:42 · usually 1–3 min` (on a phone `drawing · 0:42 · …`; `stopping…` once Stop is pressed) in the step's words' place, the working line's clock counting on past the usual time unchanged and hidden from screen readers, so the plot button's description never reads it; a shipped plot's card keeps that time in its run line as `ready in 1:12` (`PlotVersion.readyMs`), and a version the page did not time says none; the status line always stands, so a run moves nothing. At its end stand `.stop()` while any run is in progress (a button of its own that Tab reaches; when the run ends while it holds the focus, the focus moves to the message box), `.start_over()` once the confirmed data has a plot in the library, and `.feedback()` before the first plot. There is no `.send()` (since 2026-11-07; it showed while the box held text): Enter sends, and on a touch screen a small `↵` inside the box sends too, since a phone keyboard labels Enter "return"; Enter and the `↵` never confirm. Until 2026-11-04, `.confirm()` was a click of its own before `.create_plot()`; the server's `data_unconfirmed` stays the backstop. With the admin's fan-out on (the admin menu's `all 15 libraries`, the library menu's `all` until 2026-11-06), the plot button reads `.plot_all()`, the step line names it instead of `.create_plot()`, it confirms the same way, a refused confirmation opens no panel, and only step 2 holds the message box, whose description the status line then is (`plot the table as it is in all 15 libraries at once · turn all off to chat`, a line above the box until 2026-11-07). `.start_over()` (since 2026-10-12, `.create_plot()` before: the owner could not tell the two apart; beside `.send()` in the composer until 2026-11-07) is described as "new from the catalogue plot; keeps your remembered decisions": a fresh plot of the same data from the catalogue code, with the plot memory's decisions (since 2026-11-02; "without your changes" before). A wide table (since 2026-10-12, `agents/anyplot/data/shape.py`): when the spec wants one required label role and exactly one required numeric role, no numeric series family, and the table holds 2 to 20 numeric columns and no text or boolean column (date columns are kept as keys), the dataset answers carry `suggestion`, the `pivot_longer` into two columns named in the session language (`Weekday`, `Month` or `Year` when every melted header is a weekday, month or year of one language, else `Category`, and `Value`; German `Wochentag`, `Monat`, `Jahr`, `Kategorie`, `Wert`; `shape.LONG_NAMES` for en, de, fr, es, it, pt, nl and pl, English otherwise; English for every session until live round 9 of 2026-10-16, where `Weekday` and `Value` became a German heatmap's axis and colorbar titles; the language route and every turn's `done` return the recomputed offer, so the card always names what `.to_long()` applies; a name that an input column already has gives way to the role's name, then to a numbered one, so names never decide the offer; `.to_long()` binds the two new columns to the roles they were made for, `LongSuggestion.bindings`, so `Category` never takes a spec's `category` role by its name), since live test round 8 of 2026-10-16, where the role names `y` and `value` became the plot's title "Wert nach Wochentag und Stunde"; the data card shows it as `# wide table` with `.to_long()`, which `POST /v1/sessions/{sid}/dataset/to_long` applies without a model call as the next version with the new columns bound to their roles, the user's bindings on the kept columns carried over and the defaults for the rest (undo as usual), and the root's profile tool carries it as `to_long` so the summary names it in one sentence. Since live test round 7 of 2026-10-16 a wide matrix gets it too: a spec whose required roles are two label roles of a grid or grouped chart (`shape.MATRIX_ROLES`: a heatmap's `x` and `y`, a grouped box's `category` and `subcategory`, a stacked bar's `component`; never a flow's `source` and `target`, a hierarchy's `name` and `parent` or a party's `color`) and one numeric role, and a table of one row-label column with a distinct label in every row (the one text column, else the one date column, else a first column of whole numbers that is a year or every hour of a range) plus at least three numeric columns (`Stunde;Mo;...;So`) is offered the `pivot_longer` of the numeric columns into the second label role, the row labels kept as the key for the first. Years and parts of a date are keys in both rules, never melted (an unnamed 1-to-12 column counts as a month only right after a year column that repeats), and so is a first column that numbers the rows (`id`, `Nr`, `Stunde` 0 to 3), which keeps the category of `id,a,b,c` on `id`. The numeric columns the offer melts (`bindings.label_siblings`) are never bound by default to a role that takes labels, also when `carry_bindings` refills the open roles after an undo: the role stays open, so the bindings are incomplete until `.to_long()` (the default had bound the heatmap's `y` to `Mo` and `value` to `Di`, and a confirmed run drew a meaningless matrix after 170 s). Bound so by hand, the binding is valid with a warning, shown under the dropdown (the dataset answer's `suggestion.siblings`) and in the session block, where the root offers `.to_long()` before any plot; blocking the confirmation on it is an open owner decision. The thread opens with the start item (`StartItem.tsx`, since 2026-11-07; from 2026-11-04 the chat header held its thumbnail and its columns ⓘ): "You came from **<the spec's title>**. It needs", then the spec's roles as `POST /v1/sessions` answers them (since 2026-11-04, `roles`) as chips in plain words (`roleChip` in `specReference.ts`: `x · numbers`, `numbers or dates` for a role of two kinds, `y · numbers, one or more` for a family, `optional` where it applies; the kinds read numbers, categories, dates, text and yes/no), beside them the catalogue plot being adapted as a thumbnail (`CataloguePreview.tsx`: the library in use, in the site theme, from the catalogue's responsive previews of the `GET /specs/{id}` the page already makes; a click opens it large in a dialog), the columns it expects behind an ⓘ after the chips, one line each in the names the data card's dropdowns use and the kinds in the chips' words (`` `y` (numbers, one column each) · <the spec's words> ``; `numeric` until 2026-11-07, with `optional` or `computed by the plot if left open` where it applies; the spec's `data` bullets without the `Size:` and `Example:` lines from a server without roles), and last "drop a file or paste a table below" with the how-to ⓘ. It repeats no control of the composer, whose data line right above the box holds `.example_data()` and `.open()`; it is a message in the thread, so it stays first and scrolls up with the talk once data arrives. While the session has no data, `.example_data()` in the highlighted data line above the message box offers example data (since 2026-11-04; the line read `# no data at hand?  .example_data()` until 2026-11-07, when `.open()` joined it): the turn action `{"action": "example_data"}`, never typed words, because the session's first typed message sets the session language; the root answers it as a typed request for example data (`run_data_code` with `source_language` `example`), and the turn's `done` carries the table as the session's first data, with nothing to undo. While that turn runs the button stays in its place, inert, and the working line says `making example data` beside the loader, once; once there is data, or while a table waits in the box, it is inert, and after the first data the line reads `# other data? .open() · .example_data()`, muted. Since 2026-10-12 the chat header carries a language chip next to the library menu (the library pills until 2026-11-02), which names the language in its own words since 2026-11-06 (`English ▾`, `Deutsch ▾`; a language outside the list as stored) and keeps how it was set in its accessible name, which starts with the words on screen (`Reply language: Deutsch, chosen. Change`; `chosen from the offer` after a `.switch()`); on screen it read `lang: en`, `lang: de · from data`, `lang: de · detected` or `lang: de · chosen` until then. Since 2026-11-08 the thread's one language line is the offer of a language found in a message or a paste (`LanguageOfferLine.tsx`, `# German detected in your message · .switch()`), shown once per language, which the `.switch()` turns into `# replies and plot labels: Deutsch`; the `# reply language: de` notice, which a paste and the first typed message each added, is gone. A click opens a popover with the ten common languages and a field for any other language in Latin letters; a choice calls the language route, and a `done` with `language` updates the chip, never moving it from `chosen` back down to `detected` or `default`. Keyboard focus: a key press on the plot button or `.example_data()` leaves the focus on it, inert while the turn runs, and Stop is a button of its own at the status line's end, so a second press never stops the run (until 2026-11-07 a button that a run replaced with Stop moved the focus into the message box); the confirmation inside the click moves nothing (until 2026-11-04 focus followed the user's own `.confirm()` to `.create_plot()`). The **result card** (`app/src/sections/agent-chat/ResultCard.tsx`) reuses the plot page's overlay actions: the rendered plot shown inline as a blob URL, in a box that takes the loaded PNG's own aspect because the footer strip makes it taller than 16:9, with a light and dark switch that renders the other theme on demand through the theme toggle route, **Copy image** (Clipboard API `navigator.clipboard.write([new ClipboardItem({"image/png": blob})])`, falling back to download where unsupported), **Download PNG** for every rendered theme and **Open full size**; the adapted code in `CodeHighlighter` with **Copy code** (`useCopyCode`, one click), **Download plot.py** and **Download data.csv** (the pair runs unchanged); the change list and residual notes, the notes in plain words with one line per issue by its rubric id (`residualNotes.ts`, since 2026-11-05) and the review's lines in full, with their ids, measurements and code, behind the notes' ⓘ; and the quick-feedback control. Since 2026-11-02 (owner: less text, the details behind toggles rather than removed) the card shows its details folded: one status line at the end of the theme row (`second try · 5 changes ⓘ`) opens the rounds grid and the change list, `.show_code()` and `.hide_code()` open and close the code while `.copy()` and the downloads stay in view, each card keeps its own choice for the session (also across a fold of the whole card), and a new version's card starts folded; the residual notes stay in view. Since 2026-11-07 the card carries its label on its top border, `plot v2 · matplotlib · title in German`: the version, its library and the request it answered, cut to 40 characters, or `first plot` or `.start_over()` when no request asked for it (`plotSummaries` in `threadModel.ts`). The label is the card's toggle, and every version but the latest folds to it as one line, `▸ plot v1 · matplotlib · first plot` (a header row with `.expand()` and `.collapse()` until then); the version's status stands in the theme row. On a phone the image's actions stand by their names on the line right under the image (`.download()`, `.copy_png()`, `.open()`, and `.interactive()` or `.preview()` for a JavaScript plot; quiet icons in the header row until then), each named with the words on screen, since the image's top right corner holds the plot's title there and no hover shows a name. The quick feedback shows only the four reactions until one is picked, which unfolds the message box, the data box, the consent line (for an admin too since 2026-11-06) and `.send()`. `.save()` and its data box are the admin's, in the admin menu since 2026-11-06 (in a "⋯" menu at the header's end from 2026-11-02, `SessionMenu.tsx`), not in the feedback area: a save downloads the session and stores nothing, while feedback stores a case. A visitor has no `.save()` (owner decision of 2026-11-02): a visitor cannot resume a saved session, and the feedback form covers "something went wrong". The start item's last ⓘ (`InfoPopover.tsx`, shared with the public notice; after the header row's language chip from 2026-11-06 to 2026-11-07, beside the chat's `# chat` label before) opens the how-to (`DATA_HOWTO`: a table pasted or a file opened or dropped as the attachment, a note beside it, code or a formula that makes data up to 2,000 characters, the steps to `.create_plot()`, a fix in plain words, changes afterwards); the data card's `# your data` label had it until the message box took the data. The composer's placeholder names a table, a file, example data and a curve while the session has no data, a note while an attachment waits, and a change after a plot. The card has no composer of its own (since 2026-10-12): a change request or a question about the plot goes through the thread's one composer below it, and the assistant decides whether it is a change or an answer. Earlier versions stay reachable in the thread, each with its own image and code. The same card is reused unchanged when the feature goes public. `app/src/lib/sse.ts` parses the stream over `fetchWithAuth(...).body.getReader()`. The entry is the text action `.use_my_data() →` to the right of the library carousel in `app/src/sections/spec-detail/LibraryPills.tsx`, the mirror of `← .compare()` on its left, with no "experimental" of its own, which the chat's notice says once open (until 2026-11-02 it was the `.adapt()` button, a fourth overlay button on the image in `SpecDetailView.tsx`); it is wired through `onUseWithMyData` from `SpecPage.tsx` and renders only when `useAgentEligibility` (`app/src/hooks/useAgentEligibility.ts`) names a lane for the pair: the public lane while `CONFIG.features.agentChat` holds and `GET /agent/lane` says it is open, else, while the door is closed, the admin lane for `CONFIG.isDev || adminHint`, in both cases only when that lane's eligibility route answers `eligible`. `adminHint` is a localStorage flag (`anyplot.adminHint`, `app/src/utils/adminAuth.ts`) that `DebugPage` sets after `/debug/status` succeeds and clears on a refusal. The entry leads everyone through the router to `/agent`, which picks its own lane (since 2026-11-06; until then an admin got a full navigation to `/debug/agent`, so Cloudflare Access could intercept), and a visitor's browser never probes `/api/debug/*`. As built on 2026-10-10: the dataset response lists the spec's roles (`{name, kinds, required, variadic, description}`), so every role gets a dropdown, a variadic family one per member (`y1`, `y2`, ...) plus an empty one for the next, kept contiguous on the client (since 2026-11-05 a member that picks a sibling's column swaps columns with it, so both series stay; the empty next member takes the column and the family closes up; since 2026-11-06 a family's members are chips in one row instead, each with `×`, and `+ y ▾` opens a menu of the columns left, those the role cannot take greyed out with the reason, so two series or eight take one row that wraps on a phone); a refused binding set answers with the check's lines, which the panel lists; the `plot` event carries the stored `version`, which addresses the artifact and theme toggle routes (see [Agent chat](../reference/api.md#agent-chat)); the page starts nothing while a turn, a theme render, a parse or a binding change is in flight, since the server would refuse it with `409 run_active`; Stop calls the cancel route and keeps the stream open until the server's `done` (at most 30 s), so a result stored just before the stop still arrives; the session is also deleted on `pagehide` with `keepalive`, and since nothing restores it, the page asks before it goes while the session holds data, a table read from text, a plot or work in flight (since 2026-11-01, after a reload dropped the 15 plots of a `.plot_all()` unasked): a reload, a closed tab or a typed address gets the browser's `beforeunload` dialog, and a link inside the site or the back button gets the router's blocker with a `confirm`, while the page's own query edits (a library; `&all=1` until 2026-11-06) and the reload links after `session_expired` or `unreachable` pass (`app/src/hooks/useLeaveWarning.ts`); a request without a readable answer offers a reload link (until 2026-11-06 the admin's page under `/debug` reloaded once by itself, so Cloudflare Access could sign the admin in again; `/agent` is not behind Access, and its reload checks the admin anew instead); the queue position shows in the working line (`waiting · you are 3rd`), and the frame's tab says `busy` (see **Load mood** below); the entry adds `source=plot_page` to the URL, which the page reads for `agent_open` and then drops. `app/scripts/agent-bff-mock.mjs` serves the documented BFF routes and a scripted stream locally (for `scatter-basic` and the series family of `line-multi`), so the page can be driven in a browser without the API, the agents service or a model.
- **Public page** (since 2026-11-02, launch package): `/agent?spec=&library=&language=` is `AgentChatPage` on the public lane (`PublicAgentChatPage` until 2026-11-06, when the page became one for everyone), an ordinary route that opens while `GET /agent/lane` says the lane is open (`useAgentLaneOpen`, one request per page load) and says the assistant is closed otherwise. Its requests go to `/agent/*` through `src/lib/agent.ts` with the credential `PUBLIC_LANE` in place of the admin token: no cookie, the tab's random id from sessionStorage as `X-Anyplot-Anon`, the session id moved from the path into `X-Anyplot-Session` (`publicRoute`). Above the first input it shows the experimental notice, the EU AI Act's Art. 50 notice, as one line since 2026-11-02, and since 2026-11-06 as one muted line above the chat's frame in every state of the session, right-aligned from `sm` up (`// experimental AI · answers can be wrong`; `# experimental AI · answers can be wrong` with a green rule inside the chat from 2026-11-04; `· stored only if you send feedback` moved behind the ⓘ), whose ⓘ button (`About the assistant`) opens a popover with the rest, in this order: that the chat and the data live only in server memory and are deleted after an hour idle (`AGENT_SESSION_IDLE_S`), that nothing is saved unless you send feedback, which AI and where (Claude by Anthropic, via Google Cloud Vertex AI in the EU, not used for training), what not to paste, the daily limits, and a link to the legal page's `#assistant` (`sections/agent-chat/ExperimentalNotice.tsx`, texts in `lane.ts`); while the session plots in Highcharts, both lanes show the licence note. It offers no `all` and no `.save()` (since 2026-11-02; both are the admin menu's since 2026-11-06), and the feedback form's `# include data.csv` box starts unticked, with a consent line that says what sending stores (`sections/agent-chat/lane.ts`); since 2026-11-06 the notice, the box and the consent line are an admin's too. The entry points follow the same switch: the plot page's `.use_my_data() →` beside the library carousel (accessible name "Use with my data"; until 2026-11-02 an `.adapt()` button on the image) shows for everyone while the lane is open and the pair is eligible, and since 2026-11-04 leads an admin to `/agent` too (until then to `/debug/agent`, which stayed the admin's page for `all` and `.save()` until 2026-11-06 and only redirects to `/agent` since), and while the lane is open the landing page carries one line, `# new: pick any plot, then .use_my_data() →`, that leads to the catalogue (`/plots`, `nav_click{source: hero_use_my_data}`), so the visitor picks the plot first; on launch day it opened the chat on scatter-basic in matplotlib (`agent_open{source: landing}`).
- **One page** (since 2026-11-06, owner: the admin's chat must look and behave exactly like the public one and run the same code, so the owner never misses a problem a visitor has). `/agent` serves both; `/debug/agent` is only a client-side redirect to it (`app/src/routes/AgentChatRedirect.tsx`), which keeps `spec`, `library`, `language` and `source` and sets the admin hint first (a full load of that address reaches the app only through Cloudflare Access on `/debug*`, so a bookmark opens the admin's chat also in a fresh browser; the hint grants nothing, the check below still decides), with no sign-in door of its own: the admin signs in on `/debug`. The page detects the admin at runtime (`app/src/hooks/useAgentAdmin.ts`): a browser without the admin hint (and outside local development) is a visitor's and sends no request to the debug API; with the hint, the page asks the admin lane's `GET /debug/agent/status` first and waits for the answer, which picks the API (the first admin chat of the page reuses that answer for its libraries, run cap and pause instead of asking again). A 200, or `404 not_enabled` (the admin gate answers before the kill switch), runs the admin lane, also while the public door is closed; a 401 or 403 clears the hint, and no answer (an expired Cloudflare Access session turns the call into a cross-origin redirect) keeps it, and either way the page falls back to the public lane while the admin menu says the admin session expired and links to `/debug`. A refusal later in the session does the same. The page is not behind Access, so it never reloads itself for it (`reloadOnceForAccess` serves only `/debug` now). The server-side checks are unchanged (Cloudflare Access and the admin gate on `/debug/agent/*`): a browser flag never grants anything, the page only picks the API it calls. The only differences besides the API are the limits, which the server enforces per tier, and the fan-out to every library; every admin extra sits behind one admin-only element, the admin menu (`sections/agent-chat/AdminMenu.tsx`, at the header row's end where the "⋯" menu stood): the fan-out toggle `all 15 libraries` (in place of the library menu's `all` and the URL's `&all=1`), `.save()`, "view as visitor", which runs the public lane exactly (public API, bot check, public limits) for the tab (sessionStorage `anyplot.agentAsVisitor`) while the menu stays to switch back, and the admin's diagnostics (`AGENT_ENABLED` off; the error text itself is a visitor's). Since 2026-11-09 two sections join it, which the page passes as the menu's children: the running turn's progress grid (`ProgressTimeline.tsx`, also in the visitor's view, not after an expired session), and on the admin lane only the meters, `now 2 runs · today 62 %`: the runs in flight from `/status` and the share of the day budget's weighted tokens from `GET /debug/agent/budget` (`agentApi.budget`, `readBudget` and `budgetShare` in `src/lib/agent.ts`, which the debug page's live line imports too, so the two never disagree; the public lane has no `/budget`, and `agentApi.budget` asks none there). Opening the menu reads both, `/status` through the page's one reader, also during a run, when that answer feeds the meters alone and never the tab's mood; the poll reads no budget. Nothing deep in the tree knows the lane any more (`AgentLaneContext` is gone), and `app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.parity.test.tsx` renders the page for a visitor and an admin through the session's states and fails on any DOM difference outside the admin menu.
- **Load mood** (since 2026-11-09, kit 5 option C): the frame's tab reads `~/anyplot.ai/agent · ● quiet`, `● busy` or `● resting until 01:00` (`LoadMood.tsx`), the same for visitors and admins and without numbers: resting while `/status` says `paused` and its `resets_at` is ahead (the time on the visitor's own clock), busy while `waiting` is above 0 or the own turn waits in the queue (where it stands is the working line's, owner 2026-11-09), else quiet; on a phone the resting mood reads `back at 01:00`, which fits the tab at 390 px; no mood before the first answer, and none after a failed first read. The page's one reader of the lane's `/status` (`app/src/hooks/useAgentStatus.ts`) gives the first answer's libraries and run cap to the page and reads again after each run, when the tab becomes visible again and every 60 s while it is visible (`STATUS_POLL_MS`), never while a turn is in flight and never twice at once; a poll changes only the load, and a failed read keeps the last answer. A `service_paused` refusal rests the mood at once, and the mood turns quiet at the reset without a reload; a data call's `429 service_paused` does not change it, since its own line says the same with the time. The pause note above the composer (2026-11-02 to 2026-11-09) is gone. The mood's dot is a status glyph beside its word, hidden from screen readers, and the mood is a `role="status"` region of its own beside the tab's hidden path.
- **Analytics** (enum properties only, documented in [Plausible](../reference/plausible.md#agent-chat)): the pageview `/agent` (and `/debug/agent` until 2026-11-06), only for a visitor since 2026-11-06 (an admin's use of the page mutes every event, `app/src/analytics/mute.ts`), `agent_open{library,source,spec}`, `agent_data_parsed{status,size_bucket}`, `agent_data_confirmed{size_bucket}`, `agent_data_prepared{changes}`, `agent_data_undo{version}`, `agent_plot_rendered{library,status,repaired,spec}`, `agent_guardrail_block{reason}`, `agent_session_saved{include_data}`, `agent_result_feedback{reaction,include_data}`, and `copy_code{page:'agent_chat',method:'agent'}`, all built; an admin's page fired the same events until 2026-11-06 and fires none since. The session language fires no event: decided while the chat was admin-only, when the chip's numbers would have been tiny, and kept for the experimental launch.

## Repository layout

```
agents/__init__.py  agents/main.py  agents/Dockerfile  agents/cloudbuild.yaml
agents/anyplot/{__init__.py, agent.py (root_agent, ALL_AGENTS, app = App(...)), models.py, settings.py, policy.py, schemas.py, pipeline.py, run_queue.py, theme_render.py, language.py}
agents/anyplot/sub_agents/{adapter.py, reviewer.py}            # relative imports only: ADK loads the package as top-level `anyplot`
agents/anyplot/tools/{session.py, catalogue.py}
agents/anyplot/plugins/{ledger.py, scope_guard.py, budget.py, tool_safety.py}
agents/anyplot/prompts/{root,adapter,reviewer,scope_judge,data_judge}.md  refusals.yaml
agents/anyplot/data/{parse.py, bindings.py, store.py}           # no ADK import; built; the role parser lives in core/spec_roles.py
agents/anyplot/code/{regions.py, normalise.py, readiness.py, edits.py, validate.py, loader.py, export.py}   # no ADK import; built
agents/anyplot/render/{contract.py, gates.py, png.py, harness.py, serial.py, store.py, watermark.py, runtimes/python.py, backends/{remote.py, local.py, sandbox.py, fake.py}}
agents/anyplot/render/fonts/{JetBrainsMono-Regular.ttf, JetBrainsMono-Bold.ttf, OFL.txt, README.md}   # SIL OFL 1.1, unmodified
agents/renderer/{main.py, executor.py, auth.py, settings.py, wire.py, Dockerfile, cloudbuild.yaml}   # anyplot-renderer; no ADK import, no __init__.py; built
agents/evals/{matrix.py, make_fixtures.py, cases.py, pricing.py, report.py, eligibility.py, scope.py, scope.evalset.json}   # built
agents/evals/{harness/test_flows.py, sync_cases.py,
              baselines/{claude-haiku-5-5,gemini-3.8-flash}.json, fixtures/cases/<id>/{case.json, data.csv} (synthetic only),
              reports/ (gitignored), .cases/ (gitignored, synced from the bucket)}
core/catalogue/{__init__.py, queries.py}      # shared by api/mcp/server.py and agents/
core/canvas.py  core/defects.py                # lifted from impl-review.yml and automation/scripts/regen_gate.py (which keeps a parity-tested copy)
core/database/models.py (AgentFeedback)        alembic/versions/<rev>_agent_feedback.py
api/routers/agent.py                           # BFF, feedback and cases routes
app/src/pages/AgentChatPage.tsx  app/src/sections/agent-chat/{ResultCard,ResultFeedback,...}.tsx  app/src/hooks/useAgentSession.ts  app/src/lib/sse.ts
app/src/pages/DebugPage.tsx (Agent cases section)
tests/unit/agents/*  tests/unit/api/test_agent_router.py  tests/integration/agents/test_pipeline_offline.py
```

`agents/anyplot/code/` exists since 2026-10-09: `validate.py` (the two-profile AST validator), `regions.py` (the AST spans of the THEME block, the Imprint palette, the placeholder, the save calls and the canvas keywords, shared by the other modules; since 2026-10-25 also the save rule per library, `SAVE_RULES`), `normalise.py`, `readiness.py`, `edits.py`, `loader.py` and `export.py`, with tests in `tests/unit/agents/code/`. Their docstrings hold the exact rules. The 13 map specs are the specs tagged both `plot_type: map` and `data_type: geospatial`. `readiness.scan` takes the spec id as a keyword argument, because the map rule needs it and the normalised code no longer carries the catalogue header.

Files to modify: `pyproject.toml` (the extra `agents = ["google-adk==2.11.0"]`; the `agents-eval` extra with `google-adk[eval]` was dropped on 2026-10-12, unused, because its litellm and nltk carried security alerts; setuptools include `agents*`; ruff `known-first-party`; coverage source; regenerate `uv.lock` and run `uv lock --check`; assert that the lock never contains `openai-agents`, which also installs a top-level `agents` module), `.dockerignore` (`!agents`, `!prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `!prompts/library`), `core/config.py` (`agent_*` settings with the pinned defaults, `_strip_secret` entries), `api/routers/debug.py`, `api/main.py`, `api/cloudbuild.yaml` (env vars, secret binding, 401 smoke), `automation/scripts/regen_gate.py` (switch to `core/defects.py`, loaded from a sibling copy in script mode, together with its copy sites), `.github/workflows/{ci-tests,ci-lint,ci-image}.yml`, a new `.github/workflows/agents-eval.yml`, `app/src/routes/{index.tsx,paths.ts}`, `global-config.ts`, `vite-env.d.ts`, `SpecPage.tsx`, `SpecDetailView.tsx`, `DebugPage.tsx`, `app/Dockerfile`, `app/cloudbuild.yaml`, `docs/index.md`, `docs/reference/{api,plausible,mcp,repository,database}.md`, `docs/development.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (an `agents/**` routing row, kept in sync), and one `changelog.d/<slug>.md` per PR. `agents/Dockerfile` builds from `python:3.13-slim` with `uv sync --frozen --extra agents`, runs as a non-root user and starts `uvicorn agents.main:app --port 8080`; its `agents/` copy must include `agents/anyplot/render/fonts/`, because the service loads the footer-strip fonts at start and refuses to start without them, and it copies the catalogue prompt sources `agents/anyplot/policy.py` reads (`prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `prompts/library/`, `prompts/quality-criteria.md` and the two workflow prompts `impl-repair-claude.md` and `ai-quality-review.md`) to their repository paths; `tests/unit/agents/test_image.py` holds that list against every file an import of `agents.main` opens and against the root `.dockerignore`; the plotting extras, the fallback fonts (`/usr/share/fonts/noto`, from a `fonts` build stage that keeps only the allowlisted files of `fonts-noto-core` and `fonts-noto-cjk`) and the baked matplotlib font cache (`/opt/mplconfig`, whose bake fails when a fallback family is missing) belong to the renderer image, `agents/renderer/Dockerfile`, which installs `--extra lib-matplotlib --extra lib-seaborn`, since 2026-10-25 `--extra lib-plotnine --extra lib-altair` and since 2026-10-26 `--extra lib-pygal --extra lib-letsplot`, all without ADK. It links matplotlib's DejaVu Serif and DejaVu Sans Mono into `/usr/share/fonts` for vl-convert's `serif` and `monospace` (altair files that name those families draw no text without them) and, since 2026-10-26, DejaVu Sans next to them, which lets-plot's bridge draws its default text in (it falls back to a Noto Sans face without it). Since 2026-10-26 it also installs Debian's `libcairo2` for cairosvg, which brings `libfontconfig1`, `/etc/fonts/fonts.conf` and Debian's DejaVu core fonts (other bytes under the same names; matplotlib keeps resolving its own copies, and the seaborn, plotnine and altair renders of `ci-image.yml` stayed byte-identical), and copies `agents/renderer/fonts.conf` (one font directory, no cache directory) to `/opt/anyplot/fonts.conf` for lets-plot's row. The ImageMagick linked into lets-plot's bridge is a zero-configuration build that reads no `policy.xml`, so the image installs none of the programs its delegates run (`gs`, `curl`, `soffice`, `ffmpeg` and others), and the image build fails when any of 39 such programs is on the sandbox's `PATH` (`agents/renderer/delegates.py`, which `ci-image.yml` runs again in the built image; since 2026-10-26); since 2026-10-28 the harness's seccomp filter refuses every program start from the bridge whatever is on `PATH`, and every in-process run requires it. It copies the harness to `/opt/anyplot/harness.py`. The `deploy-renderer` trigger (an owner task) fires on `agents/renderer/**`, `agents/anyplot/render/harness.py`, `pyproject.toml` and `uv.lock`; the catalogue's `Project.toml` and `Manifest.toml`, toolchain inputs since 2026-10-29 (the Julia stage precompiles them), are not in its included files yet, an owner task in step 5 of `agentic/specs/261024-agent-all-libraries.md`. The `deploy-agents` trigger (an owner task) fires on `agents/**` except `agents/evals/**`, `core/**`, `prompts/library/**`, `prompts/default-style-guide.md`, `prompts/quality-criteria.md`, `prompts/workflow-prompts/impl-repair-claude.md`, `prompts/workflow-prompts/ai-quality-review.md`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `README.md` and `.dockerignore`, so an edit to any prompt source the service sends to the model redeploys it.

Reused code: the `api/mcp/server.py` tool bodies move into `core/catalogue/queries.py`; `core/database/repositories.py` (`SpecRepository.get_by_id_with_code`, `ImplRepository.get_code`); `core/utils.strip_noqa_comments`; `core/constants.py` (`LIBRARIES_METADATA`, `LIBRARY_LANGUAGES`, `INTERACTIVE_LIBRARIES`); `core/palette.py`; the defect grammar through `core/defects.py` and the canvas gate and PNG auto-reject checks through `core/canvas.py`; the render invocation shape of `agentic/workflows/modules/regen/render.py` (copied, not imported); `automation/js-render/render.mjs` for the JS runtime later; the per-IP-hash rate-limit pattern of `api/routers/feedback.py` and `api/request_context.client_ip` for the public phase.

## Roadmap

### Phase 0: local and spikes (about 1 to 1.5 weeks after the API is enabled)

Owner tasks 1 to 6 come first. Then run the spikes:

| Spike | Question | Pass criterion |
|---|---|---|
| M | One call on `eu` and `global` for both arms: `claude-haiku-5-5` through the Anthropic Vertex SDK and gemini-3.8-flash plus gemini-3.5-flash-lite through google-genai (and, for the record, the alias); the reported model version and usage; free `countTokens` on the adapter and reviewer prefixes | Recorded; under $0.01 (done on 2026-10-09: both arms answer on `eu`) |
| S | A Cloud Run sandbox in europe-west4: the deploy works; matplotlib and seaborn render with egress blocked; a blocked fetch fails fast; RSS and time at 2 and 4 vCPU; concurrent sandboxes before OOM; cold start; whether the IAM-forwarded ID-token claims are visible to the container and in what signature state; a probe suite (metadata server, DNS and HTTP egress, `/proc/1/environ`, writes outside `/work`, sudo, fork and memory bombs). Run `gcloud components update` first, because the local SDK 534 lacks the flag; ideally in a throwaway project | Everything blocked or contained |
| G | Can a sandbox read sibling run directories or files the host writes at runtime? | No; otherwise use `--rootfs` with a toolchain-only tree or one render per instance |
| X | Adaptation: 10 specs times matplotlib and seaborn times 6 datasets (renamed, x10, n=12, n=5000, date, decimal comma; the cases are in `agents/evals/fixtures/cases/` and run with `agents/evals/matrix.py`, see `agents/README.md`) on both arms, `claude-haiku-5-5` and gemini-3.8-flash, so the owner picks the phase-1 default by cost and acceptance; plotly only on figure-level gates because the phase-1 image has no Chrome. Pass means all deterministic gates plus an owner blind accept or reject on a sample of 30 renders (`python -m agents.evals.report blind` shows both arms' renders of the same cases without naming the arm). Also reports post-normaliser eligibility under the SECURITY profile and the files blocked for a missing `THEME` block or a non-literal save target (`agents/evals/eligibility.py`, no model call; it sweeps plotnine and altair too since 2026-10-25, and pygal and lets-plot since 2026-10-26) | Under $25 |
